Network ZIP Codes Import

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/BA/Medical/GBP/Network_Zip_Codes_Import.htm Path: Task Sheets/BA/Medical/GBP/Network_Zip_Codes_Import.htm

Network ZIP Codes Import

To import ZIP codes to benefit plans that have a network assigned, use the Network ZIP Codes Import form. The imported file must be a comma-delimited (.CSV) file and include headers that describe the contents of each column.

  1. Enter the Plan ID for the benefit plan.

  2. Select the Network to which you want to import the ZIP codes.

  3. Click Start Import. The Upload window displays.

  4. Click Choose File, and select the file to upload.

  5. Click Submit. The imported data displays. If the file contains invalid information, an error report displays in the form.

  • To view the data in the form, click Done with Errors.

  • To scroll through the report as needed, click Next Page and Previous Page.

  • To discard the data, click Cancel at any time.

  1. To complete the import, click Post Data. The imported ZIP codes will display in a table.

Setting Up Billing Rates for Age Banded Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/BA/Medical/GBP/Setting_Up_Billing_Rates_Age_MDV.htm Path: Task Sheets/BA/Medical/GBP/Setting_Up_Billing_Rates_Age_MDV.htm

Setting Up Billing Rates for Age Banded Plans

Use the Billing Rates form, accessed in the Actions menu in Group Benefit Plans, to set up billing rates for a tobacco banded group benefit plan.

You can use the Copy from Premium Rates option, accessed in the Actions menu to copy age-range information from the Premium Rates record for the benefit plan. This option only displays when an Effective Date is entered in Billing Rates.

  1. The Benefit Plan ID populates from the Group Benefit Plan form.

Note: Note: The Rate Group field displays when the Billing Rates Vary By Network drop-down in the Other Processing Parameters panel is set to No (the default setting).

  1. Enter the Rate Group. Your organization might charge different clients at different rates for the same benefit plan. PrismHR allows you to define these different rates using rate groups. You can use as many rate groups as needed. The rates you enter apply only to this rate group.

  2. Select the Plan Type. The drop-down lists only the plan types defined for the group benefit plan.

  3. Enter a new Effective Date, or select an existing one on the right (if any). Organizing saving rates by Effective Date allows you to view previous rates for reference purposes. The system calculates billing using the rates with the most recent effective date.

  4. Enter the age-range information for each age band in the benefit plan. For each age range:

a. Enter the From Age for each line, which is the starting age.
b. Enter the To Age, which is the upper age limit.
— —

Note: Note: The Total Bill Amount field is the sum of the Employee Bill Amount and Dependents Bill Amount. Once you enter two values, the third calculates automatically. For example, if you enter a Total Bill Amount of 500 and an Employee Bill Amountof 300, the system populates Dependents Bill Amount with 200.

c. Enter the Total Bill Amount to bill to the client for the cost of this plan.
d. Enter the Employee Bill Amount for this plan.
— —
e. Enter the Dependents Bill Amount for this plan.
— —

  1. Click Save. The form clears so that you can enter the premiums for another plan type.

  2. Repeat these steps for each plan type set up in the Plan Types panel in Group Benefits Plans.

  3. Click Close when you are finished.

Using Benefit Billing Options

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/BA/TS-Benefit_Billing_Options.htm Path: Task Sheets/BA/TS-Benefit_Billing_Options.htm

Using Benefit Billing Options

This article describes how to use benefit billing and how to set up the necessary bill codes in PrismHR.

Benefit Billing Scenarios

There are three common scenarios for benefit billing in PrismHR:

Scenario 1: PEO-sponsored benefit plan, used by the client

  • Description: The PEO sponsors a benefit plan which the client uses (alternately, the PEO manages the funds for the client’s provider). The PEO pays the full cost of the benefit plan to the plan provider, then bills the client for this payment. The PEO then deducts employee contributions to the benefit plan, and reimburses the client for this contribution.

  • Requirements: Do the following:

Action — Description

Set up bill code — You must set up a bill code on the benefit plan in the Group Benefit Plans form. The bill code handles both billing the client for the amount paid to the plan provider as well as reimbursement of employee contributions to the client.

Set up standard deduction code — You must set up a standard deduction code in the Deduction Codes form to deduct employee contributions for the benefit plan.

Set up above deduction code with no value — You must set up the above deduction code with no value in the Client Bill Rate Code field. A deduction code that includes a value would reimburse employee deductions twice to the client since the bill code already handles both billing and reimbursement.

Scenario 2: Client-sponsored benefit plan

  • Description: The client sponsors its own plan. The client pays the full cost of the benefit plan to the plan provider. Since the client pays for the benefit plan directly, the PEO does not bill the client for the cost of the benefit plan. The PEO deducts employee contributions, then reimburses the client for these contributions.

  • Requirements: Do the following:

Action — Description

Do not set up bill code — Since the client pays the plan provider directly, the PEO does not bill the client for the amount owed the benefit provider; therefore, you do not need to set up a bill code on the benefit plan.

Set up special deduction code — You must set up a special deduction code in the Deduction Codes form to deduct employee contributions for the benefit plan.

Set up above deduction code with value — You must set up the above deduction code with a value in the Client Bill Rate Code field to reimburse employee contributions to the client.

Scenario 3: PEO-sponsored benefit plan

  • Description: The PEO has a benefit plan that the employee uses without any client involvement. Since the PEO sponsors the plan and the client is not involved, the PEO does not bill the client. The PEO pays the full cost of the benefit plan to the plan provider, then deducts and keeps the employee contribution.

  • Requirements: Do the following:

Action — Description

Do not set up bill code — Since this scenario does not involve billing or reimbursing the client, you do not set up a bill code on the benefit plan.

Set up standard deduction code — You must set up a standard deduction code in the Deduction Codes form to deduct the employee’s contribution from the employee’s pay.

Set up deduction code with no value — You must set up this deduction code with no value in the Client Bill Rate Code field.

Setting Up the Bill Code

The Bill Code field, located in the Group Benefit Plans panel on the Group Benefit Plans form, specifies the billing the benefit plan uses and controls the accounting and invoicing to the client for the benefit plan.

[Image: screenshot]

To set up the bill code, do the following:

  1. Enter the Bill Code that represents the billing this benefit plan uses.

  • For PEO-sponsored benefit plans that are used by the client (scenario 1), you must select a Bill Code from the drop-down.

  • For client-sponsored benefit plans, or PEO-sponsored benefit plans that do not involve the client, (scenarios 2 and 3), you must leave the Bill Code field blank.

  1. Click Save. If you have not selected a Bill Code, PrismHR displays an alert and prompts you to confirm that you do not want to specify a bill code on this benefit plan.

  2. If you are defining a PEO-sponsored benefit plan that is used by the client (scenario 1), click Yes to finish saving. Otherwise, click No, select a Bill Code from the drop-down, and click Save.

Setting Up the Client Rebate Bill Code

The Client Rebate Bill Code field, located in the Client Rebates panel in the Deduction Codes form, specifies the client rebate code used on the deduction.

Note: Note: For more information about setting up deduction codes, refer to the Payroll Setup Guide.

To set up the client rebate bill code, do the following:

  1. Enter the Client Rebate Bill Code this deduction uses to reduce a company’s bill amounts, if applicable.

  • For PEO-sponsored benefit plans that used by the client or that do not involve the client (scenarios 1 and 3), the Client Rebate Bill Code field must be left blank.

  • For client-sponsored benefit plans (scenario 2), you must select a value from the drop-down.

  1. Click Save.

Working with Participation Requirements

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/BA/TS-Working_with_Participation_Requirements.htm Path: Task Sheets/BA/TS-Working_with_Participation_Requirements.htm

Working with Participation Requirements

To work with participation requirements, do the following:

  • Define participation requirements at the system level, client level, or both

  • Define valid waiver reasons

  • Activate pending enrollments with the new Participation Requirements Maintenance form

Defining Participation Requirement

Managing participation requirements ensures that client and employee enrollments follow carrier guidelines. You can do this at the system level, client level, or both. Defining participation requirements at the client level overrides requirements defined at the system level.

To define participation requirements at the system level, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. On the System|Change menu, select Group Benefit Plans.

  3. Enter a Benefit Plan ID or click the link to select from a list.

  4. In the Participation Requirement panel, complete the following fields:

Field Action
Participation Requirement Start Date Enter a start date.
Participation Requirement Percentage Enter a percentage. Value must be numerical from 1 to 100.
  1. Click Save.

To define participation requirements at the client level, do the following:

  1. On the Client|Change menu, select Benefit Plan Setup.

  2. In the Plan Class field, select Group Benefit.

  3. In the Participation Requirement panel, complete the following fields:

Field Action
Participation Requirement Option Select an option from the list: - No - Yes: Enables the Participation Requirement Start Date and Participation Requirement Percentage fields. - Use System Default: Uses the values defined at the system level.
  • No
  • Yes: Enables the Participation Requirement Start Date and Participation Requirement Percentage fields.
  • Use System Default: Uses the values defined at the system level. | Participation Requirement Start Date | Enter a start date. | | Participation Requirement Percentage | Enter a percentage. The value must be numerical from 1 to 100. |
  1. Click Save.

Defining Valid Waiver Reasons

After defining participation requirements, you must define valid waiver reasons. Defining valid waiver reasons enable you to determine the total eligible participation count. Note that all waiver reasons (valid or invalid) are included in the total waived employee count.

To define valid waiver reasons, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. On the System|Change menu, select System Parameters.

  3. In the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] , select Enrollment Options. The Enrollment Options form displays.

  4. Use the Valid Waiver Reason column to define the Medical Waive Reasons as valid (Yes) or invalid (No).

  5. Click Save.

Users can now submit their enrollments and enter the posting queue as pending.

Activating Pending Enrollments

When benefit enrollments contain plans with participation requirements, they enter the posting queue as pending. The Participation Requirement Maintenance form enables you to activate pending enrollments.

To activate pending enrollments, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. On the Operations|Action menu, select Participation Requirement Maintenance.

  3. In the Participation Requirement Maintenance panel, complete the following fields:

Field Action
Benefit Plan ID Enter a plan ID or click the link to select from a list.
Participation Requirement Criteria - To view posted enrollments with the start date and percentage entered at the system level on the Group Benefit Plan form, select System check. - To view posted enrollments with client overrides entered at the client level on the Benefit Plan Setup form, select a Client ID check. The Client ID field is disabled.
  • To view posted enrollments with the start date and percentage entered at the system level on the Group Benefit Plan form, select System check.
  • To view posted enrollments with client overrides entered at the client level on the Benefit Plan Setup form, select a Client ID check. The Client ID field is disabled. | Client ID | To filter clients with system-level participation requirements, select a client from the Client ID list. |
  1. Click Search. The Search Results panel displays benefit enrollments with effective dates on or before the Participation Requirement Start Date.

In the Participation Requirement Maintenance panel, information about the selected benefit plan enrollments displays.

Field — Description

Participation Requirement Needed — The total Participation Requirement percentage.

Estimated Participation Achieved — Percentage = (Pending Employee count + Active Employee count) / (Total Employee count - Waived Employee count)

Pending Employee count — The number of employees with a Pending enrollment status.

Active Employee count — The number of employees with an Active enrollment status.

Waived Employee count — The number of employees with a Waived enrollment status. Note that all waiver reasons (valid or invalid) are included in the total waived employee count.

Terminated Employee count — The number of employees with a Terminated enrollment status.

Eligible Employee count — The number of employees with an eligible benefit enrollment for this plan. Note that when adding the benefit plan after entering the employee into the system, the employee is eligible, but an eligible benefit enrollment does not exist.

Total Employee count — Total of all employee counts.

  1. Click Activate Pending Plans. A dialog displays confirming you want to change the pending enrollments to an active status.

  2. Click Yes.

When complete, pending enrollments have an active status.

Using COBRA Features to Comply with the American Rescue Plan Act

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/COVID/TS-ARPA_COBRA_Guidance.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/COVID/TS-ARPA_COBRA_Guidance.htm

Using COBRA Features to Comply with the American Rescue Plan Act

Background

The 2021 the American Rescue Plan Act (also known as the ARP and ARPA), introduced various changes and extensions to existing coronavirus-related legislation. The act also specified the creation of COBRA premium subsidies for enrollees who experience qualifying events (in other words, involuntary termination or a reduction of hours). These subsidies apply to 100% of the cost of the COBRA premium paid between April and September, 2021.

Certain individuals who could have enrolled in COBRA continuation coverage in the past, but did not do so, are provided another opportunity to enroll and are also eligible for the subsidy, along with individuals who previously enrolled in COBRA but then discontinued continuation coverage.

Additionally, the legislation specifies the creation of several new model election notices to inform potentially eligible individuals of this benefit. These model notices include:

  • A standard election notice for individuals who have qualifying events between April 1 and September 30.

  • A notice for an extended election period, for individuals who had qualifying events in the 18 months prior to April 1.

  • An “alternate” notice for individuals with state continuation coverage that would extend their coverage into the April 1–September 30 period.

To comply with the new COBRA-related requirements, some service providers have opted to start using the existing COBRA features provided by PrismHR, Others will not use these features, but still need to record and track tax credits and subsidy premiums. This document provides initial guidance for both groups, and will provide further guidance, procedures, and workarounds as more official direction becomes available.

Please read this document in its entirety and check back frequently for updates. Updates will be reflected by the revision number at the bottom of this article. If you have further questions about using these features, please contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

If you do not currently leverage PrismHR’s existing COBRA features

Note: Note: This section was written before the May and June releases. It is included here, in modified form, for informational purposes.

PrismHR leverages the existing tax deduction code, 00-19, for COBRA subsidy credits.

Even before the release of any new COBRA features, any amounts associated with this tax code will build and accrue entirely on the employer level. This means that you can begin accumulating COBRA subsidy credits under this tax code immediately, even if you do not have these features enabled.

To accumulate credits under 00-19 before the release of any new features, apply a tax adjustment to an employee and associate it with this tax code. The credits will accrue employee-by-employee, and the system will ultimately build those credits on the employer level.

Note: Note: After the May release, for MasterTax users, the credits build at the “lower” client/F-code level (in contrast to the higher employer level) by default. PrismHR also introduced a new system-level setting on the MasterTax Interface Parameters form, so users can select whether the system reports on the client or employer level by default (see Reporting COBRA Subsidy Credits on the Employer Level below). In addition, we are currently developing more refined functionality that enables users to flag COBRA plans as client-sponsored on a client-by-client basis.

Using the New ARPA/COBRA Features

In May and June 2021, PrismHR began releasing new features designed to better support the new ARPA COBRA requirements.

Note: Note: COBRA bill pending entries behave differently from standard bill pending entries. COBRA bill pending entries will be pulled into MasterTax on the next daily import, regardless of whether their associated payrolls have posted.

Setting Up Automatic Credit Generation

You can still create 00-19 tax adjustments manually, if needed. But you can also configure the system-level COBRA settings and Participant Record settings so that the system automatically generates 00-19 credits when you create COBRA invoices.

To use these new features:

  1. Go to COBRA Administration > File > COBRA Global Setup Record.

  2. Select F9-Subsidy. The COBRA Subsidy Set-up form displays.

  3. Enter a Start Date of 4/1/2021 and a Stop Date of 9/30/2021.

  4. Enter 100 in the Subsidy Percent field.

  5. Click F2-Save.

  6. Next, go to COBRA Administration > File > Participant Record.

  7. Enter the appropriate Participant ID.

  8. Set Subsidy Percent to 100 and click F2-Save.

  9. Repeat for all applicable participants.

Next time you create COBRA invoices, the system will automatically generate 00-19 tax adjustments for all the affected participants who have this setup, and will then create a corresponding invoice line item to reflect the subsidy.

Manually Generating COBRA Subsidy Credits

You can still manually accrue, track, and create invoices for full premium values. To do this, create a COBRA Transaction Entry with the Transaction Type set to S - Enrollee Subsidy Credit (use F3 to select from the list of available types).

[Image: COBRA Transaction Entry form with Transaction Type highlighted, set to Enrollee Subsidy Credit]

To manually generate a COBRA subsidy credit in PrismHR:

  1. Go to Client > Change > Client Details >Actions menu [Image: screenshot] > COBRA Credit Entry.

  2. Click New Adjustment.

  3. Select the Employee to associate with this credit.

  4. Enter the Credit Date.

  5. Select the Employer to associate with this credit.

  6. Enter the COBRA Credit Amount as a positive value. This amount will accumulate as a negative value under tax code 00-19. If you enter a negative value in this field, a positive entry will accumulate under 00-19 and on the bill pending entry.

  7. Select Credit Client? if you want to create a Bill Pending entry for this client.

  8. If you selected Credit Client, you can also enter a Credit Billing Date to associate with the newly created client bill pending entry.

  9. Click Save. The system creates the new COBRA credit under the specified employee and employer.

Reporting COBRA Subsidy Credits on the Employer Level

Note: Note: Currently, this value reports in MasterTax as a positive value. As part of our development work for MasterTax RTS file version 42, PrismHR is planning to change this to a negative value in a future release.

By default, PrismHR currently reports 00-19 on the client, or F-code, level of the MasterTax RTS and PTS files. You can change this default on the MasterTax Interface Parameters form.

To change the default reporting level from client to employer:

  1. Go to Work Centers > MasterTax Processing > Setup Forms > Mastertax Interface Parameters.

  2. Locate the new Schedule R Reporting Override panel. The panel contains a single-valued table.

  3. In the table, create a new row and then select 00-19.

  4. Click Save. The system will now default to the employer level when reporting 00-19 credits.

[Image: Detail on the Tax Codes grid in the Schedule R Reporting Override panel, with code 00-19 entered in the first row of the grid.]

Suggestions for Using Report Center

To identify subsidy-eligible individuals, you can use Report Center to review employee terminations by Date and by Termination Reason type. You can also add state information to the report if you need to identify employees who qualify for COBRA State Continuation coverage.

Additionally, if you are tracking 00-19 credits using PrismHR, you can use Report Center to review any employees who had adjustments to their Standard or Default Time Sheet Hours, which might have otherwise triggered a COBRA qualifying event. See the example below from the Employee Details > Pay tab.

[Image: Employee Compensation tab detail, with Standard hours (80.00) and Default T/S Hours highlighted]

Printing Election and Extension Notices

Note: Note: No other ARPA/COBRA notices are supported at this time. Please ensure that you manually attach the mandatory Summary of the COBRA Premium Assistance Provisions under the American Rescue Plan Act to each of your extension and election notices.

Printing Election Notices

The election notice provides information about COBRA claim opportunities to an employee who experienced a qualifying event.

After you print election notices, the system will automatically update the highlighted fields on the COBRA Participant Record > F6-Dates form:

[Image: COBRA Dates Tracking form, with three fields highlighted: ARPA Notice, ARPA Election, and ARPA Extension.]

To print a COBRA election notice as mandated under the ARPA:

  1. Navigate to COBRA Administration > Process > Print Election Notices.

  2. Enter a Due Date Range Start date and a Due Date Range End date. The system uses this range to select enrollees for notice printing, based on the date their notice is due.

  3. If necessary, indicate whether this is a Reprint of notices that were already printed.

  4. Enter a client ID in Limit Process to Company if you only want to print notices for enrollees from that client.

  5. Click F2-Print.

Printing Extension Notices

Note: Note: The Qualifying Reasons and Termination Types filters behave independently of one another. If you leverage both, they will not work together to further filter employees beyond the individual filter parameters.

The extension notice provides information about COBRA claim opportunities to an employee who could have claimed COBRA benefits in the previous year but did not. It informs the employee that their window for claiming COBRA benefits was extended.

To print a COBRA extension notice as mandated under the ARPA:

  1. Navigate to COBRA Administration > Process > Print Election Notices.

  2. Click F5-ARPA Ext Notice. A form displays.

  3. In the Qualifying Event Start Date and Qualifying Event End Date fields, specify the qualifying event date range from which the system will select enrollees.

  4. If necessary, specify the Qualifying Reasons and Termination Types for which you will generate notices. See the note at the top of this section.

  5. Click F2-Print.

Feature Development in Progress

The following features are in development:

  • Option to flag COBRA benefit plans as client-sponsored, with corresponding reporting logic for the MasterTax RTS and PTS files.

Please check back on this document frequently as further features and details become available.

Recording Education Loan Payments Under the CARES Act

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/COVID/TS-CARES_Student_Loans.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/COVID/TS-CARES_Student_Loans.htm

Recording Education Loan Payments Under the CARES Act

Use the following guidance to provide support for qualifying education loan payments made to (or on the behalf of) employees, as defined under the CARES Act and as extended under the 2021 Consolidated Appropriations Act.

Background

The CARES Act (2020) expanded the IRS definition of “educational assistance” to include qualifying payments made by employers toward employee educational loans, specifically between March 27, 2020 and January 1, 2021.

In early 2021, the Consolidated Appropriations Act extended this time period through January 1, 2025. The maximum qualifying contribution, per employee per year, is $5250. These payments do not increase the gross taxable income of the employee. This article provides a helpful history and summary of these provisions.

Solution

To provide support for this legislative requirement in PrismHR, create a new pay code exempt from all federal taxes and with an annual maximum of $5250.00.

To track these education loan payments in PrismHR:

  1. Click the Back Office menu and select System|Change > Pay Codes.

  2. Fill out the required form fields, as appropriate for the type of education payment you need to track.

  3. Set the pay code maximum: clickActions menu [Image: screenshot] > Maximums.

  4. In the table, set the Basis to Annual.

  5. Set the Maximum Earnings amount to 5250.00.

  6. Click Save to return to the Pay Codes form.

  7. Finally, exempt the pay code from all federal taxes: clickActions menu [Image: screenshot] > Tax Exemptions.

  8. Specify the following tax exemption settings in the table. When you finish, click Save to return to the Pay Codes form.

Tax ID S-Corp Principals Only? Tax Exempt States
0 - Federal Income Tax No FG
1 - FUTA Employee No FG
2 - FUTA Employer No FG
3 - Social Security Employee No FG
4 - Social Security Employer No FG
6 - Medicare Employee No FG
7 - Medicare Employer No FG
  1. Click Save on the Pay Codes form.

Creating an EEO-1 Report in PrismHR

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1%20Migrated%20Task%20Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-1-Intro.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1 Migrated Task Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-1-Intro.htm

Creating an EEO-1 Report in PrismHR

PrismHR provides an array of features to support EEO-1 reporting based on the specifications and guidance from the Equal Employment Opportunity Commission (EEOC). You can find many of these features in the EEO Processing work center.

Introduction

This document describes the process of preparing, correcting, and generating an EEO-1 reporting file for submission to the EEOC.

Please review the following sections of the document, based on your needs:

  • Background – for details about the report itself, recent changes, and other general information.

  • Setting Up EEO-1 Report Data – for lists of data fields that you need to check before running these features.

  • Recommended Workflow and Known Issues – for a high-level workflow, possible pain points, and advice related to these features.

  • Basic Procedures – for instructions on building, importing, exporting, downloading, and viewing EEO-1 reporting data.

========================================================================

EEO Reports

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1%20Migrated%20Task%20Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-12-Reports.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1 Migrated Task Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-12-Reports.htm

EEO Reports

The EEO Reports section of the work center provides additional reports you can use to review and verify employee EEO reporting data:

  • EEO Detail Report – Returns detailed employee reporting data in .csv format.

  • EEO Summary Headcount Report – Returns summary totals for the different reporting categories, in .csv format.

  • EEO Download Report – Run this report immediately before you create the EEO-1 download file. This report provides a preview of the contents of the actual file. It also uses the .csv format.

For detailed information about running each of these reports, see their corresponding online help documentation.

  • Running the EEO Detail Report

  • Running the EEO-1 Summary Headcount Report

  • Creating the EEO-1 Download Report

========================================================================

Downloading the Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1%20Migrated%20Task%20Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-13-Downloading.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1 Migrated Task Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-13-Downloading.htm

Downloading the Report

When you or your clients are ready to send your EEO-1 Report to the federal government, it can be downloaded through your browser on a client by client basis, under EEO-1 File & Pay Data Reporting.

Beginning in 2021, the system pulls data from the client level instead of the global level for certain reporting fields. You can use the EEO-1 File & Pay Data Reporting form in PrismHR to configure those fields for each client. The fields are indicated in the example below – see the download procedure for additional instructions.

Note: Note: The Report As Of Date is the field that determines how the system builds data. When selecting employees for the build, the system will look for employees who have any pay periods that include that specific date.

Creating Client EEO-1 & Pay Data Files

Note: Note: Before creating the download file, ensure that you have reviewed the settings listed in the Setting Up EEO-1 Report Data and Reviewing EEO-1 Download Setup and Data sections, especially options to include or exclude clients in the file.

To download client EEO-1 and Pay Data reporting files:

  1. In the EEO Processing work center, under EEO-1 Processing > Generate EEO-1 Reporting Files, select Client EEO-1 & Pay Data Files.

  2. Use the Generate File panel to configure the file generation settings:

Field — Action

Year — Select the reporting year.

EEO Pay Period Start Date — Pay period start date to use when collecting and calculating EEO-1 data for the year.

EEO Pay Period End Date — Pay period end date to use when collecting and calculating EEO-1 data for the year.

EEO File Type — Select Federal to produce a file to EEOC specifications. Select California to produce the California Pay Data reporting file.

  1. Click Generate File. After the file generation process finishes, you can click Download to obtain the file through your browser.

  2. If there are errors or gaps in EEO reporting data, a warning message displays. This information will not be included in the report if you proceed.

  • To ﬁx errors, export the report (see Exporting the Report), ﬁx any errors, and then re-import the data.

  • To ﬁx the errors at their root, you can also directly edit the employee data within the system and complete the Build process again. If you choose to do this, note that updating employee EEO-1 information will make those changes visible to the employee as well.

========================================================================

Background

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1%20Migrated%20Task%20Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-2-Background.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1 Migrated Task Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-2-Background.htm

Background

Note: Note: The EEOC changed its reporting requirements in 2023 for 2022 reporting.

About the EEO-1 Report

Every year, employers must file the Employer Information Report, or EEO-1 Report, with the Joint Reporting Committee of the U.S. Equal Employment Opportunity Commission. The report provides a count of each company’s employees by job class, ethnicity, race, and gender.

This document describes how you can use PrismHR to create download files for each client subject to EEO-1 reporting. The standard file output from PrismHR includes multiple records supporting most EEO-1 Type codes as described below:

  • A consolidated record for the PEO or employer.

  • A headquarters record for a single client or worksite location.

  • Establishment records for each client that has 100 or more employees. In the case of companies with government contracts of $50,000 or more, the threshold for creation of an establishment record is 50+ employees.

Where applicable, clients can report each location as a separate establishment record, regardless of the number of employees working at the location. These line items will leverage EEOC status codes as detailed in the table below.

Note: Note:

  • For 2022, the EEOC modified their “type” codes, but the file structure need not be altered and will remain the same within PrismHR.

  • The “Unit Number” mentioned in the table below refers to a field in the Other Parameters panel on the Worksite Location form, called EEO Unit Number.

EEOC Status Code — Description

1 — Single Establishment Report This code indicates that the employer has a single worksite establishment under which all data is being reported. Single-Establishment Report files should contain a single line item. This file type is not directly supported within PrismHR at this time. The file for any Single-Establishment company must be slightly modified as indicated below for upload to the EEOC.

2 — Consolidated Report Record This is displayed on the first line of any download file and contains summary information. For 2022 reporting, this record is no longer required and and will be ignored by the EEOC, but will still appear on the PrismHR file output. > Note: Note: If your download is for single-establishment reporting, you must manually change this value to 1 and ensure that any worksite locations are linked (contact Customer Support for details).

3 — Headquarters Record This appears on the second line of any file from PrismHR. The EEOC requires multi-establishment employers to designate one of their establishments as a “headquarters.” > Note: Note: If your download is for single-establishment reporting, you can remove this record.

4 — 50+ Employee Establishment, Previously Reported This establishment has 50 or more employees and it is not first time reporting (a Unit Number exists for the establishment). For 2022 Reporting, the EEOC has replaced “Type 4” and “Type 8” designations with a designation of “Establishment-Level Report” to encompass both types of records. The EEOC will self determine whether an establishment meets the “Type 4” or Type 8” designation. However, the designation will remain on PrismHR file outputs, and the EEOC will overlook the self-determined designation on any file.

6 — Establishment List Report This alternate style report is no longer permitted by the EEOC as of 2022. This is not supported by PrismHR.

8 — 50- Employee Establishment, Previously Reported This establishment has less than 50 employees and it is not first time reporting (a Unit Number exists for the establishment). For 2022 reporting, the EEOC has replaced “Type 4” and “Type 8” designations with a designation of “Establishment-Level Report” to encompass both types of records. The EEOC will self-determine whether an establishment meets the “Type 4” or Type 8” designation. However, the designation will remain on PrismHR file outputs, and the EEOC will overlook the self-determined designation on any file. Additionally, for 2022, the definition of type 8 has changed. In prior years, this code was used for all establishments with under 50 employees. PrismHR currently produces a Type 8 designation for any establishment under 50 employees.

9 — New Establishment This establishment is of any size that has not previously been reported to the EEOC. These establishments will not have a Unit Number. PrismHR will automatically designate any worksite without a Unit Number over 50 employees as a Type 9 report. For 2022 reporting, the definition of type 9 has changed. In prior years this code was used for all establishments with over 50 employees that was not previously reported. PrismHR currently produces a Type 9 designation for all such establishments

Other Documentation

For more general information about EEO-1 filing and Component 1 of the report, please consult the following official resource:

2023 EEO-1 Component 1 Data Collection Instruction Booklet

Recommended Workflow and Known Issues

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1%20Migrated%20Task%20Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-4-Workflow.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1 Migrated Task Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-4-Workflow.htm

Recommended Workflow and Known Issues

In PrismHR, the general workflow for EEO-1 reporting is:

  1. Build data for all clients.

  2. Export the built data and check for errors.

  3. Fix any errors and then Import the corrected file.

  4. Review your settings on the EEO-1 file generation form (either Service Provider or Client) and make sure you are including or excluding clients correctly.

  5. Generate the Download file.

  6. As needed, update your client inclusion settings and generate the next Download file.

  7. Continue until you have all your report files.

Additional Known Issues and Limitations

Generating a Single Report File for Corporate and Client Reporting

If you need to submit a single EEO-1 file that combines data from your corporate account database and your client accounts database, you need to generate both download files and then perform some manual adjustments to combine the two download files into one:

  1. For each data point in the Consolidated (status code 2) records, you must calculate the sum of the value on the corporate file and the value on the client file. The Consolidated record contains the totals for each file, so your combined file must include a single Status Code 2 record that reflects the overall sum of the totals across both files.

  2. Make sure to use the Headquarters (status code 3) record from your corporate account. Modify the Headquarters record from your client download file and change its status code to 4, 8, or 9 as appropriate (see About the EEO-1 Report above).

  3. Paste your modified client-level Headquarters record – now with status code 4, 8, or 9 – into the corporate download file.

  4. Copy all other Establishment records from your client download file and paste them into your corporate download file.

  5. Use this updated file for EEOC reporting.

Other Known Issues

  • Our current feature set only produces download files in the multi-establishment format. You will need to make some minor, manual modifications to each output file to bring it in line with the single-establishment format specifications.

  • In the April 2021 release, we added an additional gender field to the system. This field allows employees to better self-identify in the Employee Portal, but it is not leveraged by the EEO-1 reporting logic. Instead, you must manually record non-binary employees in the comments of your reporting file.

Basic Procedures

This section describes the core components of the EEO-1 feature set, including:

  • Building EEO-1 Data

  • Exporting EEO-1 Data

  • Importing EEO-1 Data

Note: Note: You can also clear EEO-1 data that you have build, using a dedicated form. For details, see the online help for the Clear EEO-1 Data form.

These sections are organized to replicate the order in which you would perform them during the EEO-1 reporting process. As described in the sections above, you can run builds, imports, exports, and downloads as many times as needed to ensure that your data is correct.

Building EEO-1 Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1%20Migrated%20Task%20Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-6-Building.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1 Migrated Task Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-6-Building.htm

Building EEO-1 Data

Before creating the EEO-1 Report, you must build the reporting data into the database. The build process varies depending on how you have chosen to set up your report information.

Process

In PrismHR, select Work Centers > Compliance > EEO Processing and under EEO-1 Report Processing > Build Data Registers, select Build EEO-1 & Pay Data. Then, fill out each section of the form specified below.

When you are ready to start the data build, see Running the Build, below.

EEO-1 Information

Review or set up the following fields according to your reporting needs. This data will display in Consolidated record of the EEO-1 reporting file.

Field — Action

Unit number Assigned by the EEOC — Enter the unit number assigned by the EEOC.

Headquarters Company — Enter the company headquarters ID number.

Headquarters Location — Enter the company headquarters location ID number.

Is Federal Contractor or Financial Institution — Select this option if the company counts as a financial institution or does contract work for the federal government.

Dun & Bradstreet Number — If the company has a Dun & Bradstreet number, enter it here. > Note: Note: Data entered on this field will still appear in the 2022 and prior downloads only. This will no longer be used from 2023 and will be ignored by the EEOC.

Unique Entity ID — If you do business with the federal government, enter your 12-character, alphanumeric Unique Entity Identifier (UEI) here. This number can be entered on both the client and worksite level. Any Unique Entity ID on the worksite level will just be reported under that worksite establishment. Any unique entity id on the client level will be applied to all other establishments for that client. > Note: Note: For purposes of the EEO-1 Component 1 data collection, eligible federal contractors will no longer provide the “DUNS Numbers” associated with their headquarters and/or establishments in the EEO-1 Component 1 Online Filing System (OFS) or in any data files uploaded to the OFS. Begining with the 2022 EEO-1 Component 1 data collection, employers (for example single-establishment and multi-establishment employers) that are federal contactors must instead provide UEIs. For example, a multi-establishment employer must provide UEIs for any headquarters and/or establishments which are federal contractors.

Note: Note:

  • On the Client EEO-1 & Pay Data Files form, these fields are overwritten on a client by client basis but must still be entered here.

  • On the Service Provider EEO-1 Files form, under the Generate EEO-1 Files section, the Report As-Of Date field is used as the pay period end date for all clients included in the EEO-1 build.

Client Settings

Use this section to determine the inclusion and exclusion of client data in the data build process.

Field Action
Exclude ASO Companies Select this option to exclude clients set up as Administrative Services Organizations.
Build Data For Specify whether the system will build data for: - All clients - All clients except for ASOs - Only specific clients (a grid displays for you to specify the clients) - All clients except specific clients (a grid displays for you to specify the clients)
  • All clients
  • All clients except for ASOs
  • Only specific clients (a grid displays for you to specify the clients)
  • All clients except specific clients (a grid displays for you to specify the clients) ### Running the Build

To build EEO data:

  1. Complete the following setup fields in the Build Options panel:
Field Action
Report As‑Of Date The date for the report, based on year and employee-related information (such as active/inactive status and position). > Note: Note: As a best practice, PrismHR recommends using the date for the last scheduled payroll batch of the reporting year.
Build Only if 100/50 Threshold Met Select this option to only build data for the following clients: - Clients who are not Federal Contractors and who have 100+ employees - Clients who are Federal Contractors and who have 50+ employees > Note: Note: The values 100 and 50, above, are defined based on the EEO-1 build logic (specifically the Report As Of Date). These values do not refer to employee counts in general.
  • Clients who are not Federal Contractors and who have 100+ employees
  • Clients who are Federal Contractors and who have 50+ employees | Use 40/20 Hours Worked Approximation | For FLSA exempt employees: - To display an approximation of hours worked based on full/part‑time status and weeks worked, enable this option. Full-time exempt employees display 40 hours a week. Part-time exempt employees display 20 hours a week. - To display the actual number of hours worked, leave this option unchecked. |
  • To display an approximation of hours worked based on full/part‑time status and weeks worked, enable this option. Full-time exempt employees display 40 hours a week. Part-time exempt employees display 20 hours a week.
  • To display the actual number of hours worked, leave this option unchecked. | Build Component 2 Data | This data is currently not reported, and this option should not be selected. It is preserved purely for prior year reporting. Check this box to include component 2 data in the EEO-1 build. |
  1. Click Build. The Job Control form displays the progress of the build process.

The EEO-1 data is now in the system and ready to be included in your report.

Exporting EEO-1 Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1%20Migrated%20Task%20Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-8-Exporting.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1 Migrated Task Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-8-Exporting.htm

Exporting EEO-1 Data

The export feature lets you view EEO-1 data on an employee-by-employee level. The format of the exported file is identical to that of the import file. This lets you fix any errors in employee EEO-1 data and then re-import quickly. To expedite this process, you can also choose to export only employees with errors.

  • Use the Export EEO-1 Data feature to create .csv export files of employee-level EEO-1 data from the latest build.

  • After exporting this data, you can review the export file for data errors.

  • Unlike the HRPyramid version of this process, which designated errors using an asterisk (*), this process prints the word Error to indicate incorrect or missing data.

  • After correcting the error fields on the export file, re-upload it using the EEO-1 import process to correct the data in the system. For details, see Importing EEO-1 Data.

  • If the file contains errors, the relevant records will display at the top of the export file (see the examples below). This also differs from the HRPyramid process, which printed errors at the end of the file.

Examples of the export are available in the online help for the Export EEO-1 Data form. See Exporting EEO-1 Data.

Note: Note: This file does not meet the specifications for submission to the EEOC and contains information about specific employees. To submit your EEO-1 report to the federal government, proceed to Downloading the Report below.

To export EEO-1 data from the most recent build:

  1. Go to Work Centers > Compliance > EEO Processing > EEO-1 Report Processing > Import & Export Data > Export EEO-1 Data.

  2. Select the Year of the data to export.

  3. Specify whether to export all employees or only employees with invalid positions, gender, or ethnicity.

If you export all employees, employees with errors will display at the top of the output file.

  1. Click Export Data. A new form displays the progress of the export.

  2. Review the export progress and click Close when finished.

  3. On the Export EEO-1 Data form, click Download EEO-1 Data. Your browser displays the downloaded .csv file.

  4. Open the export file and review its contents. Make modifications if necessary.

========================================================================

Importing EEO-1 Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1%20Migrated%20Task%20Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-9-Importing.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/EEO/EEO-1 Migrated Task Sheet/TS-EEO-Migrated-9-Importing.htm

Importing EEO-1 Data

Use the Import EEO-1 Data form to correct any errors that were identified by the Export EEO-1 Data feature. The form also provides some import preferences you can customize.

Note: Note: Use either .txt. or .csv files with this feature. The format of the input file must match the format generated by the EEO-1 data export feature. For details, see Exporting EEO-1 Data.

Import File Requirements

Detailed information about import file requirements can be found in the online help for the Import EEO-1 Data form. See Importing EEO-1 Data.

Procedure

To import EEO-1 data into PrismHR:

  1. Go to Work Centers > Compliance > EEO Processing > EEO-1 Report Processing > Import & Export Data > Import EEO-1 Data.

  2. The EEO-1 Report As Of Date populates automatically from the EEO-1 configuration. Change this value if necessary.

  3. Use the Replace Behavior option to specify how the import process works:

  • Modify and Replace Existing Data – if data exists in the system for employees listed in the file, PrismHR overwrites that data with the data from the import file.

  • Clear All Data and Replace – PrismHR deletes all EEO-1 register data for all employees (even employees not listed in the import file) and creates new EEO-1 registers using only data from the import file.

  1. Select whether to Import Employees That Are Not in Prism. Otherwise, PrismHR will ignore data for any employees not already in the EEO-1 registers.

For details, review the Import File Requirements section, above.

  1. In the Import File panel, specify the number of Header Lines to Skip. For example, enter 1 if the import file has header data or labels in the first row.

  2. Click Choose File. A new form displays where you can upload the import file.

  3. Upload the file and click submit.

  4. Click Import Data to run the import. The Job Control form displays the imported employees.

  5. After the process completes, select Error Report to review any errors.

  • EEO Summary Headcount Report – Use this report to return summary totals for the different reporting categories.

  • EEO-1 Download Report – Run this report before you create the EEO-1 download file. This report provides a preview of the contents of the actual file.

The following sections describe these settings and reports in more detail, so you can ensure that your data is configured correctly.

Quick Start Guide for EEO-1 Processing

Quick Start Guide for EEO-1 Processing

Every year, employers must file the Employer Information Report, or EEO-1 Report, with the Joint Reporting Committee of the U.S. Equal Employment Opportunity Commission. The report includes a count of each company’s employees by job class, ethnicity, race, and gender, and may also include pay information.

This document provides a high-level outline of the EEO-1 processing workflow in PrismHR, including how to comply with the new client-level reporting requirement from the EEOC.

Introduction

The general workflow is depicted in the diagram below.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: While not shown in the diagram, you can clear EEO-1 data that you previously built. You can also correct data errors using the relevant PrismHR forms instead of the export feature. However, you must remember to clear and rebuild your EEO-1 data in this case.

All core EEO-1 features are now located in the new EEO Processing Work Center.

EEO Processing Work Center

[Image: screenshot]

Client-level Reporting in PrismHR

You can use one of the above forms to generate EEO-1 and California Pay Data reporting files for particular clients, in alignment with the latest guidelines. The client-level download form is located in the EEO Processing work center, and is called Client EEO-1 & Pay Data Files.

Clients can also self-serve from this form: you can provide client users with access to the form and they can run the download themselves.

Client EEO-1 & Pay Data Settings

[Image: screenshot]

However, there are a number of important steps you need to complete before this data is ready for clients to download. The utilities in the EEO Processing center can help you compile that data and correct any errors before you make the files accessible to clients.

At a high level, the EEO-1 processing workflow can be divided into 5 steps:

# Step Details Related Forms
1 Make sure all the relevant reporting data is set up correctly. If this setup is done correctly ahead of time, it will significantly simplify the procedure and minimize build errors. Various forms are involved. See the longer task sheet, Generating EEO-1 Reporting Files, for a complete list of forms and fields. See Setting Up EEO-1 Report Data
2 Build data into the database. You set the parameters for the build. PrismHR creates the database automatically. Build EEO-1 & Pay Data

- EEO Summary Headcount Report
- EEO Download Report
5 Download Reporting Files PrismHR supports generation of reporting files for both service providers and client companies. For self-service purposes, you can also give clients access to the client-level download form (see above). Client EEO-1 & Pay Data Files Service Provider EEO-1 Files (if needed)

Creating California Pay Data Reporting Files

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/EEO/TS-CA_PayData_Reporting.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/EEO/TS-CA_PayData_Reporting.htm

Creating California Pay Data Reporting Files

Beginning in 2021, any organization that:

  • is subject to EEO reporting and

  • has employees assigned to or working from an establishment in California

must annually report data about their employees’ pay and hours worked to the California Department of Fair Employment and Housing (DFEH). The report must also be broken down by employer, gender, ethnicity, and other factors.

By leveraging the same features we used to create the (currently not required) “Component 2” of the federal EEO-1 report, PrismHR supports the generation of this report and its state-specific requirements. These requirements include a non-binary gender option and logic to determine whether an employee worked in, or reported to, an establishment in California during the prior year.

If you need to review details about the federal EEO-1 reporting features, please see the article on Creating California Pay Data Reporting Files. For detailed information about California reporting, please consult the state government’s user guide.

Introduction

To determine which employees to include on the report, employers must first select a “Snapshot” pay period that falls between October 1, 2020 and December 31, 2020. They must report on each employee’s establishment, job category, race/ethnicity, sex, earnings, and hours worked, and must group the employees on the report using these characteristics.

Unlike the federal EEO-1, the California report also requires that:

  • Non-binary employees be reported in the same manner as male and female employees.

  • When calculating employee earnings, employers use Form W-2 Box 5 wages if the employee was subject to Medicare withholding, and use Box 1 wages otherwise.

  • Paid time off be included in the employee hours worked calculation.

  • Employers with multiple establishments report non-consolidated data for all establishments, even those that do not strictly meet the reporting requirements.

  • Employers submit corrected reports if corrections were made to an employee Form W-2, and those resulted in changes to that employee’s report data.

Note: Note: California uses the categories “Employer” and “Establishment” to divide up its report sections. The meaning of “Employer” on this report is not equivalent to its meaning in PrismHR. For California reporting purposes, for PEOs in particular, the “Employer” is the equivalent of a client and “Establishments” are (roughly) the equivalent of worksite locations. Depending on the structure of your organization, more complex configurations are also possible. PrismHR provides some settings to account for these organizational structures, but some service providers with more unorthodox configurations will need to manually adjust their report data to ensure that each entity is reported accurately.

Before You Begin

Make sure that you have set up your required reporting data in other parts of the system. These forms and fields are detailed in the Creating an EEO-1 Report Download article, available at the link above. Please review these requirements to ensure that your data is set up properly.

Note About California Position Codes

On the federal EEO-1 report, there are ten position codes: the first two are 1-1 and 1-2, and the others are numbered 2 through 9.

On the California report, there are still ten position codes, but they are numbered 1 through 10.

Please only use the federal position codes in the system and do not try to enter position codes 1 or 10. Please also note that, while the Export feature will still export data that includes the federal position codes, these will be translated into the California-specific codes when you create your California submission file.

Setting Up Report Data

Note: Note: The descriptions of these fields are simplifications of the official reporting requirements. If you have any questions regarding what to enter in certain fields, consult the guidelines provided by California or contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

You can use the new EEO-1 File & Pay Data Reporting form to set up organization-specific pay reporting information. As its name implies, this form can be used to set up report data for both the federal EEO-1 and the California Pay Data reports.

Main Setup Form

[Image: screenshot]

Bottom of the Setup Form (File Generation)

[Image: screenshot]

To set up California Pay Data reporting information:

  1. First, ensure that you selected the correct client.

  2. Click theReports menu and then select Client > Report > EEO-1 File & Pay Data Reporting.

  3. Review the Company Information panel. This information pre-populates on the form based on the currently selected client, and cannot be edited directly. If you need to make changes to this data, do it on the associated setup form (in this case, Client Details). You can also adjust this information on the output file, if needed.

  4. Next, fill out the Report Parameters panel:

Field Name — Description

Headquarters Location — Select the Worksite Location of the organization’s headquarters. The report will display the associated address information.

Federal EEO-1 filed in prior year — Select this option if your organization filed a federal-level EEO-1 report last year.

Entire Company has at least 100 Employees — Select this option if the client employs at least 100 people.

In Enterprise with at least 100 Employees — Select this option if you meet this condition according to California reporting guidelines.

Federal Contractor Or Financial Institution — Select whether the client is a financial institution or a contractor for the federal government.

Dun & Bradstreet Number — Enter the employer’s primary Dun & Bradstreet (DUNS) number.

  1. Enter contact information for the Certifying Official.

  2. Enter California-specific reporting information in the California Pay Data panel:

Field Name — Description

Company is a California State Contractor — Select this option if the client is a contractor for the State of California.

California Pay Data filed in Prior Year — Select this option if this client filed this report last year.

California SEIN — > Note: Note: This field is auto-populated and cannot be edited. The California state ID number for this client.

  1. If the client and zero or more other clients belong to a parent company, enter that information in the Parent Company (If applicable) section. List any Parent FEIN(s) and Affiliated FEIN(s) in the provided tables.

  2. If you want to include data from other clients in your system while creating the California download file, enter those in the table marked Additional Companies to be Included in this Companies EEO-1. Use this feature if multiple clients in your system share a single FEIN.

  3. Click Save.

Please note that, at this time, certain fields do not have worksite location override options. This includes Federal EEO-1 Filed in Prior Year?, NAICS Code, Major Activity, and California Pay Data filed in Prior Year (though this last field will be “No” for all 2021 filings). PrismHR is working to provide support for location-based overrides of these fields. If you need to change these values on the worksite level, you can still manually edit the data in the final Download file.

Building Report Data

Similar to the federal EEO-1 reporting process, you must Build your report data before you can transmit it. When you build report data, the system organizes this data and incorporates it into specific database registers, in preparation for the generation of export and download files.

The EEO-1 Download form, which is used to initiate the Build process, contains various fields intended only for federal-level EEO-1 reporting. When running the California Pay Data report, you only need to worry about these specific fields: Start Pay Date, Report As Of Date, Show Employee If Pay is Zero, and Include/Exclude Clients.

Note: Note: The Report As Of Date is the field that determines how the system builds data. When selecting employees for the build, the system will look for employees who have any pay periods that include that specific date.

Field Name — Description and Important Notes

Start Pay Date and Report As Of Date — The system uses these values to determine which payroll vouchers it needs to look up while performing the data build. This corresponds to the California “Snapshot” range (inclusive of the dates in these fields). If you are using different snapshot periods for different clients, be sure to change the dates on this form and then re-build the data each time. In PrismHR, on the EEO-1 File & Pay Data Reporting form, these dates display as read-only values in the Pay Period Start Date and Pay Period End Date fields.

Show Employee If Pay is Zero — During the build process, if this field is set to Y, the system will include data for employees who had an Active status class within the provided date range, even if they were not paid during that period. Otherwise, these employees will be left off the data build.

Include/Exclude Clients — If you want to include (or exclude) data from multiple clients in your build, use this table to specify those details. Enter I for “Include” or E for “Exclude.” This feature is useful, for instance, if your system breaks a single-FEIN company into several client entities. When building California pay data for that company, you can include data from all the associated client entities in a single build. If you select I and then leave this table blank, the system will build data for any clients that have 100 or more employees within the specified date range.

Before You Build

When you build EEO-1 and Pay Data Reporting information, the system first looks at the dates you entered and selects any payrolls with pay period start and end dates that “cross” the Report As Of Date. The system records data for the employees who were compensated in those batches.

Note: Note: Even though the contents of the California and Federal downloads are slightly different, the ultimate, system-built source of this data is the same. There are no separate “California” and “Federal” build processes: only separate download files that leverage the data in different ways.

Please also note that the federal output has not yet been updated for 2021, so the sentence above is currently true only in principle. PrismHR is developing these processes in such a way that, when federal guidance is more solid, this “one build, many downloads” logic is retained.

Running a Build will overwrite any data from prior builds. However, Import data will remain in the system unless you purposefully delete it using the Clear button. In addition, the date ranges that populate on the form are determined by the last build/import for that client and year.

More About the Build Process

Refer to the table below for general descriptions of the build logic for employee hours, wages, and California indicators:

Calculation Logic
Hours The system calculates this value using all employee Hours Worked within the snapshot period, unless those hours were paid under a Standard, Earnings-based, Hourly pay code with a rate class that is not Premium or DoubleTime, and which is not flagged for paying productive work (in other words, a general setup for PTO). In this case the system will use the Hours Paid under that pay code. The system excludes any pay codes flagged for exclusion from ACA reporting.
Wages Wages are calculating using data from Form W-2, Box 5. Wages are also built using data from Box 1, and will be leveraged if Box 5 wages do not exist. The system retrieves this information directly from employee payroll voucher data, or from client-level pay data in the case of multiple clients. Regardless of whether Box 1 or Box 5 wages are used, the logic is the same: - If the employee works for a single client and receives a single Form W-2, the system uses the data from this Form W-2. - If the employee works for multiple clients and receives a single Form W-2, the system splits the Form W-2 data proportionally for each client. - If the employee works for a single client and receives multiple Forms W-2, the system aggregates the values from each form. On the final download, if one or more employees in a particular row did not have Box 5 wages, the system will populate a Comment specifying the number of employees for whom this was the case.
  • If the employee works for a single client and receives a single Form W-2, the system uses the data from this Form W-2.
  • If the employee works for multiple clients and receives a single Form W-2, the system splits the Form W-2 data proportionally for each client.
  • If the employee works for a single client and receives multiple Forms W-2, the system aggregates the values from each form. | Include on California Download? | Y - If, on the Report As Of Date for this build, any of the following are true: - The employee’s assigned worksite location (for the purposes of this report) is in California. In other words, they “report to” a California location. - The employee’s actual worksite location is in California (not just for the purposes of the report). In other words, they “work from” a California location. - California SUTA wages accrued on the employee’s voucher during a pay period that falls within the specified date range - The employee had no vouchers within the specified date range, but did accrue California SUTA wages at some point in the reporting year N - If none of the above are true. |
  • The employee’s assigned worksite location (for the purposes of this report) is in California. In other words, they “report to” a California location.
  • The employee’s actual worksite location is in California (not just for the purposes of the report). In other words, they “work from” a California location.
  • California SUTA wages accrued on the employee’s voucher during a pay period that falls within the specified date range
  • The employee had no vouchers within the specified date range, but did accrue California SUTA wages at some point in the reporting year ### Procedure

To build California pay data:

  1. Navigate to your usual EEO-1 file build screen.

  2. Review the data in the fields listed above. Be sure to change the Start Pay Date and Report As Of Date values to match your intended snapshot period, and include data from any other clients as necessary.

  3. When your configuration is ready, click Build.

  4. When the build finishes, click Continue. The data is now ready for import, export, or file generation. Be sure to review and correct your data using the Import/Export features before you submit it.

Using Import and Export to Handle Errors

Note: Note: The export file is not your download file. Only submit the Download file to the State of California.

After you build your data, PrismHR recommends exporting it to check for any errors that might have occurred during the build process.

As in the federal feature set, the system will display a warning if it encounters any non-fatal errors while building a particular piece of data, and will populate an asterisk (*) in that field on the export, so you can easily locate and correct it. If an asterisk still exists for an employee when you generate the Download file, that employee will not be included on the file. Fatal errors will cause the build process to stop and the system will display a message indicating that a fatal error occurred.

Feel free to edit any other fields on the download if necessary (not just the ones with asterisks). Service providers with more complex structures may need to manually adjust their data at this stage. After reviewing the exported data and correcting it, you can Import your corrected data back into the system.

To review and correct report data:

  1. Ensure that you are on Screen 2 of the EEO-1 Download form.

  2. Click the Export button.

  3. Enter the PC Folder Path and PC Filename, which specify where to save the export file.

  4. Click F2-Save. The system generates a .XLS file, which you can open in a spreadsheet viewer like Microsoft Excel.

  5. Search for any asterisks in the spreadsheet and correct the data.

  6. When you finish correcting the file, save it as a .TXT file (not .XLS).

  7. Return to the EEO-1 Download form and select Import. A dialog displays.

  8. Select Yes. A file upload dialog displays.

  9. In the new dialog, enter the PC Folder Path and PC Filename of the .TXT file. You must append .TXT to the PC Filename.

  10. Click F2-Done. A dialog displays asking whether you want to Add or Replace the import file.

  11. Select Replace. The system returns you to the EEO-1 Download form.

  12. Click F2-Save.

California-specific Export Columns

The EEO-1 Import/Export feature was modified and now includes new columns to support certain fields required by California (and possibly, in future, the Federal Government):

Column — Notes

A — Year

B — Company

C — Employee ID

D — Worksite Location

E — Originally used for Form W-2 Box 1 wages. Now it displays Box 5 wages.

F — Originally used for Hours Worked. Now this column also includes employee Hours Paid.

G — No changes to this column, but keep in mind that the Job Category/Position codes will be different on the different output files. For more information, see the Note About California Position Codes.

H — The Gender column now supports M, F, and X as valid values.

I — Race/Ethnicity (no changes were made to this field from the federal-level output file).

J — Optional new column. Displays Form W-2 Box 1 wages if the value in Column E is 0. On the final download, if one or more employees in a particular row did not have Box 5 wages, the system will populate a Comment specifying the number of employees for whom this was the case.

K — Optional new column. Currently, this column is unused: it is intended as a placeholder for “binary gender” reporting in case federal guidelines do not change this year. Until that happens, this column just displays the same value as Column H.

L — Optional new column: “Include on California Output?” Valid values are Y and N according to certain internal system rules, or blank (empty). Y means the employee’s data will appear on the California Pay Data output. If this field is set to N or is empty, the employee’s data will not appear on this output file.

M through Q — Informational only.

Creating the Report Download

Note: Note: The file will only generate if data has already been built for that company and year, and 1 or more employees within that build are flagged for inclusion on the California output file (Column L on the export file = Y).

After building, reviewing, and correcting your data, you are ready to create the official reporting file. You can perform this final step in PrismHR.

To create the California pay data report download file:

  1. Log back into PrismHR and ensure that you have selected the correct client.

  2. Click theReports menu and then select Client > Report > EEO-1 File & Pay Data Reporting.

  3. Review the Pay Period Start Date and Pay Period End Date to ensure that they capture the appropriate snapshot period for this reporting entity. If need to edit these values, you must change them on the build screen and rebuild your data (see Building Report Data above).

  4. At the bottom of the page, set EEO File Type to California.

  5. Click Download CA PayData Report. The system displays a Job Control Status Report illustrating the download progress.

  6. When the download finishes, click Close.
