Using PrismHR to Comply with California SB-95

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/Forms/TS-CA_SB-95.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/Forms/TS-CA_SB-95.htm

Using PrismHR to Comply with California SB-95

Note: Note: To help ensure that your SB-95 pay codes do not exceed any limits, PrismHR recommends using the Pay Codes >Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

Maximums form to set up maximum earnings amounts.

Background

In March 2021, the California state government passed Senate Bill No. 95 (henceforth called SB-95), which mandates the retroactive provision of paid sick leave to employees for work missed as a result of the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic. This legislation applies to any COVID-related sick leave taken by employees between January 1, 2021 and September 30, 2021.

While PrismHR does not include features specifically developed for compliance with SB-95, you can leverage existing features to manage compliant sick time and, if appropriate, also claim federal (FFCRA - Families First Coronavirus Response Act) credits on that sick time.

In particular, you can use the system’s PTO features to ensure that sick leave paid under SB-95 will appear on employee pay stubs (this is required by the legislation).

For additional details about SB-95, please see California’s official FAQ.

General Procedure

To configure the system for compliance with California SB-95:

  1. If needed, use the COVID-19 Leave Estimate Report to calculate the required PTO hours for part-time/variable hour employees. See Estimating Employee SB-95 PTO Hours.

  2. Set up new PTO plans to pay full-time and part-time employees for the appropriate number of PTO hours. See Setting Up SB-95 PTO Plans.

  3. Enroll your employees in the new PTO plans. See Enrolling Employees in SB-95 PTO Plans

  4. As an optional step, you can associate these PTO plans with federal COVID-19 compliance features in order to claim federally specified credits on all or part of these sick leave payments. See Integrating SB-95 PTO Plans with Federal COVID-19 Features.

  5. Pay your employees for their leave under the appropriate PTO plans. The use of a PTO plan will ensure that COVID-19 sick leave payments display on employee pay stubs, as mandated by the legislation.

Estimating Employee SB-95 PTO Hours

There are many similarities between the California SB-95 requirements and the employee paid sick leave hours mandated by the FFRCA. As a result, the COVID-19 Leave Estimate Report can provide insight into the number of PTO hours you need to assign to each plan.

To determine hours estimates for affected employees:

  1. Click theReports menu .

  2. Select COVID-19 Leave Estimate Report under HR|Report.

  3. In the As Of Date field, enter a date that allows you to capture the six-month look-back period appropriately. The period’s end date is determined by the value in this field.

  4. Click Run. The report generates and displays.

  5. Note the value in each employee’s Sick Leave Hours - Suggested column.

The report uses the following basic calculation to determine this value:

  • Full-time employees receive 80 hours on the report automatically.

  • Part-time employees receive the greater of the following two values: either a 6-month look-back average of their hours OR their Standard Hours in PrismHR.

Based on the results of the report, you can set up employee SB-95 PTO plans and specify the number of allowed hours under that plan.

Setting Up SB-95 PTO Plans

You can use PrismHR’s existing PTO functionality to set up these new COVID-related paid sick leave plans for your employees. To conserve space, this document assumes that you are familiar with the PTO feature set and have reviewed the sections in the Paid Time Off User Guide related to the setup of new plans. In short, you may need to:

  • Create new PTO Classes and PTO Register Types to track this sick leave.

  • Set up one or more new PTO Benefit Plans for SB-95 paid sick leave.

  • Create one or more PTO Absence Codes.

  • Associate those PTO Absence Codes with corresponding Register Types.

The Copy from another plan feature can be especially useful during setup. This setting, located on the Client > Change > PTO Benefit Plans form, can shorten some of your setup time if you have a preexisting PTO benefit plan for employee sick leave. For details, see Defining PTO Benefit Plans in the Paid Time Off User Guide.

Click here to view this guide on the PrismHR Customer Resource Center.

[Image: PTO Benefit Plans form. “Copy From Another Plan” option is highlighted.]

At your discretion, you can make these plans visible to your employees through Employee Portal (EP), or manage them solely from PrismHR itself. The PTO user guide also includes instructions for sharing PTO plans through EP.

If you still have questions about PTO plan setup after consulting the user guide, please contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team for assistance.

Enrolling Employees in SB-95 PTO Plans

Please refer to the guidance provided by the State of California when you determine the employees impacted by this new legislation. When you have identified those employees, you can use the HR|Action > PTO Register Enrollment form to enroll each employee in your newly created plan and associate them with the appropriate PTO register type.

You can also enroll multiple employees automatically by configuring a PTO Auto Enroll Rule and then importing the affected employees’ hours. For more information, see the Processing PTO Auto Register Enrollment topic in the Paid Time Off User Guide. For general details about enrollment, you can review the entire Maintaining Employee PTO Information chapter.

Click here to view this guide on the PrismHR Customer Resource Center.

[Image: PTO Register Enrollment form, with all fields blank.]

Integrating SB-95 PTO Plans with Federal COVID-19 Features

If the reasons for an employee’s SB-95 sick leave are also valid for paying sick leave under the FFCRA, you can use pay codes as a sort of bridge between your SB-95 PTO absence codes and the system-level COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters. This enables you to accrue FFCRA credits on those leave payments while also displaying sick leave on vouchers, in compliance with SB-95.

Before you begin, you need to set up new pay codes to associate with both your system-level COVID-19 parameters and your new PTO absence codes. As with the setup of the ARPA feature set, these pay codes should be identical to your FFCRA pay codes, except that:

  • They do not have any tax exemptions.

  • They do not have their Factor set to 0.67 (these are only used on FFCRA-specific pay codes).

If you pursue this strategy, you may also want to create a third SB-95 pay code to pay sick leave that does not qualify for FFCRA credits.

Note: Note: As part of this procedure, you need to set your system- or client-level Default Wage Cap Logic to No Limits. This is because California defines a higher wage limit for dependent care sick leave under SB-95. If the system is set up to respect federal wage limits, you will be unable to continue paying SB-95 sick leave after that lower wage limit is reached. Therefore, we recommend setting up this integration only if you are ready to closely monitor your federal- vs. state-compliant wage accumulation.

Instead of changing the setting on the system-level Default Wage Cap Logic, you also have the option to change the setting only on clients with employes impacted by SB-95. To do this, open the COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters >Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

Client Overrides form and adjust the settings for each affected client.

To set up an integration between SB-95 PTO plans and FFCRA credit accumulation:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. Select COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters under System|Change.

  3. Add the pay codes you created to the following fields:

  • COVID-19 Emergency Paid Sick Leave (Self-Care) Pay Code

  • Emergency Paid Sick Leave (Dependent-Care) Pay Code

This will ensure that FFCRA credits accumulate on your SB-95 sick wages.

  1. Set the Default Wage Cap Logic (on either the system or client level) to No Limits.

  2. Click Save.

  3. Open the Client menu.

  4. Under Client|Change, select PTO Absence Codes. The form displays.

  5. Enter or select the Absence Code you created for your SB-95 PTO plans.

  6. Complete all the relevant fields. In the Pay Code table, add the same pay codes you added in step 2.

  7. If you want to offer these absence codes as leave requests in Employee Portal, select the Enable in Employee Portal setting and follow the instructions in the note below.

  8. When you finish, click Save.

Note: Note: If you added multiple pay codes to the PTO Absence Codes form and want to display these PTO benefit plans in Employee Portal, you need to specify one of those codes in the Leave Pay Code field. This field determines the pay code that will ultimately display on the employee’s time sheet when they request SB-95 leave. While the employee’s requested hours will deduct from all pay codes specified on this form, only the code specified in the Leave Pay Code field will be incorporated into the payroll and printed on the employee’s voucher.

Activating the MasterTax Interface

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/MasterTax_Interface_Setup/Activating%20the%20MasterTax%20Interface.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/MasterTax_Interface_Setup/Activating the MasterTax Interface.htm

After PrismHR finishes rebuilding the Tax Journal file, and before any live payrolls are posted, you must activate the interface.

Building the Interface Files

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/MasterTax_Interface_Setup/Building%20the%20Interface%20Files.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/MasterTax_Interface_Setup/Building the Interface Files.htm

Building the Interface Files

There are three files that pass tax data into the MasterTax system from PrismHR:

  • Payroll Tax Summary Import file (PTS)

  • Reconciliation Tax Summary Import file (RTS)

  • Monthly Tax Summary (MTS). This only pulls the tax codes for Pennsylvania and Illinois.

Note: Note: You must build the PTS and RTS files in order to use MasterTax. The MTS file is optional.

Current Limitations (RTS)

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/MasterTax_Interface_Setup/Current%20Limitations%20(RTS).htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/MasterTax_Interface_Setup/Current Limitations (RTS).htm

Current Limitations (RTS)

Several statistics in the MasterTax RTS specification are not tracked by PrismHR and are not included in the current interface. The statistics are:

  • New Jersey Statistic 5: The number of employees covered under a private state disability plan during the quarter.

  • Oregon Statistic 5: The number of hours worked during the quarter.

  • Utah Statistic 5: The number of new hires during the quarter.

Setting Up New York SDI Tax

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/NY-SDI_Tax.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/NY-SDI_Tax.htm

Setting Up New York SDI Tax

The New York State Disability Insurance (NY SDI) Tax pays for temporary disability insurance for employees within the State of New York. The system calculates the NY SDI tax directly for employees and optionally for client. The system bypasses Vertex tax calculation engine for this tax. Therefore, your organization must set up the tax rates in PrismHR. See Calculating NY SDI Tax for more information on how PrismHR calculates the NY SDI tax.

Note: Note: As of January 1, 2011, the rate for calculating employee NY SDI is 0.50% of wages per week up to a maximum of $0.60.

Establishing Tax Rates and Limits

Set various tax rates and limits in the Tax Rates form.

To establish tax rates and limits:

  1. Click [Image: screenshot] (Back Office).

  2. From System|Change select System Parameters.

  3. Select Tax Rate Maintenance from the Actions menu. The Tax Rates form opens. [Image: screenshot] The employee tax is always calculated. The employer (client) tax calculation is optional. To calculate the employer (client) tax, enter the monthly tax rate per employee.

  4. Complete these fields:

Field — Description

New York SDI Max Per Week — Enter the amount that is deducted each week from an employee who lives in New York.

New York SDI Percent — Enter the percent that is the taxable gross rate.

New York SDI Employer Rate (EE/Month) — Enter the employer rate that is calculated monthly for each employee who lives in New York.

Allow Override By Client — Select to permit overrides of the NY SDI tax.

  1. Click Save.

Setting Tax Parameters

Set tax information for the client in the Tax Parameters panel.

To set tax parameters:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.

  3. Select the Tax tab. [Image: screenshot]

  4. Complete the following fields:

Field — Description

Calculate Employer Paid New York SDI — Select to calculate the employer-paid New York disability tax for each of the client’s employees.

New York SDI Employer Rate (EE/Month) — Enter the insurance premium rate (per employee/per month) paid by the client for New York Disability Insurance.

New York MCT Override Rate — Enter the rate based on the total company payroll expense. If left blank, the system uses the default rate of 0.34% to calculate the tax.

  1. Click Save.

Note: Note: To credit the employer (client) for the amount of any NY SDI that has been deducted from an employee’s pay check, you must set up an unbundled billing rule. See the Payroll User Guide for more information on setting up unbundled billing rules.

Calculating NY SDI Tax

The system calculates the NY SDI tax for employees and employers (client).

Tax Type Batch Type Replaces Regular Batch? Voucher Type Calculation Method
Employee Regular N/A Regular Lesser of (Weeks-Worked X 0.60) or (Taxable Gross X 0.05%)
Employee Regular N/A Vacation If Pay Period is the same as Regular voucher no tax will calculate. Otherwise will use same calculation method as a regular voucher.
Employee Regular N/A Supplemental No tax will be calculated
Employee Special/Manual Yes Regular Lesser of (Weeks-Worked X 0.60) or (Taxable Gross X 0.05%)
Employee Special/Manual Yes Vacation If Pay Period is the same as Regular voucher no tax will calculate. Otherwise will use same calculation method as a regular voucher.
Employee Special/Manual Yes Supplemental No tax will be calculated
Employee Special/Manual No Regular Will loop thru vouchers with pay dates within pay period entered on batch. For any voucher regular voucher found that did not take maximum 0.60 will apply tax calculation to this voucher to make up for tax not withheld.
Employee Special/Manual No Vacation No tax will be calculated
Employee Special/Manual No Supplemental No tax will be calculated
Employee Manual Check N/A Regular Tax calculated same as regular check in a regular batch
Employee Manual Check N/A Supplemental No tax will be calculated
Employer Regular N/A Regular Tax calculated at a rate of 4.30 per month. Tax billed on the first voucher processed in the month.
Employer Regular N/A Vacation If Pay Period is the same as Regular voucher no tax will be calculated. Otherwise will use the same calculation method as a regular voucher
Employer Regular N/A Supplemental No tax will be calculated
Employer Special/Manual No Regular No tax will be calculated
Employer Special/Manual No Vacation No tax will be calculated
Employer Special/Manual No Supplemental No tax will be calculated
Employer Special/Manual Yes Regular Tax calculated at a rate of 4.30 per month. Tax billed on the first voucher processed in the month.
Employer Special/Manual Yes Vacation If Pay Period is the same as Regular voucher no tax will be calculated. Otherwise will use the same calculation method as a regular voucher
Employer Special/Manual Yes Supplemental No tax will be calculated
Employer Manual Check N/A Regular Tax calculated same as a Regular voucher in a Regular batch.
Employer Manual Check N/A Supplemental No tax will be calculated

Locating Employee Tax Withholding Forms

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/Taxes-Deductions/TS-Locating-Employee-Tax-Forms.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/Taxes-Deductions/TS-Locating-Employee-Tax-Forms.htm

Locating Employee Tax Withholding Forms

Sometimes it is necessary to review local, state, or federal tax election forms completed during a certain time frame. There can be various reasons for this, including:

  • periodic review

  • concerns or issues regarding a particular form, or

  • the form was filled out before supporting features were fully integrated into PrismHR.

For example, you might need to locate forms associated with:

  • Portland Metro (Oregon) SHS tax

  • Multnomah County (Oregon) Preschool For All tax

  • Colorado Annual Withholding Allowance elections, as specified on state form DR 0004

Document Management provides search and filter options for the documents stored in your system, including employee tax forms.

Procedure

Note: Note: Pending the release of performance enhancements in the near future, large searches may take some time to complete.

To find employee tax forms in Document Management:

  1. In the Client menu, under Client|Change, select Manage Documents.

  2. Select Advanced Search.

  3. In the Assigned To filter, select Employee.

  4. Select Companies, as appropriate, to refine your search.

  5. Under Document Categories, search for “Tax” and select any of the options as appropriate. For example, State Tax Form.

  6. You can further filter the results by date range, if necessary.

  7. Click Search.The filtered results populate.

  8. In large lists, you might need to refine your search with additional terms (such as the name or ID of the tax form). Use the Search all fields box to narrow your results with particular words or phrases.

Examples

Example 1: Results from initial search

Example 2: Filtered results after searching for a tax form

MasterTax RTS 42: Calculations and Workarounds

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/Taxes-Deductions/TS-MasterTax_2021.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/Taxes-Deductions/TS-MasterTax_2021.htm

MasterTax RTS 42: Calculations and Workarounds

Background

The American Rescue Plan Act took effect on April 1, 2021 and made multiple changes to earlier coronavirus-related legislation. One major change was the end of the employee-side Social Security tax credit for COVID-19 qualified leave wages, which PrismHR calculated and accrued under tax code 00‑87. The elimination of this tax credit entails major differences in tax reporting for the later quarters of 2021.

Please note that, in accordance with the federal guidance provided under the ARPA, the new version of the MasterTax RTS file supports:

  • Its own internal calculation logic for the value that PrismHR associates with tax code 00-87.

  • Pre-April 1 and post-March 31 credit divisions in the statistics of the 501 records for the following tax codes: 00-80, 00-81, and the health care credit codes FE0000-116H and FE0000-117H.

PrismHR uses its own logic to determine the value of 00-87 and to break up the credit reporting under the 501 statistics. In addition, we recognize that clients adopted varying approaches to ARPA compliance. This document provides some basic workaround recommendations, assuming certain system settings were configured in accordance with previous PrismHR guidance.

In September 2021, PrismHR released new RTS build settings to automate some of this calculation logic. See PrismHR Solutions for more information.

Guide to Acronyms and Tax Codes

Acronym or Tax Code Meaning
FFCRA Families First Coronavirus Response Act (2020)
ARPA American Rescue Plan Act (2021, often called “The ARP” in official documents)
00-12 Employee-side Social Security tax
00-16 Employer-side Social Security tax
00-80 Qualifying wage credits for COVID-19 FMLA leave under the FFCRA
00-81 Qualifying wage credits for COVID-19 Sick Leave under the FFCRA
00-84 Qualifying healthcare-related expenses associated with FFCRA payments
00-87 Social Security Tax Credits on qualified wages under the FFCRA
FE0000-116H MasterTax code for the portion of 00-84 associated with 00-80 wages
FE0000-117H MasterTax code for the portion of 00-84 associated with 00-81 wages

PrismHR Logic and Workarounds

To accommodate the split between pre-April and post-March COVID-19 credits, PrismHR implemented logic that divides entire vouchers (not their individual credits) into those two categories, based on whether the voucher Pay Period End Date falls before or after April 1.

However, we recognize that many customers are using their own configurations to comply with this credit reporting split, so this logic will not be a perfect fit for every use case.

We have identified two major alternative or workaround approaches:

  • Treat all COVID-19 credits on these vouchers as “post-March” credits. This simpler approach is popular with some customers.

  • Opt for a more granular COVID-19 credit breakdown. This requires additional manual calculation.

About the RTS Statistics

On version 42 of the MasterTax RTS file, in the 501 record for tax codes 00-80 and 00-81, there are three important statistic definitions.

Statistic Description Calculation Logic
1 Social Security taxable amount for the period after 3/31/21 All 00-80 and 00-81 credits associated with payroll vouchers that have Pay Period End Dates on or after 4/1/21.
2 Social Security taxable amount for the period before 4/1/21 The difference between the values stored under 00-12 and 00-16, up to the total amount of the applicable FFCRA credits.
5 All creditable wages under the tax code, for the period before 4/1/21 All 00-80 and 00-81 credits associated with payroll vouchers that have Pay Period End Dates on or before 3/31/21.

Note: Note: The FE0000-116H and -117H codes use their own “Statistic 5” value, discussed below.

The values you submit in these statistics will vary depending on your approach toward ARPA compliance, as described below.

Statistic 5 Under FE0000-116H and -117H

These credits break down the value stored in tax code 00-84. In the RTS file, their 501 records have a unique Statistic 5 value representing pre-April 1 health care tax credits. In other words, Statistic 5 displays a subset of the total value stored under 116H or 117H. The “excluded” amounts correspond to any 00-84 credits accrued on vouchers with pay period end dates that fall on or after April 1.

PrismHR calculates this value voucher-by-voucher, pulling healthcare credits from any vouchers that meet these criteria. It then divides this amount into sub-values for Sick Leave and FMLA.

If you want to treat all credits as accruing after April 1, you can set Statistic 5 to 0 for these credit records.

Note: Note: There are extra considerations for customers who want a more granular data breakdown. Because healthcare credit calculations are period-based, it is difficult to obtain a precise day-by-day analysis of credit accruals. Instead, we recommend apportioning employee 00-84 credits based on percentages of the whole wages paid under FMLA vs. Sick Leave pay codes, even if this is an approximation.

Understanding Previous System Setup Guidance

If you followed previous PrismHR setup guidance as specified in our Support Office Hours and the corresponding documentation, the statistic values on your RTS file should be accurate. If not, you may need to perform some manual calculations and adjustments to the file.

When this document refers to “previous guidance”, this means:

  • Removing the Social Security tax exemption from any COVID-19 sick leave/FMLA pay codes after April 1, or creating new pay codes without this tax exemption for the “post-March” payments.

  • Creating separate payrolls for COVID-19 sick leave/FMLA payments associated with pay periods before March 31.

  • (In some cases) Ensuring that no employee received COVID-19 sick or FMLA wages that exceeded their Social Security taxable wage limit prior to taking this COVID-19 leave. The workaround sections below will specify when this requirement must be satisfied.

How does this affect Form 941?

PrismHR is implementing the same logic across both the RTS file generation features and our Form 941 reports. Therefore, if you need to make adjustments to the Form 941 report output, you can use the exact same workarounds described here.

PrismHR Solutions

In September 2021, PrismHR released two new MasterTax RTS build settings to help automate some credit reporting calculations for 2021.

On the MasterTax RTS File Build form, you can select the following two options:

  • Use tax code 00-87 to determine SS credit within MasterTax?

  • Use COVID Credits as post March 31?

[Image: MasterTax RTS File Build form, with two fields highlighted: one is called “Use tax code 00-87 to determine SS credit with MasterTax” and the other is called “Use COVID Credits as post March 31”]

Use tax code 00-87 to determine SS credit within MasterTax?

If you select this option, the system performs the following calculation during the RTS build: (00-87 tax amount associated with line item) / (0.062).

After computing this value, the system uses this value to fill in Statistic 1 of the 501 record for tax code 00-81, up to the value reported under 00-81 on the 500 record.

If any amount remains after reaching the 00-81 reporting value, the system enters the remainder into Statistic 1 of the 501 record for tax code 00-80.

Use COVID Credits as post March 31?

Select this option to consider any COVID-19 credits accrued in Q2 2021 as “post-March 31” credits, which means they were accrued subject to the American Rescue Plan Act. These credits will be reported accordingly on the RTS file.

On the file itself, this means:

  • On 501 records for 00-80 and 00-81, the full value under these tax codes populates under statistic 1.

  • On FE0000-116H and FE0000-117H records, statistic 1 is set to 0.

Alternative Approaches

This section documents workarounds for this issue that we recommended before we released the solutions outlined above. They are included here for your reference.

1. Associating all Q2 Leave with Dates After March 31

Note: Note: If you calculated COVID-19-related healthcare credits and decide to use this approach, you must zero out Statistic 5 on the MasterTax records for those credits (FE0000-116H and FE0000-117H).

If no employee who took COVID-19 leave exceeded their Social Security taxable wage limit prior to, or while taking, that leave:

  1. In the RTS file, set Statistic 1 equal to the full value of 00-80 or 00-81 as appropriate.

  2. Set Statistic 2 equal to 0.

  3. Set Statistic 5 equal to 0.

  4. Repeat for all applicable clients or employers.

The example below illustrates the 500 and 501 records associated with tax code 00-81. The statistics on the right are separated by plus signs. Note that Statistic 1 is the same for both records, while every other statistic in the 501 record is zeroed out.

[Image: Detail view of MasterTax RTS file, focused on 500 and 501 records for tax code 00-81.]

If you think some employees exceeded their Social Security wage limit, but you still used our recommended setup for calculating 00-87 accurately:

  1. See the section below on using 00-87 to calculate Statistic 1.

  2. Set Statistic 2 equal to 0.

  3. Set Statistic 5 equal to 0.

  4. Repeat for all applicable clients or employers.

2. Using 00-87 Credits to Calculate Statistic 1

Note: Note: Beginning in PrismHR 1.24.0, the system features an option to perform this process automatically. You will receive a prompt during RTS file generation: select Yes to automatically allocate values to Statistic 1 of the 501 records using the procedure below.

MasterTax performs its own calculation to determine the value of “00-87”, based on data from tax codes 00-80 and 00-81. Therefore, in RTS version 42, PrismHR no longer passes 00-87 data into MasterTax. Instead, you have the option to run this process automatically or perform the manual calculations and adjust the file as needed.

You can use this approach even if you have employees who exceeded their Social Security taxable wage limit for the quarter.

To calculate Statistic 1:

  1. Take the 00-87 tax amount for the quarter and divide it by 0.062. This is the exact inverse of what MasterTax does in their own program. This ensures proper conversion between our calculated values and the calculation logic implemented by MasterTax.

  2. Enter the resulting value in Statistic 1 of the 501 record for 00-81 or 00-80, as appropriate.

  3. Repeat this process for any other clients or employers, as needed.

To calculate Statistic 2:

If none of your employees went over their Social Security tax limit, and your pay codes were configured appropriately to exempt Social Security tax only on pre-ARPA COVID leave, then Statistic 2 most likely calculated correctly. For this calculation, PrismHR uses the difference between employer-side and employee-side Social Security tax for the quarter. It enters that value in Statistic 2, capped by the total amount of FFCRA credits associated with that record.

3. Handling Issues with Statistic 2

FFCRA qualified wages are only exempt from Social Security tax prior to the start of the ARPA (before April 1, 2021). After that date, this type of payment is no longer exempt.

As in previous quarters, PrismHR relies on the presence of employer-level Social Security tax exemptions as an indicator of major tax-related changes in a particular quarter. If you left the tax exemption enabled on your FFCRA pay codes, the RTS file statistics will reflect those wages as accruing before April 1 even if this is not the case. The system cannot tell that these are post-April 1 credits because they still have the employer-level tax exemption.

You must use tax adjustments to correct this data issue. You have the option of using either major workaround strategy here:

  • Counting all credits as accruing after April 1, or

  • Taking a more detailed approach to your credit calculations

To resolve this issue using the “post-April 1” workaround strategy:

  1. First, ensure that you calculated Statistic 1 correctly and zeroed out statistic 2.

  2. Create a tax adjustment to apply the 00-87 credit to the employee record.

  3. Set the credit value equal to the difference between the 00-16 and 00-12 accruals for the employee (this should be a negative value).

  4. Repeat for all employees who took COVID-19 leave, regardless of whether they exceeded their Social Security tax limit.

To resolve this issue using a more granular approach:

  • You can try using the COVID-19 Recalculation Utility to recalculate your Social Security tax credits for April 1 and beyond. Please note that this strategy assumes you still used distinct pay codes for FFCRA and ARPA wages, even though you did not remove the tax exemption on the latter.

  • You can perform manual calculations to adjust this data. This option will be highly case-specific. Please contact customer support if you need to take this approach and have further questions.

4. Taking a More Granular Approach Toward Statistic 5 of 00-80 and 00-81

If you need an even more granular approach than those outlined above (for example, you want to define your credit split based on charge dates), your system-generated Statistic 5 value may be underestimated.

To calculate Statistic 5 (sum of all pre-April creditable COVID-19 wages), PrismHR currently uses the voucher-level pay period end date break described previously. If you want to count all your credits as accrued after March 31, you must set this statistic to 0 as described previously.

Workarounds

You may need to take some manual steps to determine your data in this case.

Scenario — Workaround

No employees who took COVID-19 leave went over their Social Security taxable wage limit AND you correctly calculated Statistic 2. — Set Statistic 5 equal to Statistic 2.

Employees did go over their Social Security limit AND your COVID-19 pay code data aligns perfectly with the credit values for the quarter. In other words, the credits were not reduced by any tax sheltering or similar features. — You might be able to use pay code data, broken out by charge date.

You need to take an even more granular approach (COVID-19 qualified wages are further modified by one or more factors). — In this case, manual calculation is probably necessary. > Note: Note: If this describes your situation, make sure you also make the corresponding modifications to Statistic 5 for your healthcare credits (FE000-116H and -117H).

If you need further guidance on this process, please feel free to contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

Updating Employee Fields from an Import File

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/TS-Expanded_EE_Import_2020.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/TS-Expanded_EE_Import_2020.htm

Updating Employee Fields from an Import File

You can use the Expanded Employee Import file to bulk load employees into PrismHR.

When you use the Expanded Employee Import method to import employee information, enter the information in the Template_for_Expanded_Employee_Import_TXT.xls file. The contents of this file are listed in the following table. The file template is also available on the Customer Resource Center.

Important Notes About the 2020 Form W-4

Beginning December 2019, this import supports the new 2020 Form W-4 filing statuses and fields. Please note the following updates:

  • In column AV, you can enter the new filing statuses (SS, MJ, and H).

  • If your state is using the new Form W-4 (Colorado, Delaware, Nebraska, New Mexico, North Dakota, South Carolina, and Utah), you must enter the new filing statuses in this field to avoid calculation errors.

  • Federal, work state, and home state values are now supported for the new Form W-4 fields, beginning in column GX. Note that there are no validations on these fields.

  • You must enter dollar amounts in the Other Income, Dependents, and Deductions fields. See the Form W-4 instructions for more information.

Information about this import file

  • File Type: ASCII, Tab Delimited

  • File Use: Stores employee data imported from foreign system

  • Key Format: Sequential Integer

Other Notes

There are a few more things to note for certain import fields, marked with asterisks (*) in the Reqd/Optl column of the table below:

  • **One asterisk (*): This field is not required if ONHRP.ONBOARDED (Field 161) is set to Y**.

  • Two asterisks (): This field is only optional if ONHRP.ONBOARDED (Field 161) is set to Y**. Otherwise, it is required.

  • Three asterisks (*): This field is required if ONHRP.ONBOARDED (Field 161) is set to Y**. Otherwise, it is optional.

Setting Up Global PTO Plans

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/TS-Global_PTO.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/TS-Global_PTO.htm

Setting Up Global PTO Plans

You can use PrismHR to set up PTO plans on the system level and automatically assign those plans to employees based on work location.

Background

As described in the Time Off User Guide (previously titled PTO User Guide), the PrismHR PTO features originally applied only to clients. This presented difficulties for organizations that needed to track paid time off for employees who regularly change location — such as those in the staffing industry.

In response to this challenge, PrismHR introduced support for Global PTO plans that can track the accrual of employee paid time based on the employee’s worksite location.

These new features enable you to set up discrete Global PTO plans and assign those to a state or even a specific GeoCode range. This setup structure provides significant flexibility in defining how and when employee paid time off accrues.

Activating Global PTO Features

Note: Note: Refresh your browser to see the changes from this feature code.

If you want to enable the new global PTO features, you must first apply the associated feature code.

To activate global PTO features:

  1. Click Back Office and select System|Change > System Parameters.

  2. In the upper right corner of the form, in the Custom Feature Codes table, create a new row.

  3. Enter GLOBAL.PTO in this new row (be sure to include the period between GLOBAL and PTO).

  4. Click Save.

  5. Refresh your browser and then log in again, if necessary. The Global PTO forms and fields are now accessible.

[Image: Custom Feature Codes grid with GLOBAL.PTO entered. No description displays for the feature code.]

About the Features

With these features enabled:

  • You can establish system-level PTO codes and plans, and then assign those at the state and local levels using new fields on the State Rules form.

  • Next time you run a payroll, PrismHR will detect any employees who work within the specified state and GeoCode ranges. The system will automatically perform the PTO accrual calculations associated with the specific PTO plans assigned to those locations. Those calculated accruals will be stored in a separate database record created for this purpose.

  • To fine-tune the global PTO auto-enrollment and hour accrual, you can use client-level opt-in settings and employee-level opt-out settings (described below).

  • If you want to review PTO accruals to ensure that they are set up accurately, you can use a new view option on the Employee PTO Register Inquiry form.

  • New in June 2022: For staffing customers, we now support global PTO plans for federal contractors. The FG state code provides a grid for Global PTO plans but not GeoCodes, as they are not necessary. If an employee PLACEMENT record indicates that they are a federal contractor, the auto-enrollment and accrual logic will assign them a PTO plan associated with FG.

  • New in June 2022: The PTO “auto-enroll and accrue” logic also takes effect whenever you perform a global PTO data import using the PTO Register Import form.

  • New in June 2022: A new payroll report is available. This report displays a list of employees who were auto-enrolled in global PTO plans within the current payroll batch. You can run this report during payroll processing or separately. See Reviewing Global PTO Auto-Enrollments for a Payroll Batch.

  • New in June 2022: Several options are now available for printing Global PTO register data on employee checks. Instead of a binary yes/no option, you can select Always, Never (default), or If Used/Accrued Only for any global PTO register.

  • New in March 2024: Leave requests made in the Employee Portal that the system pulls into timesheets using the red dot can be accepted there.

  • New in September 2024: Previously, all Global PTO functionality for the following forms were hidden unless the custom feature code GLOBAL.PTO was enabled. All Global PTO functionality are now available on the following forms without using the custom feature code GLOBAL.PTO.

  • System / Change / Global PTO Benefit Plans

  • System / Change / Global PTO Register Types

  • PTO Reporting / Reporting / Global PTO Auto Enrollment Report

  • System / Change / State Rules (Global PTO Plans)

  • Client / Change / Client Other (Enable Global PTO)

  • PTO Plan Setup and Enrollment / Plan Setup / PTO Absence Codes (Global PTO Checkbox)

  • New in March 2026: The legacy PTO terminology is being updated to match the terminology used in the Time Off Setup Workflow feature introduced in an earlier release. Note that these updates will be done over several releases. (See the “Understanding Time Off Setup Workflow Process Terminology” article on the CRC, which provides a description of these updates.)

Note: The GLOBAL.PTO custom feature code is still required to calculate Global PTO accruals. This requirement will be removed in an upcoming release.

Creating Global PTO Register Types

Use the Global PTO Register Types form to set up PTO register types for plans that are not client-specific. For more detailed documentation, see Setting Up Global PTO Register Types.

PTO registers track the usable hours of PTO that employees accrue over the course of the year. The events the trigger PTO accrual, and the strict definition of “year”, are defined on this form.

After setting up these rules, you can assign these codes using a specialized Register Types table on the PTO Absence Codes form. See Reviewing Accrual Histories.

To set up a global PTO register type:

  1. Go to Back Office > System|Change > Global PTO Register Types.

  2. Enter or select a Global PTO Type Code.

  3. Enter a Description of the global PTO register type.

  4. Select the Year End Basis – the means by which the system measures the end of a year (Anniversary or Fiscal).

If you selected Fiscal, specify a Fiscal End Month and Day.

  1. In the Trigger Method field, select Pay Period Accrual.

  2. Set the other optional parameters, as needed. You can find descriptions of these fields in the online help for either the Global PTO Register Types form or the client-level PTO Register Types form.

Both forms use the same logic for these fields. See Reviewing Accrual Histories.

  1. Click Save.

[Image: Global PTO Register types form]

Creating Global Absence Codes

In PrismHR, client-defined PTO absence codes track the accumulation of PTO hours defined under a particular category.

PTO absence codes are associated with both pay codes (Back Office > System > Change > Pay Codes) and PTO register types (Client > Change > PTO Register Types).

To create a global PTO absence code:

  1. Go to Client > Change > PTO Absence Codes.

  2. Enter an Absence Code identifier and a Reason.

  3. Enable the Updates PTO Register option.

  4. Select Global PTO. The Register Types table on the right-hand side of the form is replaced by a Global Register Types table.

  5. Assign the appropriate Pay Codes and Global Register Types in those tables.

  6. Fill out the Leave Request Processing section as desired.

For details about these fields, see Creating Time Off Absence Codes

  1. Click Save.

[Image: PTO Absence Codes form, with “Global PTO” option enabled and highlighted. The Global Register Types grid is also highlighted.]

Creating Global PTO Benefit Plans

Use the Global PTO Benefit Plans form to set up and maintain the hour carryover and accrual rules related to system-level PTO plans. For more details, see Maintaining Global PTO Benefit Plans.

Note: Note: In contrast to the client-level PTO Benefit Plans form, there is no Calculation Basis option. Global PTO plans are designed to automatically use the “Flat Amount By Hours Worked” calculation basis.

To configure a global PTO benefit plan:

  1. Go to Back Office > System|Change > Global PTO Benefit Plans.

  2. Enter or select a Global PTO Benefit Plan ID. Use the Copy from another plan option if you want to use an existing plan as your template.

  3. Enter a brief Plan Description.

  4. Specify the Global PTO Register Type, configured above, that you will use to track accumulations and PTO usage under this benefit plan.

  5. Fill out the remaining fields as needed. For details about these fields, review the online help documentation for Maintaining Global PTO Benefit Plans.

  6. Click Save.

Opt-ins and Opt-outs

Opting Clients into Auto-enrollment and Accrual

You can use a setting on the Client Details form to enable global PTO plans for that client. On the Client Details > Other tab, select Global PTO Plan Auto Enroll & Accrue.

[Image: Client Details Other tab option called Global PTO Plan Auto Enroll and Accrue]

Opting Employees out of Auto-enrollment and Accrual

You can use a setting on the Employee Details form to opt employees out of global PTO plan calculations. On the Employee Details > Other tab, select Disable PTO Auto Enroll & Accrue.

[Image: Employee Details Other Tab option, called Disable PTO Auto Enroll and Accrue]

Assigning PTO Plans on the State and Local Level

Note: Note: You cannot set up GeoCode ranges for plans assigned to the FG state code (Federal Government — use this code for the PTO plans of employees who are federal contractors).

On the System > Change > State Rules form, you can use the Global PTO panel to specify the PTO plans to offer on both the state and local level.

  • In the State Plan Id panel, enter the IDs of any global PTO plans you want to offer to employees who work in that state.

  • Use the other panel to offer local PTO plans to employees who work within certain GeoCode ranges. You can use this to refine PTO accrual down to worksite locations within a specific locality.

Enter the Starting GeoCode and Ending GeoCode to specify the range. Then, enter a Local Plan Id that you want to offer within that range.

Note: Note: Currently, local PTO benefit plans accrue hours in addition to those of the state-level PTO plans.

[Image: Global PTO panel on the State Rules form, showing the State Plan ID grid and the Geocode Range/Local Plan ID grid.]

Deduction Logic

PrismHR uses the following process to select PTO registers for time off deductions. Note that a local PTO plan is any plan assigned to a GeoCode range on the State Rules form. The specific GeoCode is determined by the worksite location associated with the PTO hour payout.

[Image: Example of using Global PTO panel on the State Rules form. State Plan ID grid has one entry, GLOBAL.PTO. Values are provided for Starting Geocode and Ending Geocode, and both are labeled to indicate they are validated against worksite location. The Local Plan ID column is set to “GLOBCA”]

The basic PTO deduction logic takes this form:

  1. Deduct from the local PTO plan that matches the worksite location GeoCode on State Rules (if one exists). Repeat until hours run out in the register. If this occurs, move on to the next step.

  2. Continue deducting from any other local PTO plans that match the GeoCode and are associated with the employee. If none meet these criteria, proceed to the next step.

  3. Look for state-level global PTO registers (no GeoCode range) assigned on the State Rules form. Deduct from any qualifying state PTO plans in order. If PTO hours are still required after all these deductions, the system generates a payroll failure or warning depending on the following settings:

  • Max Usage Limit Per Plan Year (Global PTO Benefit Plans form)

  • Stop Init If Exceed Limit (Global PTO Benefit Plans form)

  • Display a Warning If Employee Uses More Hours Than Available (Global PTO Register Types form)

[Image: Global PTO Benefit Plans form, with two fields highlighted: Max Usage Limit Per Plan Year and Stop Init If Exceed Limit]

[Image: Global PTO Register Types form with one field highlighted: Display a Warning if Employee Uses More Hours Than Available]

Reviewing Accrual Histories

There is a new Report View option on the HR > View > Employee PTO Register Inquiry form, called Global PTO. Select this option to review the Charge Date, Hours Worked, and Pay Rate associated with an employee’s global PTO accruals, along with their associated Pay Codes and Project assignments.

[Image: Employee PTO Register Inquiry form, with Report View option set to “Global PTO”. Displays the Charge Date, PTO Pay Code, Hours Worked, Pay Rate, and Project for two PTO entries.]

Reviewing Global PTO Auto-Enrollments for a Payroll Batch

You can use a new on-demand payroll batch report to review a list of employees who were auto-enrolled in global PTO plans during batch processing. For details, see Running the Global PTO Auto Enrollment Report.

To run the Employee PTO Auto Enrollment Report:

  1. Click Work Centers and then select Work Centers|PTO > Reporting > Global PTO Auto Enrollment Report. Otherwise, select this report during payroll batch processing.

  2. Enter the batch number in the Batch ID field.

  3. Click Run and review the report output.

Configuring “Print On Check” Functionality

You can adjust which global PTO register hours display on employee checks, based on register activity.

To update PTO register check printing settings:

  1. Click Back Office and then select System|Change > Global PTO Register Types.

  2. Locate the Print On Check option.

  3. Select one of the following. Note that –Select– is equivalent to “Never”.

  • Always – always print employee global PTO register data on the check, no matter what

  • Never – default option. Never print data from this register on the check.

  • If Used/Accrued Only – only print on check if the employee used or accrued hours under this register within the payroll batch

  1. Click Save.

========================================================================

Recalculating the SUTA Taxable Wage Base

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/CMP/TS-Recalc_SUTA_Taxable_WageBase_Prism.htm Path: Task Sheets/CMP/TS-Recalc_SUTA_Taxable_WageBase_Prism.htm

Recalculating the SUTA Taxable Wage Base

Important: Make sure the SUTA Rates for the appropriate state have been updated before running this utility. For details, refer to the Payroll Setup Guide.

This task sheet describes how to recalculate data for a state unemployment tax (SUTA) Taxable Wage base. You may need to do this for several reasons:

  • A state changed its SUI (state unemployment insurance) wage base

  • A state changed its SUTA rate

  • SUTA calculation errors occurred

Recent Changes

States will often lower the state unemployment insurance (SUI) rate or wage base after a quarter has already begun. The following states and territories recently implemented this type of change:

  • Delaware

  • West Virginia (see this article for details)

  • U.S. Virgin Islands

Note: Note: When recalculating SUTA data, you can choose from two options: create adjustment vouchers for the recalculated amounts, or update the existing amounts directly. You do not need to rebuild tax registers if you choose to create adjustment vouchers.

Reviewing the Tax Register Detail Report

We recommend reviewing the Tax Register Detail Report before making any SUTA changes, so you can see or record the initial SUTA tax rate information.

[Image: screenshot]

After recalculating the SUTA rates and limits, re-run this report and review it again to ensure the SUTA recalculation worked as expected.

To run the Tax Register Detail Report:

  1. In the Reports menu, select Payroll|Report > Tax Register Report Entry to access the report setup form.

  2. For Report Type, select Employer Detail.

  3. Enter the Tax Year and Quarter as appropriate.

  4. Limit the report to specific Employers and Tax Authorities, if appropriate.

  5. In the Tax ID grid, enter 24 (core SUTA tax code) or the tax code for the appropriate SUTA-related tax (this also includes SUTA “minor” taxes or non-SUTA taxes that follow the same calculation guidelines).

  6. Click Build Data. A Job Control form displays the progress of the data build. Click Close when the build finishes.

  7. If the build was successful, a new Run Report button displays. Click this button to download the report.

  8. Review the report for SUTA details and proceed to the next section.

Example Report Output

[Image: screenshot]

Recalculating SUTA Taxes

Important: We strongly recommend running this process on a quiet system (such as after work hours), to prevent any changes to the data while it is rebuilt.

In PrismHR, you can use one form to recalculate SUTA Taxes for states that require employer-level SUTA reporting or client-level SUTA reporting.

[Image: screenshot]

To recalculate SUTA for an employer reporting state:

  1. From the Payroll menu, select Payroll|Action > Recalculate Taxes on Vouchers.

  2. For Tax Type, select Miscellaneous.

  3. Specify whether the SUTA Reporting Entity is the Employer or the Client.

  4. Enter the Employer ID or Client ID of the subject of the SUTA recalculation.

  5. Select the Tax Code for the appropriate state.

  6. Enter the Start Date and End Date for the recalculation.

  7. Enter the Taxable Wage Limit for the selected state and, optionally, the Tax Rate in percent form (without the percent sign).

  8. For Update Tax, select Yes.

  9. Specify whether you want the utility to Create new adjustment vouchers or Update existing vouchers. Note that rebuilding tax registers is not required if you generate adjustment vouchers.

  10. If you need to create new adjustment vouchers:

  • In the Generate Separate Adjustments for Each Quarter field, select Yes or No.

  • If you answered No above, enter the specific Adjustment Date.

  • Specify whether you want to Suppress Billing for these adjustments. If you select No, PrismHR generates an invoice for each adjustment voucher that it creates. However, we recommend selecting Yes and creating a manual Client Bill Pending item or a miscellaneous invoice to represent the difference, instead.

  1. Click Preview Changes to preview the effects of your calculation setup. You can also use the Download Report option to get the results in a separate file.

  2. When you are ready to recalculate, click Update Tax Values.

  3. Proceed to the next section.

Rebuilding Tax Registers for the Year

You can rebuild tax registers at both the client- and system-level. Both of these builds can be performed from the Register Rebuilds form in PrismHR.

Note: Note: If your SUTA changes only affected a client or a subset of clients, you do not need to perform a system-level rebuild. You can also skip this step if you selected Create new adjustment vouchers in the previous procedure.

If any payrolls are in process during a rebuild, you must reinitialize them before they can be posted.

To rebuild client or system tax registers:

Note: Note: We strongly recommend backing up your system data before rebuilding tax registers.

  1. Click Back Office and select Operations|Action > Register Rebuilds.

  2. For the Register Type, select either Tax - Company Registers or Tax - Rebuild All Tax Registers for a Year, as appropriate.

  3. Enter the tax rebuild Year.

  4. Click Rebuild.

  5. Run the Tax Register Detail report again to verify the SUTA changes. See Reviewing the Tax Register Detail Report.

  6. If you want to send billing adjustments to the client, use the information in the second report to set up manual billing adjustments. See Creating Client Bill Pending Items for more information on making adjustments.

  7. Once you have verified the information in the SUTA Tax Change Report, re-initialize all payrolls that are currently in process.

  8. Proceed to the next section.

Creating a New MasterTax RTS File

Customers who use MasterTax™ must also create a new MasterTax Reconciliation Tax Summary Import (RTS) file, in order to import the recalculated quarterly tax data into MasterTax

Note: Note: Certain features, like the client and employer filters, can only be used for newer versions of the RTS file. To see the version your system uses, expand the Configuration Information panel at the bottom of the form.

To create a new MasterTax RTS file:

  1. From the Work Centers menu, select Work Centers|Tax > MasterTax Processing. Then, under File Exports, select MasterTax RTS File Build.

  2. Fill in the Tax Year and Tax Quarter fields with the appropriate values.

  3. If you need to limit the RTS build to a specific employer or client, enter its ID in the Limit to Emloyer ID and Limit to Client fields. Note that the Limit to Client field can only be used for clients that are located in a SUTA reporting state.

  4. Indicate whether you want to exclude Pennsylvania and Ohio locals when no taxes are withheld.

  5. Do not select the Build W2 Records? option.

  6. Set the other RTS parameters as appropriate. For descriptions of these fields, see the online help for the MasterTax RTS File Build form.

  7. Click Generate File.

Creating Client Bill Pending Items

Changes to SUTA might require manual entry of billing adjustments. Use this procedure to create those billing line items.

To set up new client bill pending items:

  1. In the Client menu, select Client|Action > Bill Pending.

  2. Click New Entry. The Bill Pending Details form displays.

  3. Complete the following fields as needed:

Field — Description

Bill Date — Enter or select the Bill Date on which billing will occur.

Event — Enter or select the billing Event code. For example, you may have an existing code for SUTA adjustments.

Billing Units — Enter the Billing Units.

Bill Rate — Enter the Bill Rate for each billing unit.

Bill Amount — The Bill Amount field is equal to Billing Units x Bill Rate. If you change the Bill Amount, the system recalculates the Bill Rate to make the numbers work.

Employee — Enter or select the ID of the Employee. The employee’s name displays, as well as any Location, Division, Department, Shift, or Project assignments.

Pay Group — Enter or select the Pay Group code. If you specify a pay group, you must enter a Location so the system can calculate any taxes. Note: You can enter an employee or a pay group (or neither), but you cannot have both in the same line item entry.

Payroll Type — Select the payroll batch type. For example, you can select the Regularly Scheduled payroll type if you only want to bill on a regularly scheduled payroll. (Note: The Select option (default) assumes all payroll types.)

  1. Complete the remaining fields as needed.

  2. Click Save. This bill pending item will be present in the next initialized invoice.

  3. Repeat for all necessary billing adjustments.

Employee Portal On-Premise Setup

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/EP/Employee_Portal_%20On-Premise_Setup.htm Path: Task Sheets/EP/Employee_Portal_ On-Premise_Setup.htm

Employee Portal On-Premise Setup

This task sheet explains how to set up the on-premise environment for EP.

Note: Note: These steps require basic knowledge of VIM as a text editor in Linux. In the instructions that follow, the referenced text editor is VIM.

Download the Open Virtual Appliance

The open virtual appliance (OVA) will allow customers to create an on-premise instance of the Employee Portal (EP). To download the open virtual appliance, ask your contact at PrismHR for a link.

Configure OVA for First Use

To configure the OVA correctly, you must provide a valid IP address.

  1. Import the OVA file into your virtual environment.

  2. After the OVA is successfully imported to your virtual environment, power on the appliance.

  3. Going forward we will assume that the OVA is placed in the correct network segment and that an IP address has been reserved.

  4. Log in to the server as user phradmin with the password that was provided to you in a separate secure message.

Note: Note: It is strongly recommended that you change both the root user and phradmin password (which are set to be the same) in the OVA.

Step 1—Change the Hostname

Change the hostname of your server to fit the naming convention that you prefer and assign the IP address that was previously reserved by your IT/Operations department.

  1. Using the VIM editor, perform the following steps to change the hostname of your server:

[phradmin@dockerce ~]$ sudo su -

Last login: EDT 2018 on pts/0

[root@dockerce ~]# vim /etc/hostname

  1. Modify the existing name from dockerce to whatever naming convention works in your environment.

  2. Save the file.

  3. Next, run the following command and modify the HOSTNAME entry to match the entry in the previous step.

[root@dockerce ~]# vim /etc/sysconfig/network

  1. For the HOSTNAME change to take effect immediately, perform a quick reboot of the server with the following command:

[root@dockerce ~]# reboot

Step 2—Assign a Static IP Address

The virtual appliance is configured to use DHCP by default, but this is not recommended for long-term usage. If you do not wish to apply a static IP address, please skip this section and proceed to the next configuration item.

Note: Note: To modify the DHCP address and add a permanent static IP, use the command in the next section to configure your server with the unique IP address that was provided by your IT/Operations department.

The following items are used for static IP configuration:

IP Address

SUBNET MASK

GATEWAY

DNS1

DNS2 (optional)

Use the following command to modify your configuration to support a static IP configuration:

[root@dockerce ~]# vim /etc/sysconfig/network-scripts/ifcfg-ens33

  1. The file will contain the following entries. Using VIM, make changes to the locations marked with XXX.

TYPE=Ethernet

PROXY_METHOD=none

BROWSER_ONLY=no

BOOTPROTO=dhcp -> change dhcp to none

DEFROUTE=yes

IPV4_FAILURE_FATAL=no

IPV6INIT=yes

IPV6_AUTOCONF=yes

IPV6_DEFROUTE=yes

IPV6_FAILURE_FATAL=no

IPV6_ADDR_GEN_MODE=stable-privacy

NAME=ens33

UUID=f030becd-7cbe-45fb-b69d-6831270a2a08

DEVICE=ens33

ONBOOT=yes

#GATEWAY=“XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX” -> Remove the ‘#’ and change to Gateway IP provided by your Network Team

#NETMASK=“XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX” -> Remove the ‘#’ and change to Subnet Mask IP provided by your Network Team

#IPADDR=“XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX” -> Remove the ‘#’ and change to Static IP provided by your Network Team

#DNS1=“XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX” -> Remove the ‘#’ and change to DNS Primary IP provided by your Network Team

#DNS2=“XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX” -> Remove the ‘#’ and change to DNS Secondary IP provided by your Network Team. (If secondary DNS is not required, make no modifications to this line)

  1. Once modified and saved, restart your network to activate the new IPs with the following command:

[root@dockerce ~]# systemctl restart network

Config File Modifications

There are a number of configuration files that EP requires to operate. These files need to be modified to match your company’s unique environment. The following sections will advise on how to tailor the configuration to work with your specific network and domain requirements.

Step 1—Configure the IP Address

If you have chosen to utilize a DHCP address, execute the following command to obtain the IP address of your appliance. You will need this IP address for the config files.

[root@dockerce ~]# ifconfig ens33

Note: Note: Because the IP address is explicitly defined in the configurations that you will modify below, using a DHCP configuration is not advised. It is strongly advised to set a static IP address on the appliance level or create a DHCP reservation on your DHCP server for this appliance.

Because your environment has a specific IP address scheme, the IP address assigned to your new virtual appliance (whether DHCP or STATIC), must be specified in the following file.

[root@dockerce ~]# vim /prismhr/configs/docker-compose.yml

  1. Modify lines 199, 205, 247, 254 in the /prismhr/configs/docker-compose.yml file.

  2. There will be entries in the following format that must be changed. To quickly jump to a specific line number using VIM, use the ‘:’ in edit mode. For example, to jump to line 199, type :199, then press the ENTER/RETURN key.

o: addr=XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX,rw -> Replace the XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX on line # with your assigned IP address.

Step 2—Enable or Disable SSL

By default, the OVA enables SSL for your instance of on-premise Employee Portal. This requires you to provide a valid SSL Certificate and corresponding key. If you have a valid SSL Certificate and wish to apply it to your Employee Portal, follow these steps. Otherwise, proceed to the next section, Disable SSL (If Required).

Note: Note: The following notation is important.

Each configuration file that needs to be modified will have the following notation:

  • YOURDOMAIN.com

  • YOURDOMAIN_cert.pem

  • YOURDOMAIN_key.pem

The SSL certificate configuration requires the following:

  • Valid certificate

  • Valid key

For example, if your domain name is ACME.com, the required files will be ACME_com_cert.pem and ACME_com_key.pem.

  1. Copy the two pem files to the /prismhr/containerdata/nginx/certs folder.

There are two configuration files that require you to modify the names of your certs:

  • /prismhr/configs/nginx/ess2.conf

  • /prismhr/configs/nginx/ess-api.conf

We will use the ess2.conf file as an example of this one-time configuration.

server{

server_name ~.*onprem-epct.YOURDOMAIN.com; -> REPLACE YOURDOMAIN with your company’s domain name

sub_filter_once on;

sub_filter ‘ESS_API_PATH’ https://ess-api.YOURDOMAIN.com; -> REPLACE YOURDOMAIN with your company’s domain name

sub_filter_types application/javascript;

#listen 0.0.0.0:80;

#Enable for SSL

listen 0.0.0.0:443;

ssl on;

ssl_certificate /etc/nginx/certs/YOURDOMAIN_cert.pem; -> REPLACE YOURDOMAIN with your company’s domain name

ssl_certificate_key /etc/nginx/certs/YOURDOMAIN_key.pem; -> REPLACE YOURDOMAIN with your company’s domain name

add_header Strict-Transport-Security “max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains” always;

location / {

proxy_pass http://mss:8000;

}

}

server{

server_name ~.*onprem-ep.YOURDOMAIN.com; -> REPLACE YOURDOMAIN with your certificate domain name

sub_filter_once on;

sub_filter ‘ESS_API_PATH’ https://ess-api.YOURDOMAIN.com; -> REPLACE YOURDOMAIN with your certificate domain name

sub_filter_types application/javascript;

#listen 0.0.0.0:80;

#Enable for SSL

listen 0.0.0.0:443;

ssl on;

ssl_certificate /etc/nginx/certs/YOURDOMAIN_cert.pem; -> REPLACE YOURDOMAIN with your pem file name

ssl_certificate_key /etc/nginx/certs/YOURDOMAIN_key.pem; -> REPLACE YOURDOMAIN with your key file name

add_header Strict-Transport-Security “max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains” always;

location / {

proxy_pass http://ess2:8000;

}

}

  1. Perform the domain insertion for both files listed at the start of this section, replacing YOURDOMAIN with the exact name on your cert file.

  2. Copy both conf files to the /prismhr/containerdata/nginx directory.

[root@dockerce prismhr]# cp /prismhr/configs/nginx/*

/prismhr/containerdata/nginx/

Disable SSL (If Required)

There may be instances where SSL is not required. To disable the default SSL setting, follow these steps:

The following files will need to be modified to disable SSL.

  • /prismhr/configs/nginx/ess2.conf

  • /prismhr/configs/nginx/ess-api.conf

  • /prismhr/configs/docker-compose.yml

/prismhr/configs/docker-compose.yml file

  1. Modify the /prismhr/configs/docker-compose.yml file to DISABLE SSL.

[root@dockerce ~]# vim /prismhr/configs/docker-compose.yml

version: ‘3.3’

services:

proxy:

image: docker-dtr.prismhr.com/prismhr/nginx:1.13

ports:

#- “8010:80” -> REMOVE the #

#Enable for SSL

  • “443:443” -> ADD a # to the start of the line

read_only: true

volumes:

  • proxy_cache:/var/cache/nginx

  • proxy_run:/var/run/nginx

  • proxy_tmp:/tmp

  • proxy_conf:/etc/nginx/conf.d

#Enable for SSL

  • proxy_certs:/etc/nginx/certs -> ADD a # to the start of the line

…Scroll down to the next block of code that contains “Enable for SSL”

#Enable for SSL

proxy_certs: -> ADD a # to the start of the line

driver: local -> ADD a # to the start of the line

driver_opts: -> ADD a # to the start of the line

type: “nfs” -> ADD a # to the start of the line

o: addr=XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX,rw -> ADD a # to the start of the line

device: “:/prismhr/containerdata/nginx/certs” -> ADD a # to the start of the line

  1. Save the file.

/prismhr/configs/nginx/ess2.conf file

  1. Edit the file: /prismhr/configs/nginx/ess2.conf

[root@dockerce ~]# vim /prismhr/configs/nginx/ess2.conf

  1. Find each block of code that contains “#Enable for SSL”

server{

server_name ~.*onprem-epct.YOURDOMAIN.com;

sub_filter_once on;

sub_filter ‘ESS_API_PATH’ https://ess-api.YOURDOMAIN.com;

sub_filter_types application/javascript;

#listen 0.0.0.0:80; -> REMOVE THE #

#Enable for SSL

listen 0.0.0.0:443; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

ssl on; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

ssl_certificate /etc/nginx/certs/YOURDOMAIN_cert.pem; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

ssl_certificate_key /etc/nginx/certs/YOURDOMAIN_key.pem; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

add_header Strict-Transport-Security “max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains” always -> ADD a # to the start of the line

proxy_pass http://mss:8000;

}

}

server{

server_name ~.*onprem-ep.YOURDOMAIN.com;

sub_filter_once on;

sub_filter ‘ESS_API_PATH’ https://ess-api.YOURDOMAIN.com;

sub_filter_types application/javascript;

#listen 0.0.0.0:80; -> REMOVE THE #

#Enable for SSL

listen 0.0.0.0:443;-> ADD a # to the start of the line

ssl on;-> ADD a # to the start of the line

ssl_certificate /etc/nginx/certs/YOURDOMAIN_cert.pem; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

ssl_certificate_key /etc/nginx/certs/YOURDOMAIN_com_key.pem; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

add_header Strict-Transport-Security “max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains” always; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

location / {

proxy_pass http://ess2:8000;

  1. Save this file.

/prismhr/configs/nginx/ess-api.conf file

  1. Edit the file: /prismhr/configs/nginx/ess-api.conf

[root@dockerce ~]# vim /prismhr/configs/nginx/ess-api.conf

  1. Find each block of code that contains “#Enable for SSL”

server {

add_header X-Request-Id $request_id;

add_header Strict-Transport-Security “max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains” always;

set $cors ““;

server_name ess-api.YOURDOMAIN.com;

#listen 0.0.0.0:80; -> REMOVE THE #

#Enable for SSL

listen 0.0.0.0:443; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

ssl on; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

ssl_certificate /etc/nginx/certs/YOURDOMAIN_cert.pem; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

ssl_certificate_key /etc/nginx/certs/YOURDOMAIN_key.pem; -> ADD a # to the start of the line

if ($http_origin ~* (?:(?:.*.YOURDOMAIN.com)) ) {

set $cors “true”;

}

location / {

if ($cors = “true”) {

add_header Strict-Transport-Security “max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains” always;

add_header ‘Access-Control-Allow-Origin’ $http_origin always;

add_header ‘Access-Control-Allow-Methods’ ‘POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE, GET, OPTIONS, HEAD’ always;

add_header ‘Access-Control-Allow-Headers’ ‘Origin, X-Requested-With, Content-Type, Accept’ always;

add_header ‘Access-Control-Allow-Credentials’ ‘true’ always;

}

proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto https;

proxy_set_header X-Request-Id $request_id;

proxy_set_header Host $host;

proxy_pass http://ess-api:1337;

}

}

  1. Save this file.

  2. Copy the ess and ess-api conf files to the /prismhr/containerdata/nginx directory.

[root@dockerce prismhr]# cp /prismhr/configs/nginx/* /prismhr/containerdata/nginx/

Configure the Connection to your Data Sources

  1. Edit or adjust the following file:

/prismhr/configs/config.json

[root@dockerce ~]# vim /prismhr/configs/config.json

[

{

“id”: “001”,

“name”: “Prototype”, -> Replace “Prototype” with something such as “Live”, “Test”, etc. so you have a unique value

“ess2Hostname”: “onprem-ep.YOURDOMAINHERE.com”,

“adminToolHostname”: “onprem-epct.YOURDOMAINHERE.com”,

“ess1Url”: “xxx.YOURDOMAINHERE.com/xxx”, -> This section is your ESS 1.0 URL (such as ess.YOURDOMAIN.com/phree)

“uoj”: {

“host”: “XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX”, -> Replace xxx.xxx.xxx.xxx with the IP address of your PrismHR DB server

“username”: “XXXXX”, -> Replace xxxxx with the username making the connection to the PrismHR DB server

“password”: “XXXXX”, -> Replace xxxxx with the password to the username you specified above

“account”: “X:\\davison\\accounts\\HRPY.DBRUN.XXX” -> Modify the path to the Prism account that you are connecting to

},

“docs”: {

“share”: “\\XXX.XXX.XXX.XXX\accounts”, -> Replace xxx.xxx.xxx.xxx with the IP address of your PrismHR Web Server. If your ESS 1.0 application is not under accounts, you will need to change it to that share name

“username”: “XXXXX”, -> Replace xxxxx with the username making the connection to the PrismHR web server

“domain”: “XXXXX”, -> Repalce xxxxx with the domain making the connection

“password”: “XXXXX”, -> Replace xxxxx with the password to the username you specified above

“globalDocsPath”: “XXX\custom\webdocs” -> Replace xxx with the corresponding PrismHR ESS webapp (often this will be phree\custom\webdocs)

},

“slavic”: {

“url”: “xxxxxx”, -> Optional: Replace xxxxx with URL used for your Slavic SSO

“clientId”: “xxxxxx” -> Optional: Replace xxxxx with ID provided by Slavic for SSO

}

}

]

  1. Copy the config.json file to the /prismhr/containerdata/config directory.

[root@dockerce prismhr]# cp /prismhr/configs/config.json /prismhr/containerdata/configs/

Deploy and Start EP

Now that your configurations have been performed to customize your environment for SSL and domain name, you are ready to start using EP.

Perform the following:

  1. [root@dockerce ~]# cd /prismhr/configs

  2. [root@dockerce ~]# docker stack deploy --compose-file docker-compose.yml epstack

  3. Wait approximately 2 minutes to allow all services to start.

  4. [root@dockerce ~]# docker service ls

Note: Note: All services must run 1/1 replicas.

To verify that EP is running, open a web browser and enter the following:

  • For SSL—https://IP-Address/

  • For SSL-disabled environments—http://IP-Address
