Setting Up Third-Party WOTC Form Integration

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/OBE/Setting_Up_Third_Party_WOTC_Form_Integration.htm Path: Task Sheets/OBE/Setting_Up_Third_Party_WOTC_Form_Integration.htm

Setting Up Third-Party WOTC Form Integration

Third-party Work Opportunity Tax Credit (WOTC) forms can be included in the onboarding process using custom forms. When creating a custom form, a hyperlink to the third-party WOTC form can be included, along with any additional information about the form and a field to confirm the employee has completed the form. Once information for the third-party WOTC form has been defined, the custom form can be added to a workflow, and employees can use the hyperlink on the custom form to access and complete the third-party WOTC form during the normal onboarding process.

[Image: screenshot]

Adding a Third-Party URL to a Custom Form

  1. Create a custom form as normal and access the Layout tab on the Edit Custom Form. Refer to Creating Custom Forms (Onboarding) for more information.

  2. From Field Type, drag the Third Party URL field type to the layout on the right.

  3. Click [Image: screenshot] on the Third Party URL field type to display the Field Details panel.

  4. In the Field Label field, enter the text to display as a hyperlink on the form. For example, enter Click here to access the WOTC form.

  5. In the Source URL field, enter the URL of the third-party WOTC form. When the employee clicks this hyperlink during onboarding, the employee’s prehire ID, the client ID, and the PEO ID are automatically appended to the URL, creating a unique URL for the employee. This unique URL allows the form provider to collect information about the employee using an API call and automatically enter that information in the appropriate fields on the WOTC form, eliminating the need for the employee to manually enter their information again.

  6. Click Done to close the Field Details panel.

  7. Optional: drag additional field types to the layout on the right as needed. For example, use the Paragraph field type to enter additional information about the form, or use the Checkbox field type to add a checkbox for the employee to confirm they have completed the form.

  8. Optional: to preview the form before saving, click Preview Form. If additional languages are enabled on your system, a form preview includes an additional language, such as Spanish.

  9. Click Save. The Submit Form dialog box opens, prompting you to specify when the change goes into effect. Refer to Creating Custom Forms (Onboarding) for more information.

  10. Click Submit Form when the form is complete.

  11. Add the form to a workflow as normal. Refer to Creating a Workflow (Onboarding) for more information.

========================================================================

Setting Up ASO Client Accounting

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/ASO_Client_Company_Accounting.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/ASO_Client_Company_Accounting.htm

Setting Up ASO Client Accounting

Note: These instructions assume you have already setup an ASO client.

You can setup ASO Client Accounting using these Client menu options:

  • ASO Accounting Codes

  • ASO G/L Account Setup

  • ASO Global G/L Account Setup

Creating ASO Accounting Codes

Use the ASO Accounting Codes form to create the core chart of account numbers for your client.

To create the core chart of account numbers, do the following:

  1. Select ASO Accounting Codes from the Client|Change menu. The ASO Accounting Codes form opens.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Account code.

  2. Enter the Account Name.

  3. Select Prefix/Suffix Allowed if the client uses a prefix or a suffix in addition to the core account number.

  4. Click Save.

  5. Repeat this procedure for each account you need to setup.

Setting Up ASO Global G/L Account

Use the ASO Global General Ledger Account Setup form to setup global parameters and build the structure that determines how to assign the account numbers to codes within PrismHR.

To setup global parameters and build the structure, do the following:

  1. Select ASO Global G/L Account Setup from the Client|Change menu. The ASO Global G/L Account Setup form opens.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select Allocate Company Contributions if the allocation of company contributions is for cost accounting purposes. The system allocates the company contributions in the same proportion as time sheet earnings.

  2. Select Allocate Employee Deductions to base the allocation of the employee deductions on the same proportion as time sheet earnings. The system allocates the employee deductions in the same proportion as time sheet earnings.

  3. Enter an Account Prefix the client can use.

  4. Enter an Account Suffix the client can use.

  5. Select a Segment Delimiter to separate your client’s G/L account segments.

  6. Enter a Core Account Segment No that indicates the segment of the account number contained in the G/L account setup.

  7. Select an Accounting System Type that determines the type of download PrismHR will create for the client.

Note: Accounting packages that use comma-delimited text files can use the Peachtree format. Therefore, it is also the generic choice for the accounting system type.

  1. Use the Prefix/Suffix Overrides panel to create rules for the global prefix/suffix options.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: This example shows that if the DEPARTMENT code in the system was ADMIN, the client wants the number 100 instead.

  1. Use these panels to setup the ASO Client Accounting, so the G/L codes are assigned to various codes within PrismHR.

[Image: screenshot]

  • You can assign a specific G/L to each pay code, deduction code, benefit plan (including retirement plans), workers’ compensation plan, tax code, and cash account.

  • You can indicate all codes by using an * in the code field and then assigning it a G/L number. You can also assign a G/L account number to the Administration Fee charged to the client.

  • You can setup ASO Client Accounting for a client with a simple chart of accounts, or one that is more complex. The system cannot accommodate all structures, but there are several ways to set these up using the prefix/suffix options to achieve what the client needs.

  1. Use the Bill Code ASO Accounts panel to enter any bill code and account information.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Setting Up ASO G/L Account

Use the ASO General Ledger Account Setup form to build the structure for chart of account number segments if they are different than what is setup in the global parameters. Here you can indicate what overrides, if any, are necessary to meet the client’s account structure requirements.

To build the structure for chart of account number segments, do the following:

  1. Select ASO G/L Account Setup from the Client|Change menu. The ASO G/L Account Setup form opens.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the core Account code.

  2. Select a Segment Delimiter to separate your client’s G/L account segments.

  3. Enter an account Prefix Value, if required.

  4. Enter an account Suffix Value, if required.

  5. Enter an Account Prefix/Suffix Override the client can use.

  6. Enter an Account Override the client may use.

  • The example shows the department code ACCT (as setup in the Department Codes for the client) is replaced with a numeric value of 700. This means that the account number would show as 700-6000 in the report or download for the client.

  • The example also shows that LOCATION was chosen as the field to create an override. When it is equal to MAIN, PrismHR overrides it with an account number 6000.10. So instead of the account number displaying as 6000, it is 6000.10.

If you had both the Prefix/Suffix and the Account overrides filled out in this example, the account number for reports and downloads would be 700-6000.10.

  1. Click Save.

Setting Up Form W-2 Billing Fees

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Billing/Annual-W2-Fee/Form_W-2_Billing_Fees.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Billing/Annual-W2-Fee/Form_W-2_Billing_Fees.htm

Setting Up Form W-2 Billing Fees

Your organization can set up billing for Forms W-2 (W-2s) as a production fee and a per employee fee. You need to define billing codes and billing rules to enable this feature.

Background

Some important things to know about the fees generated by these billing rules:

  • Both rules generate a one-time yearly charge for the client-billing rule combination.

  • The Annual W-2 Production Fee is a one-time flat fee that the system will bill for the current year the first time the rule applies (for example, it would run in 2024 if included in a payroll). If your organization would prefer that it run in January, set the Rule Start Date to a date in January 2025.

  • The Annual Fee per Form W-2 billing rule is based on a count of generated W‑2s. W‑2s are generated in the first quarter of the following year (for example, your organization would generate 2024 W‑2s in Q1 2025). If W‑2s have not been generated yet (such as in early January), then the system processes the rule during each payroll until your organization generates the W‑2s.

PrismHR recommends that you use the option to generate all Form W‑2s at once; you might need to make adjustments for additional Form W‑2s generated after the fee is charged.

  • Each rule generates a miscellaneous billing charge.

Billing Codes

You must set up the billing codes that you intend to use for any client.

To set up the appropriate billing codes:

  1. Create billing codes W2EE and W2BASE.

  2. Set the descriptions and other information as needed.

[Image: Using the Billing Codes form to create codes W2EE and W2BASE.]

Billing Rules

Create unbundled billing rules for each client that should be charged the fees. You can use either or both as needed.

  • For the rule that charges per Form W-2, select Annual Fee Per W-2 from the Based On drop-down.

  • For the rule that charges once per production of all W-2s, select Annual W-2 Production Fee from the Based On drop-down. To ensure that the rule runs only in January, specify a Rule Start Date.

[Image: Using the Billing Rules (Unbundled) form to set up rules based on Annual W-2 Production Fee and Annual Fee Per W-2.]

The system generates the fees when a user creates the employee W-2s (see Printing Employee W-2 Forms).

Related Topics

Using Billing Codes

Using Unbundled Billing Rules

Importing the File

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Billing/Client-Bill-Pending-Import/Client_Bill_Pending_Import.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Billing/Client-Bill-Pending-Import/Client_Bill_Pending_Import.htm

Importing the File

Important! The Bill Pending Import form has been retired. Instead, use the Bill Pending import template in the Data Import Tool. (Refer to the PrismHR Payroll Administration Guide for more information.)

Note: Note the following:

  • If your user role is set to FULL for the Data Import Tool and you enter Bill Pending Import in the search box or select it from the Operations|Import Processing menu, the system redirects you to the Data Import Tool.

  • If your user role is set to INQUIRY for the Data Import Tool, when you try to access the Bill Pending Import form a message displays, “The Bill Pending Import has been moved to the Data Import Tool. Access to the Data Import Tool will need to be added to your user role.” You can access the Data Import Tool by either entering it in the search box or selecting it from the Operations|Import Processing menu, but you cannot use it for importing until you set your user role to FULL. (Clicking OK in the message does not open the Data Import Tool.)

  • If your user role is set to DENY for the Data Import Tool, you cannot access it.

Setting Up Garnishment Setup Fees

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Billing/Garnishment_Setup_Fee.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Billing/Garnishment_Setup_Fee.htm

Setting Up Garnishment Setup Fees

You can set up a billing event for garnishment setup fees and add it to client records for any clients that are charged for those setups.

This system triggers the billing event when a user sets up and saves a garnishment using the Employee Garnishments form. See Notes About Take-On Clients.) You must setup the billing event as described below, and then assign the GSF event code to each client that you need to charge.

Creating GSF Billing Event Code

To begin, you must create a billing event code named GSF. Once you have this in PrismHR, you can use it for all clients that should be charged this fee.

To create a billing event code, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. On the System|Change menu, select Billing Events.

  3. Enter GSF as the Bill Event Code.

  4. Enter a Description for the garnishment setup fee.

  5. Enter the Bill Code related to the garnishment setup fee.

  6. Select Show Detailed if you want to display the details of this billing event on an invoice.

[Image: Creation of GSF billing event with GARNFEE bill code.]

  1. The Standard Price is not used for this particular fee. You will enter the rate for each client in the Client Details Billing tab (see below).

  2. If appropriate, enter values in the other fields (see Establishing Billing Events for more information).

  3. Click Save.

Assigning Fee Setup in Client Details

Once the GSF billing event exists, you assign it to each client that you want to charge for garnishment setups. The system only charges the fee to clients that have it assigned.

To assign the GSF billing event, do the following:

  1. On the Client|Change menu, select Client Details.

  2. Select the Billing tab.

  3. In the Fee Rate Overrides panel, enter or select GSF as the Bill Event Code.

  4. Enter the Rate to charge the client for each garnishment setup.

[Image: Assigning a rate to GSF in the Fee Rate Overrides panel]

  1. If appropriate, enter values in the other fields (see Specifying Client Billing Options).

  2. Click Save.

Notes About Take-On Clients

The garnishment setup fee billing event does not trigger if there are any garnishments setup when importing a take-on client. You should assume that the client is exempt from the fee during that time, or that you can manually enter it as a one-line bulk fee.

Also, note that if you have a new client and the billing template is set to skip employee fees for new clients, the system will not trigger the billing event for any garnishments setup during the first 30 days. This is the same as the behavior of the new employee setup (NES) fee. (See Defining Methods for Billing Clients.)

Stack Invoice with Workers’ Comp Broken Out

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Billing/StackWC_Format_Invoice.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Billing/StackWC_Format_Invoice.htm

Stack Invoice with Workers’ Comp Broken Out

Your organization can use a stack invoice with workers’ compensation broken out for clients.

Once you have completed these procedures, the next time you process a payroll the reports will include the invoice with the report title “Client Invoice Print using STACKWC format”. Workers’ compensation breaks out by state and class combination.

Set Up the Billing Format

You need to create a billing format (invoice) with the Stack Invoice with Workers Comp Broken out alternate print routine.

To set up the billing format:

  1. Open Billing Formats (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change).

  2. Enter the Format ID.

  3. Enter the Format Description.

  4. Select Stack Invoice with Workers Comp Broken out from the Alternate Print Routine drop-down.

  5. Enter a Message ID for any special message format. These formats are defined in System Parameters.

  6. Select Show Past Due to include any open/past due amounts on the invoice.

  7. Select Past Due In Invoice Total to show open and past due invoices in the invoice total. Otherwise, there is only a statement listing at the end.

  8. Select Show On Remit to show open/past due invoice listings on the remittance form.

[Image: Example STACKWC billing format with Alternate Print Routine set to Stack Invoice with Workers Comp Broken Out]

  1. For each line:
  • Enter the Line Description that appears on the invoice in the position indicated, depending on the value of the Print if 0 field.

Note: Note: Position indicated means the order of these line items determines the order of the billing lines when printed. In other words, the first description in this column is the first line printed on the bill, the second description is the second line printed, and so on.

  • If appropriate, enter the Company Account number used to show clients what journal effect to post on their records. This is for special client accounting.

  • Select Yes from the Print if zero drop-down to print the line on the invoice even when the value is zero. If you select No, the invoice does not include the line if its value is zero for that invoice.

Click

[Image: magnifying glass over paper]

to see the list of bill codes included in the line.

Click

[Image: pen and paper]

to add bill codes to the line item.

Enter each Bill Code. The Bill Description displays.

Click Save in the Bill Codes for Line Item form.

[Image: Example of the Bill Codes for GROSS WAGES grid]

  1. Click Save in the Billing Formats form.

Set Up the Billing Template

Make sure that the billing template used by the client is set to bill for and sort by workers’ compensation.

To set up the billing template:

  1. Open Billing Templates (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change).

  2. Enter the Template ID used for the client.

  3. Verify that the Workers Comp Sort is selected.

  4. Verify that Workers Comp (if Hourly) and Workers Comp TRIA are selected in the Add column.

[Image: Unbundled PEO billing template. Workers Comp Sort is enabled, along with Workers Comp (if Hourly), Workers Comp TRIA. Other W/C fields are highlighted as well.]

Note: Note: See Defining Methods for Billing Clients for information on the other fields in the form.

  1. Click Save.

Set Billing Information for the Client

Verify that the client is set up to use the stack invoice with workers’ compensation broken out billing format.

  1. Make sure that the correct client record displays.

  2. Open Client Details (found in the Client menu under Client|Change).

  3. Select the Billing tab.

  4. Verify that Billing Template is one that bills for workers’ compensation (see Set Up the Billing Template).

  5. Verify that the Bill Format is the one that you set up to use the Stack Invoice with Workers Comp Broken out alternate print routine (see Set Up the Billing Format).

  6. Select Pay Codes with W/C Class w/Price Levels from the Bundled Billing Based On drop-down.

[Image: Billing panel with Unbundled Billing Template, STACKWC Bill Format, and bundled billing based on Pay Codes with W/C Class w/ Price Levels.]

  1. Click Save.

Blocking and Suppressing Taxes

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Blocking_and_Suppressing_Taxes.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Blocking_and_Suppressing_Taxes.htm

Blocking and Suppressing Taxes

There are several ways to block or suppress taxes. For example, you may want to block a local tax. You may also have taxable wages, but no tax.

Resolution or Workaround

Blocking a Tax

There are several ways to block taxes. When you block taxes, the wages will still accrue, but the tax calculation will be blocked.

  • Open the Tax tab on Employee Details (found in the HR menu under HR|Change).

[Image: screenshot]

  • You can override for Federal Income Tax in the yellow highlighted section. In the Override Type drop-down, select Block Withholding.

In the following example, the employee’s Federal Income Tax calculation is $238.88.

[Image: screenshot]

The​ Block Withholding​ option allows you to block calculated tax. Wages will still accrue. For example, if you have requested to block the tax, then the amount withheld will be $0.00. The override is denoted in the Filing Status panel of the Employee Voucher in the Override Type and Override Amount fields.

[Image: screenshot]

  • You can override for State Income Tax in the purple highlighted section. One of the options available is to Block Withholding.

In the following example, the employee’s State Income Tax calculation is $50.30.

[Image: screenshot]

The Block Withholding option allows you to block calculated tax. Wages will still accrue. For example, if you have requested to block the tax, then the amount withheld will be $0.00. The override is denoted in the Filing Status panel of the voucher in the Override Type and Override Amount fields.

[Image: screenshot]

Tax Calculation Overrides

  • Open Tax Calculation Overrides (found in the Payroll menu under Payroll|Action).

[Image: screenshot]

In the Income Tax Overrides panel, you can override all the taxes in the same section. One of the options in the Override Type drop-down is Block Withholding.

To override, enter the authority ID (not the whole Tax Deduction Code). Entering 00 will only affect Federal Income Tax and entering a state code will only affect the State Income Tax.

A Starting Pay Date and Ending Pay Date must be entered in order for these to take effect. The entry will be ignored if there is no date.

In the following example, the employee’s Federal Income Tax calculation is $224.60. The State Income Tax calculation is $39.00.

[Image: screenshot]

The Block Withholding option allows you to block calculated tax. Wages will still accrue. For example, if you have requested to block Federal Income Tax and State Income Tax, then the amount withheld will be $0.00 and $0.00. The override is denoted in the Filing Status panel of the voucher in the Override Type and Override Amount.

Note: Note: This will not affect Local Income Tax. Even if entered, it will be ignored.

[Image: screenshot]

Suppress Taxes

There are several places in the system that can be used to suppress taxes. Suppressing taxes not only suppresses the tax calculation, but will also suppress the wages from accruing. In most cases, reciprocity is still taken into consideration when using suppress.

The following are examples:

  • Open System Parameters (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change). Open Miscellaneous Global Parameters by selecting Miscellaneous Parameters in the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] .

This will suppress taxes system-wide and is available for all tax codes (employee and/or employer).

Note: Note: Reciprocity is still taken into consideration when using suppress at the system level.

[Image: screenshot]

In the example above, the tax code 38-26 is being suppressed. This means on the calculated voucher, this tax will not appear at all.

[Image: screenshot]

If the tax suppression is removed, the wages are accrued and the tax is calculated:

[Image: screenshot]

  • Open the Tax tab on Client Details (found in the Client menu under Client|Change).

This will suppress taxes client-wide and is available for all tax codes (employee and/or employer).

Note: Note: Reciprocity is still taken into consideration when using suppress at the client level.

[Image: screenshot]

In the example above, the tax code 361133200-39 is being suppressed. This means on the calculated voucher, this tax will not appear at all.

[Image: screenshot]

If the tax suppression is removed, the wages are accrued and the tax is calculated:

[Image: screenshot]

  • Open Tax Calculation Overrides (found in the Payroll menu under Payroll|Action). In the Miscellaneous Overrides panel, check the Suppress Local Tax checkbox.

This checkbox will allow you suppress either the resident city tax or the resident local tax for the specified employee.

Note: Note: Reciprocity is still taken into consideration when using suppress here.

[Image: screenshot]

In the example above, the resident city tax is chosen. This will suppress both the wages and the taxes. The tax code will not appear on the voucher at all.

[Image: screenshot]

If the tax suppression is removed, the wages are accrued and the tax is calculated:

[Image: screenshot]

  • Open Tax Calculation Overrides (found in the Payroll under Payroll|Action). In the Miscellaneous Overrides panel, select Yes in the Suppress Work State Tax drop-down.

This will allow you suppress the work state tax for the specified employee.

Note: Note: Reciprocity is also suppressed using this option.

[Image: screenshot]

In the example above, this will suppress both the wages and the taxes. The tax code will not appear on the voucher at all (in this case, New Jersey Income Tax (tax code 31-20)).

[Image: screenshot]

If the tax suppression is removed, the wages are accrued and the tax is calculated:

[Image: screenshot]

  • Open Tax Calculation Overrides (found in the Payroll menu under Payroll|Action). In the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] , select Suppress Taxes.

This screen allows you to suppress taxes at the employee level for all tax codes.

Note: Note: Reciprocity is still taken into consideration when using suppress here.

[Image: screenshot]

In the example above, this will suppress both the wages and the taxes. The tax code will not appear on the voucher at all (in this case, New Jersey Income Tax (tax code 31-20).

[Image: screenshot]

If the tax suppression is removed, the wages are accrued and the tax is calculated:

[Image: screenshot]

Blocking and Suppressing Taxes

  • The tax is suppressed, but the resident tax is still not taking enough

If you want to suppress a tax and not have the reciprocity taken into consideration, what you would want to do is both block the tax and suppress the tax. This will act like the suppression (i.e., no wages will accrue), but will block the tax from reciprocity.

In the following example, the employee lives in Norwalk, OH and works in Sandusky, OH. The tax rate for Norwalk is 1.5% and the tax rate for Sandusky is 1.25%.

The following conditions are applicable to wages taxes for Ohio residents who live and work in different Ohio municipalities:

  • Withhold tax for city employment, and

  • Withhold tax for the city of residence, reduced by the credit amount allowed by the city of residence.

With no suppressions or blocks, the employee’s tax will calculate at 1.25% for Sandusky and will calculate at .25% for Norwalk. This is because Norwalk allows a 100% credit up to 1.50% for the tax paid to the city of employment (which was 1.25%).

[Image: screenshot]

If the Sandusky location is suppressed, it will suppress both the tax and the wages. Reciprocity is still taken into consideration when using suppress, so the Norwalk taxes will still be reduced by what would have calculated for Sandusky.

[Image: screenshot]

If the Sandusky tax is blocked, it will just block the tax from calculating but the wages will still accrue. Reciprocity is also blocked here.

[Image: screenshot]

But, if you do not want the wages to accrue either, if you block and suppress the tax, it will act like a suppression but will block the reciprocity.

[Image: screenshot]

Calculating Overtime Using FLSA Rulesets

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Calculating_OT_Using_FLSA_Rulesets.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Calculating_OT_Using_FLSA_Rulesets.htm

Calculating Overtime Using FLSA Rulesets

The Fair Labor Standard Act (FLSA) instructs employers how to calculate overtime. Before using the FLSA Ruleset, there are some system setup issues that need to be completed.

The tasks that need to be completed are:

  • Set up pay codes and additional pay options

  • Set up FLSA Rulesets

  • Update system parameters, pay codes, and pay groups

  • Assign an FLSA Ruleset

Pay Code Setup

FLSA pay codes can be set up on the Pay Codes form. The following sections assume you have already navigated this form.

To set up pay codes for FLSA rulesets:

  1. In the Back Office menu, select System|Change > System Parameters.

  2. Enter pay code information in the Pay Codes panel.

  3. Enter relevant bill codes in the Billing panel.

  4. Select any additional pay code attributes in the Processing Options panel.

  5. Click Save.

The following example will explain how to set up pay codes for half-time pay (HALF) and overtime pay (O/T1).

For more information on each field in the three panels, see Working with Pay Codes.

Setting Up Half-Time Pay Codes

  1. Enter the Pay Code ID, such as Half.

  2. Enter the following information in the Pay Codes panel:

Field Name Value
Pay Type Regular
Pay Class Earnings
Cash/Non-Cash Cash
Calculation Method Standard
Pay Reason Hours
Pay Stub Name HALF PAY
Retirement Earnings Enabled
Subject to Work Comp. Yes, This Pay Code is Subject to W/C

Enter any other field values as required.

  1. Enter the following information in the Standard Method Rules panel:
Field Name Value
Factor 0.50
Rate Class Premium

  1. In the Billing panel, set the Bill Code to 000.

  2. In the Processing Options panel, select Pay Code is Used to Pay Productive Work.

  3. Click Save.

Setting Up Overtime Pay

  1. Enter the Pay Code ID, such as O/T1.

  2. Enter the following information in the Pay Codes panel:

Field Name Value
Pay Type Regular
Pay Class Earnings
Cash/Non-Cash Cash
Calculation Method Standard
Pay Reason Hours
Pay Stub Name OT REGULAR
Retirement Earnings Enabled
Subject to Work Comp. Yes, This Pay Code is Subject to W/C

Enter any other field values as required.

  1. Enter the following information in the Standard Method Rules panel:
Field Name Value
Factor 1.00
Rate Class Premium

  1. In the Billing panel, set the Bill Code to 000.

  2. In the Processing Options panel, select Pay Code is Used to Pay Productive Work.

  3. Click Save.

Including Additional Pay

You can use several pay code Processing Options to tell PrismHR what additional pay should be included.

Make the following updates to any pay codes that should be included in the overtime calculation, such as commissions, discretionary bonuses, and tips.

  1. Enter or select the pay code to modify.

  2. In the Processing Options panel, select either Include Hours/Amount Paid in Overtime Calculation or Include Amount Paid Only in Overtime Calculation:

Field Name — Description

Include Hours/Amount Paid in Overtime Calculation — Enable this option if you want the system to look at the Hours/Units Paid record when calculating the overtime hours.

Use Hours Paid instead of Hours Worked in Overtime Calculation — Enable this option if you want the system to look at the Hours Worked field to calculate the overtime hours/rate (excluding Sick, Vac, and so on).

Include Amount Paid Only in Overtime Calculation — For any fixed amount pay codes (such as Bonus) that you want the system to calculate into the weighted O/T rate.

Note: Note: Do not try to enter hours worked into a fixed-amount pay code and then mark it to calculate based on hours worked – this will not work. Hours worked and fixed flat amounts must be reported separately for FLSA calculations.

FLSA Ruleset Setup

You can now assign these pay codes to the FLSA Ruleset.

To create and update FLSA rulesets:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. Under System|Change, select FLSA Ruleset.

  3. Enter or select a Ruleset ID.

There are two Reporting Methods: (O) Overtime Hours Reported Separately and (S) Straight Hours Reported on One Pay Code.

(O) Overtime Hours Reported Separately

In the Premium Pay Code field, enter the pay code you set up with a factor of 0.50. (Example: HALF).

(S) Straight Hours Reported on One Pay Code

Set the straight reporting method:

  1. In the When Hours Worked in a Week Are Greater Than field, enter the number of hours worked on a weekly basis before overtime is to be applied.

For example, 40.

  1. In the Pay Excess Hours with Pay Code field, enter the pay code used to pay employees who work overtime.

(Premium pay code with 0.50 factor, for example: HALF).

Set up the daily overtime rules:

  1. In When Hours Worked in a Day are Greater Than, enter the number of hours worked in a day if there is a daily straight pay hour limit.

  2. In the Pay Excess Hours with Pay Code field, enter the premium pay code of appropriate factor, example: HALF.

  3. In the When Consecutive Days Worked in a Week is GT field, enter the minimum number of consecutive days employees must work in one week to receive premium pay.

  4. Click Save.

Tipped Positions

In order to create FLSA rulesets for tipped positions, the following actions must be completed first:

  • Updating System Parameters

  • Updating Pay Codes

  • Updating Position Codes

  • Updating Pay Groups

After setting these up, you can assign the FLSA ruleset.

Updating Pay Codes

Create Pay Codes: System / Change / Pay Codes

Tip Credit Makeup

  1. Enter or select the Pay Code ID, for example, TCM.

  2. Set up the Pay Codes panel:

Field Name Value
Pay Type Regular
Pay Class Earnings
Cash/Non-Cash Cash
Calculation Method Standard
Pay Reason Hours
Pay Stub Name TIP MAKEUP
Retirement Earnings Enabled
Subject to Work Comp. Yes, This Pay Code is Subject to W/C

Enter any other field values as required.

  1. Enter the following information in the Standard Method Rules panel:
Field Name Value
Factor 1.00
Rate Class Straight

  1. In the Billing panel, set the Bill Code to 000.

  2. In the Processing Options panel, enable the following options:

  • Pay Code is Used to Pay Productive Work

  • Used to Pay Cash Wage for Tipped Employees

  • Include Amount Paid Only in Overtime Calculation

  1. Click Save.

Reported Tips

  1. Enter or select the Pay Code ID, for example RTIPS.

  2. Set up the Pay Codes panel:

Field Name Value
Pay Type Regular
Pay Class Reported Tips
Cash/Non-Cash Non Cash
Calculation Method Fixed
Pay Reason Units
Print On Paystub YES, in All Cases
Pay Stub Name
Retirement Earnings Disabled
Subject to Work Comp. No, This Pay Code is Not Subject to W/C

Enter any other field values as required.

  1. In the Fixed Method Rules panel, set the Rate Class to Straight.

  2. In the Billing panel, set the Bill Code to 000.

Set Non-Cash Rebate Bill Code to TIPCR.

  1. Leave the Processing Options disabled.

  2. Click Save.

Updating System Parameters

After setting up the Tip Credit Make Up pay code, assign it globally using the Global Codes panel of the System Parameters form.

[Image: TCM pay code entered in the Tip Credit Make Up Pay Code field on System Parameters]

Updating Position Codes

To update position codes for FLSA rulesets:

  1. Select the Client menu.

  2. Under Client|Change, select Positions.

  3. Enter or select the appropriate Position Code.

  4. Enable the Tipped Position option.

  5. Update any other fields as required.

  6. Click Save.

Assigning the FLSA Ruleset to a Client

To assign an FLSA ruleset to a client:

  1. Select the Client menu.

  2. Under Client|Change, select Client Details.

  3. Select the Payroll tab.

  4. Enter the ruleset ID in the FLSA Ruleset field.

  5. Specify the FLSA Work Week Ends On day.

  6. Click Save.

Updating Worksite Locations

To update worksite locations for FLSA rulesets:

  1. Select the Client menu.

  2. Under Client|Change, select Worksite Locations.

  3. Enter or select the appropriate Location Code.

  4. Expand the Other Parameters panel.

  5. Enter the FLSA Ruleset and, for tipped employees, the FLSA Ruleset (Tipped EE).

  6. Update any other fields as required.

  7. Click Save.

Updating Pay Groups

To update pay groups for FLSA rulesets:

  1. Select the Client menu.

  2. Under Client|Change, select Pay Groups.

  3. Enter or select the appropriate Pay Group.

  4. Select a day in the Work Week Ends On field.

  5. Update any other fields as required.

  6. Click Save.

Time Entry and Calculation

Depending on the FLSA reporting method you chose to set up, you will submit all of the straight hours either under one pay code OR reporting the overtime separately, along with any additional pay codes (such as your tip code).

Once you finalize and calculate it, the payroll will reflect the weighted overtime on review vouchers and reports.

Setting Up Certified Payroll Reporting

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Certified_Payroll_Reporting.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Certified_Payroll_Reporting.htm

Setting Up Certified Payroll Reporting

PrismHR provides features to support the completion of Form WH-347 and other Certified Payroll Reporting documents.

You can use these features to:

  • Set up the Pay Codes and Project Codes you want to include in the certified payroll

  • Configure project information on the Time Sheet Detail screen of the payroll batch

  • Generate Certified Payroll Reports to guide you in your data entry and submission

You can use the Certified Payroll Report Format to apply a set of options to state-specific Certified Payroll Reports. You can also create reports for specific states if they require additional information that is not on the standard Form WH-347 report.

Background

If an organization performs contracted or sub-contracted work on an industrial government project — such as construction or waste management — it must pay its employees in accordance with federal guidelines and demonstrate its compliance by filing Certified Payroll Reporting forms. These forms provide Time Sheet data to prove that employees were paid appropriately for their work. The federal version is Form WH-347, but states can also define their own versions. For example, the state of California has special guidelines for submitting compliance information in an XML format.

You can also set up PrismHR to track this data under a “Certified Payroll” project. There are five major steps in this setup process:

  1. Create Pay Codes and flag them for Certified Payroll Reporting.

  2. Create a new Project to track this work under the appropriate client.

  3. When entering Time Sheet information, specify employee certified payroll hours using these new Pay and Project Codes.

  4. Optional (Only complete this step if you know what you’re doing!): Set up additional Certified Payroll Reporting Formats as needed.

  5. Generate the report.

Step 1: Defining Pay Codes for Certified Payroll Reporting

You can use the Pay Codes form to flag a specific pay code as Certified Payroll Reporting-related.

Note: Note: For some state-specific versions of the Certified Payroll Report, you must assign fringe class codes to the appropriate fringe pay codes used by the company. Otherwise, no amounts will display in that section of the report.

To define a Pay Code for certified payroll:

  1. Click Back Office. Select System > Change > Pay Codes.

  2. Enter any required information in the Pay Codes panel.

  3. Locate the Processing Options panel at the bottom of the screen.

  4. Select Include in Certified Pay Report Detail if you want employee hours worked to display on the report.

  5. If you want employee fringe benefits (meals, pension contributions, and others) to display on the report, select Include on Certified Pay Report Fringe Summary.

  6. If you enabled the option in the previous step, select the appropriate value from the Certified Pay Report Fringe Class field.

  7. Click Save.

Repeat this process for every pay code you need to include in the Certified Payroll Report. Don’t forget to set up this Pay Code on the Control and Time Sheet tabs of the Client Details form, as you would for any other pay code.

Step 2: Defining a Project for Certified Payroll

Use the Client > Change > Projects form to specify the details that will appear on your report.

Initial Setup

To define a client project for certified payroll:

  1. Open the Client > Change > Projects form.

  2. Enter a Project Code, or click Project to select one from a list.

  3. Enter a Description for this certified payroll project code.

  4. Select Certified Payroll. The Certified Payroll Details panel displays.

  5. In the Certified Payroll Details panel, enter the appropriate Certified Payroll Report Format, or click Certified Payroll Report Format to select one from a list.

Entering Other Certified Payroll Details

Fill out the other Certified Payroll Details information, if required:

  • Enter the Wage Decision Number for this certified payroll project.

  • Enter the Wage Decision Date for this certified payroll project.

  • Enter the Project City.

  • Enter the two-character Project State postal code, or select from a list by clicking Project State.

Setting up Calculation Options for Certified Pay Rates

You can use the Certified Pay Rates table to enter the certified pay rate information for each position code related to the project.

You can also use the settings below to modify the system pay rate calculation logic:

Field Description
Use Higher of Prevailing Wage Rate or Employee’s Pay Rate This setting determines the logic for calculating an employee’s certified pay rate. Select this option if you want to use the following logic: - First, the system compares the employee’s Hourly Rate (displayed in their Employee Details) with the sum of the Base Rate and Fringe Rate in the Certified Pay Rates record. If the Hourly Rate is higher than the sum of Base Rate and Fringe Rate, then the employee’s Hourly Rate is used. - If the employee’s Hourly Rate is not higher than the combined Project Base Rate and Fringe Rate, then the system compares the Hourly Rate to the Project Base Rate and pays the higher of the two. Then, the system compares the employee Benefits Per Hour (stored in Employee Details) with the Project Fringe Rate. If the employee Benefits Per Hour is higher, then no action is required and the system calculates the pay as the higher of the employee Hourly Rate or Project Base Rate. - If the Project Fringe Rate is higher, the system pays the difference between the two in the employee’s Hourly Rate of pay.
  • First, the system compares the employee’s Hourly Rate (displayed in their Employee Details) with the sum of the Base Rate and Fringe Rate in the Certified Pay Rates record. If the Hourly Rate is higher than the sum of Base Rate and Fringe Rate, then the employee’s Hourly Rate is used.
  • If the employee’s Hourly Rate is not higher than the combined Project Base Rate and Fringe Rate, then the system compares the Hourly Rate to the Project Base Rate and pays the higher of the two. Then, the system compares the employee Benefits Per Hour (stored in Employee Details) with the Project Fringe Rate. If the employee Benefits Per Hour is higher, then no action is required and the system calculates the pay as the higher of the employee Hourly Rate or Project Base Rate.
  • If the Project Fringe Rate is higher, the system pays the difference between the two in the employee’s Hourly Rate of pay. | Subtract Benefits/Hour from Calculate Pay Rate | Select this option if the employee’s cost of benefits per hour should be subtracted from their pay rate when the system is determining the higher of the employee or job rate (see description above). | | Filter Cert Pay on Job | Select this option if you want to use position codes to determine whether to include wages in the Certified Payroll Report. | ### Setting Up Basic Report Information

To start setting up certified payroll reporting:

  1. Indicate whether you are working with a Contractor Or [a] Subcontractor.

  2. Enter the Name and Address of the Contractor.

  3. Enter the Project and Location.

  4. Enter the Project or Contract No.

  5. Enter the Signatory Name of the person working on this project.

  6. Enter the Signatory Title of the person working on this project.

  7. Select the Fringe Benefits Paid to Approved Plans option if fringe benefits are paid for this project.

  8. Select the Fringe Benefits Paid in Cash option if fringe benefits are paid in cash for this project.

  9. Select Report Hours Paid Instead Of Hours Worked? if you want to include pay codes that are flagged for inclusion in the Certified Payroll Report, but which do not represent hours worked — for example, sick time.

Setting Up Parameters for the Certified Payroll California XML Output

In the Certified Payroll California XML Parameters panel, enter any required information for clients that submit certified payrolls in XML format to the state of California.

[Image: screenshot]

To configure parameters for the CA XML output:

  1. Select the CA License Type. The options are: Contractor State License Board, Professional License, or Other License.

  2. Enter the CA License No that should be presented in the CA XML file.

If you selected Other License as the CA License Type, enter the license type and license number in this field, separated by a colon. For example: California Motor Carrier Permit: 0123456.

  1. Enter the CA PWCR, which is the 10-digit California Public Works Contractor Registration number. Enter NA in this field for “not applicable”.

  2. Enter the CA Ins Number for this project.

  3. Enter the CA Contractor Email for this project.

  4. Enter the Awarding Body for this project.

  5. Enter the Contract Agency Id and the name of the Contract Agency.

  6. Enter the CA Awarding Body ID, which is the ID number of the public entity that owns the project.

  7. Enter the CA Awarding Body Proj No, which is the project number assigned to the project by the public entity.

  8. Enter the CA Awarding Body Contract ID, which is the contract ID given by the public entity to the project.

  9. Select CA Stmt of NonPerformance only if you need to indicate a state of non-performance. The system will not include employees in the file. Do not select this option if you want to include employees in the file (this is typical, and the value in the CA XML file will be false for this option).

Proceed to the Options panel.

Setting Up Other Options

Note: Note: If this project is no longer active, select Obsolete.

To set up other certified payroll options:

  1. Select Track Phases if the phases of this project are tracked for job costing purposes.

  2. Enter the Project Class, or click Project Class to select one from a list.

  3. Select Other W/C Coverage in Place if other Workers’ Compensation coverage applies to this project.

  4. If this is a union project, select the Union Project option. Then, enter the Union Code to use for the Union Trust Fund Report. If you select this option, the “OC” sub-class will be used for hours worked on this project.

  5. When you finish with your setup, click Save.

  6. Repeat this process for every Project you want to include in the Certified Payroll Report.

Step 3 (Optional): Maintaining Certified Payroll Report Formats

WARNING: Do not change the fields in the Certified Payroll Report Format form unless PrismHR directs you to do so.

You can use the Certified Payroll Report Format form to ensure that your report meets Federal or State specifications. More specifically, this form configures the options available for displaying Certified Payroll Reports generated using mvQuery.

Please note that the information on the Certified Payroll Report Format form controls the mvQuery version of the Certified Payroll Report.

To configure Certified Payroll Report formats:

  1. Open the Client > Change > Projects form.

  2. Select Certified Payroll Report Format to select the appropriate format for the certified payroll report. If the project is federally-funded, then you should select FG (federal government) as the Certified Payroll Report Format. If the project is state-funded and the state report requires additional information, then you should use the format for that specific state.

  3. Enter a Wage Decision Number.

  4. Enter a Wage Decision Date.

  5. Enter a Project City.

  6. Enter the two-character Project State code.

  7. Click Save.

Step 4: Entering Time Sheet Data

Once you have set up the appropriate Pay Codes and Project Codes, you can associate them with individual employees using their Detail Time Sheet Entry form.

To enter detailed time sheet information for certified payrolls:

  1. Open the SuperBatch form (found in the Back Office menu under Operations > Action).

  2. After you enter the necessary information in the SuperBatch form, click the time sheet icon for the appropriate client. The Summary Time Sheet Entry form displays.

  3. Click an employee to display the Detail Time Sheet Entry form for that employee.

  4. If you need to change the Charge Date, place your cursor in the field and enter the correct date (or select it from the calendar).

  5. Enter any remaining information.

  6. Ensure that the Project field contains the Certified Payroll Project. If you need to change the Project, you can enter the correct Project code or click Project to select it.

  7. Click Save. Repeat this process for another charge date, if necessary.

  8. When you finish with this employee, click Return. The system returns you to the Summary Time Sheet Entry form.

  9. Repeat this process for each employee as necessary.

  10. Click Return. The system returns you to the SuperBatch form.

  11. Repeat this process as needed for every payroll batch you need to include in the Certified Payroll Report.

Step 5: Creating Certified Payroll Reports

You can create a Certified Payroll Report using PrismHR. Currently, only the WH-347 format is supported in Web Edition.

Creating a Certified Payroll Report in PrismHR

Certified Payroll Report formats are controlled by the Certified Payroll Report Parameters form in PrismHR. Make sure you set up the proper format before creating a report (see Step 3 (Optional): Maintaining Certified Payroll Report Formats).

Note: Note: If you are running this report for vouchers that have not been posted yet, select the Process Initialized Vouchers Instead of Posted option.

[Image: screenshot]

To create a Certified Payroll Report in PrismHR:

  1. ClickReports menu and select Payroll > Report > Certified Payroll Report.

  2. Enter the Week Ending Date.

  3. Enter values for the Project Code and Payroll Number.

  4. Click Run to create the report. Samples of the Certified Payroll Report are shown below.

Converting a PEO Client to an ASO Client

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Converting_PEO_to_ASO.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Converting_PEO_to_ASO.htm

Converting a PEO Client to an ASO Client

Note: You should have a basic understanding of Employer Details and Client Details in PrismHR before completing this process.

To perform this conversion, complete the following steps:

  1. Entering Employer Details: Create a new employer.

  2. Establishing Employer SUTA Rate: Enter the SUTA cost for the new employer.

  3. Establishing Employer W/C Accrual Mods: Set up the Workers’ Compensation cost for the new employer.

  4. Entering Employer Bank Accounts: Set up the employer’s bank accounts for cutting checks and/or direct deposit.

  5. Defining Employer Billing Templates: Set up billing templates for the ASO.

  6. Selecting Client Details: Select the client that is changing to the ASO; update the Detail, Control, and Billing tabs.

Entering Employer Details

Use the Employer Details form to enter a new employer ID and general information about the employer. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To enter a new employer ID and general information about the employer, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Employer|Change, select Employer Details.

  3. Enter the new Employer ID, which should be the same as the ASO’s current client ID.

  4. Enter the Employer Print Name, which is the way the employer’s name should print on checks, reports, and so on.

  5. Enter the Federal EIN issued to the employer by the IRS in the format ##-#######.

Note: The accounting files put the company number before the account number. QuickBooks and PeachTree cannot process the numbers, so PrismHR removes the company number when it builds files for those programs. You can use the G/L Company Number for accounting purposes to group data, such as for an ASO.

  1. Enter the G/L Company Number used as the prefix to the general ledger account number.

  2. Refer to the online help or the Employer Administration User Guide for information on the other fields in Employee Details form.

  3. Click Save.

Establishing Employer SUTA Rate

Use the Employer Tax Rates (SUTA) form to establish the SUTA and State Disability Insurance rate information for an employer and the states where they do business. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To establish the SUTA and State Disability Insurance rate information, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Employer|Change, select Employer Tax Rates (SUTA).

  3. Define the general Tax Rate information:

Employer — Enter the Employer ID.

State — Enter or select the State in which the employer does business.

Effective Date or New Date — Select an existing Effective Date, or click New Date and enter the date on which the rates go into effect. Note: The effective date is when the rate goes into effect, not when the employer received the rate.

  1. Enter the employer’s SUTA Rate for the state.

  2. Enter other information as needed. (Refer to the online help or the Employer Administration User Guide.)

  3. Click Save.

Establishing Employer W/C Accrual Mods

Use the Employer W/C Accrual Mods form to establish the workers’ compensation rates for a selected employer and state. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To establish the workers’ compensation rates, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Employer|Change, select Employer W/C Accrual Modifiers.

  3. Enter the Employer ID.

  4. Enter or select the State Code in which the employer does business.

  5. Enter a new Effective Date, or select an existing date.

  6. Enter the employer’s Discount/Debit rate for this state.

  7. Enter the employer’s Modifier rate for this state.

The Effective Rate displays, based on the Discount/Debit and Modifier rates.

  1. You can override the rates by policy in the Override by W/C Policy panel.

  2. Enter other information as needed. (Refer to the online help or the Employer Administration User Guide.)

  3. Click Save.

Entering Employer Bank Accounts

If you are going to cut checks or direct deposit information from the client’s own bank account, enter the basic information about the account in the Bank Account panel of the Bank Accounts form. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: The 00 Checking Account is a special purpose account, the master account for all transactions. Each file header shows the 00 Checking Account as the main file header, with the funds being transferred into the account from whatever checking account the individual ACH shows it is processed. This adds a level of abstraction to the process, but allows a single account to process all the ACH clearing with only summary transactions from each of the actual checking accounts.

To enter employer bank account information, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Bank Accounts.

  3. Enter the bank Account ID for this account. It must be an integer.

  4. Enter a Reference for the name of the financial institution where this account is maintained, as well as any other information. For example, ASO’s Payroll Cash Account at Fleet Bank.

  5. Enter the Bank Name.

  6. Enter the Bank Address and City, State & ZIP Code, which is the street or mailing address.

  7. Enter the Transit Number assigned to this financial institution. This number prints on checks drawn on this bank unless you enter a different number in Transit Number (Chks) below.

  8. Enter the client’s Account Number at this financial institution. This account is debited when an offsetting settlement entry is created.

  9. Enter the Fractional Transit number that appears on checks. This number is the same for all checks drawn on a unique checking account, typically in the format NN/NNN.

  10. Enter the Check Title.

  11. Enter the Employer ID who is the owner of the checking account, from a general ledger point of view. (See See Entering Employer Details.)

  12. Enter other information as needed; refer to the online help or the Payroll User Guide.

  13. Click Save.

Defining Employer Billing Templates

Use the Billing Templates form to define an ASO template for the client, if necessary. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To define an ASO template, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Billing Templates.

  3. Enter the Template ID used to access this billing template.

  4. From the Template Type drop-down, select ASO.

  5. Complete the other fields as needed for this client. (Refer to the online help or the Billing User Guide for more information.)

  6. Click Save.

Selecting Client Details

Select the client you are changing to an ASO client;

To update the information, do the following:

  1. Make sure the correct client record is shown near the top of the page. If not, click it and then select the correct client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Client Details.

Detail Tab: Status

The Status panel contains client business information. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select Administrative Services Only from the Service Type drop-down.

  2. Enter the Employer ID that you created in Employer Details.

  3. Click Save.

Control Tab: Employer Accounting

In the Employer Accounting panel, you can specify the ASO general ledger template that contains an association between benefit plans, retirement plans, billing codes, or pay codes to general ledger accounts if necessary.

Note: Note: You will only change this information if you have a separate general ledger template for ASO service types.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select the G/L Template to use for checking accounts, accounts receivable, or inter-company transaction accounts. Template codes allow different clients to use different G/L account numbers for posting.

  2. Enter any G/L codes you want to use.

GL Code Enter the GL Code that represents a general ledger distribution.
Misc G/L Code Enter the Misc G/L Code that represents a special transaction.
  1. Click Save.

Account Tab: Bank Account

Note: Note: You will only need to change this if you are using the client’s bank account information. (See See Entering Employer Bank Accounts.)

The Bank Accounts panel establishes the various bank accounts used by the client. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select the account ID for each bank account:

Payroll Check Account — The account from which to draw payroll checks.

Direct Deposit Account — The account from which to draw direct deposit (ACH) amounts. If left blank, the system uses the Payroll Check Account.

Garnishment Check Account — The account from which to draw wage garnishments checks of the company’s employees.

Child Support EFT Account — The account from which to draw child support garnishments electronically.

Receipts Account — The account to use for ACH or Credit Card receipts.

Third Party Check Account — The account from which to draw Third Party checks.

  1. Click Save.

Billing Tab: Billing

Use the Billing panel found in the Billing tab to specify the information required to bill the client. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select the Billing Template used to compute billing for this client.

  2. Enter the Bill Format of the invoice to send to the customer.

  3. Enter the A/R Customer No., which is the ID of the client to bill.

  4. Click Save.

Correcting a Duplicate Employee Record

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Correcting_a_Duplicate_Employee_Record_Prism.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Correcting_a_Duplicate_Employee_Record_Prism.htm

Correcting a Duplicate Employee Record

WARNING: PrismHR strongly advises you to test this in your Demo account before performing these tasks in your Live account.

If an employee is entered twice with two different Social Security numbers (SSNs), two different sets of records for that employee exist in the system. If the system pays the employee under both SSNs, you must transfer the information from the incorrect record to the correct record to ensure that only one Form W-2 generates.

Terminating Employee Record with Incorrect SSN

First, you must terminate the incorrect employee on the Employee Termination form.

To terminate the incorrect employee, do the following:

  1. Click the HR menu.

  2. From HR|Action, select Employee Termination. The Employee Termination form opens.

  3. Complete the following:

Use this option To do this
Employee ID Enter the employee ID with the incorrect SSN (required).
Termination Status Code Select a termination status code.
Reason Code Select a reason code (required).
Termination Date Select the termination date (required).
Termination Explanation Enter an explanation for the termination. For example, Incorrect SSN.
  1. Click Save.

Editing the Terminated Employee Record

After terminating the employee record with the incorrect SSN, you can edit the terminated employee SSN on the Employee Details form.

To edit the employee SSN, do the following:

  1. Click the HR menu.

  2. From HR|Change, select Employee Details. The Employee Details form opens.

  3. Click the Personal tab.

  4. In the Personal panel, click Edit SSN. Enter a unique SSN that differs from the correct employee record. For example, 777-88-9999.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click the Name tab.

  2. To prevent anyone from using the employee record, edit the employee name so users know it is not a valid employee record. For example, John Doe - DO NOT USE.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

If the employee was paid under the incorrect SSN, you must transfer the tax amounts to the correct employee record. To transfer the tax amounts, subtract the taxes from the incorrect tax register and add them to the correct tax register. First, you must obtain the employee and employer tax totals for the employee paid under the incorrect SSN.

Obtaining Tax Totals for Tax Adjustments

You can find the employee and employer tax totals for the employee paid under the incorrect SSN on the Payroll Voucher form.

To obtain tax totals for tax adjustments, do the following:

  1. Click the Payroll menu.

  2. From Payroll|View, select Payroll Voucher. The Payroll Voucher form opens.

  3. Select the correct Voucher Number, then click the Tax tab. Make note of the information in the Employee Tax and Employer Tax panels.

After obtaining the employee and employer tax totals, you can adjust the taxes.

Adjusting Taxes

With the employee and employer tax totals for the employee paid under the incorrect SSN, you can make a tax adjustment on the Tax Adjustment form.

To make tax adjustments for the employee paid with the incorrect SSN, do the following:

  1. Click the Payroll menu.

  2. From Payroll|Change, select Tax Adjustment. The Tax Adjustment form opens.

  3. Enter the Employee ID, Adjustment Date, Employer, and negative amount for taxes.

If you do not want to bill the client for the tax adjustment, enable Suppress Billing. If disabled, the system bills the client for the adjustment and updates the billing registers. Then, you must enter both the employee and employer tax withholdings.

  1. Click Save.

To make tax adjustments for the employee with the correct SSN, do the following:

  1. Click the Payroll menu.

  2. From Payroll|Change, select Tax Adjustment. The Tax Adjustment form opens.

  3. Click New adjustment.

  4. Enter the Employee ID, Adjustment Date, Employer, and a positive amount for taxes.

If you do not want to bill the client for the tax adjustment, enable Suppress Billing. If disabled, the system bills the client for the adjustment and updates the billing registers. Then, you must enter both the employee and employer tax withholdings.

  1. Click Save. The system displays a dialog box asking if you want to print a refund check now, of if you want to update scheduled payments and reimburse next pay period. Click No.

After clicking No, you can go to the Scheduled Payments form to delete the tax refund for both the incorrect and correct employee record.

Deleting Tax Refunds

To delete the tax refund for both the incorrect and correct employee record, use the Scheduled Payments form.

To delete the tax refunds, do the following:

  1. Click the Payroll menu.

  2. From Payroll|Change, select Scheduled Payments. The Scheduled Payments form opens.

  3. Enter the Employee ID.

  4. Delete the tax refund from the grid.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

After deleting the tax refunds from the system, you can use the Employee Tax Summary form to verify the taxable earnings and tax amounts for the incorrect and correct employee.

Verifying Taxes Adjusted Correctly

To verify the taxable earnings and tax amounts for the incorrect and correct employee, use the Employee Tax Summary form.

To verify the tax information, do the following:

  1. Click [Image: screenshot] (Back Office).

  2. From Employer|View, select Employee Tax Summary. The Employee Tax Summary form opens.

  3. Enter the incorrect Employee ID and Year. (No data displays.)

  4. Enter the correct Employee ID and Year. The amounts should reflect the tax adjustment and any wages paid under the correct employee ID.

Both employees have adjustment vouchers in their history explaining the pay and tax adjustments.

If you do not want to perform a tax adjustment to correct the paid wages and taxes, you can void payroll vouchers for the incorrect SSN and then calculate and post the pay for the correct SSN. Things to consider:

  • You can post pay by pay date or by quarter.

  • Do not to enter pay that crosses from one quarter to the next.

  • Before processing your ACH file, remove any ACH entries for crediting and debiting the employee and client.

The manager must log in to PrismHR (not Employee Portal (EP)) to manage their employees’ time off requests. Worksite managers and trusted advisors can view, approve, and cancel employee Time Off requests.

Performing Multiple ACH Processing

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Multiple_ACH_Processing.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Multiple_ACH_Processing.htm

Performing Multiple ACH Processing

To complete multiple ACH processing, the following must occur:

  • Define email addresses for ACH orignators and user who runs ACH process

  • Select ACH files and process them by clicking Build File

  • Verify processing status

  • Review system-generated emails

  • System sends files to SFTP server

Step 1: Before Processing Multiple ACH Files

Perform the following steps before starting multiple ACH processing:

  1. Define email addresses for employees who must be notified about ACH processing in the Email addresses for multiple ACH processing panel on the ACH Originators form, which maintains the financial institution information. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Note the email address of the user who runs the ACH process because that user also receives an email after the process finishes. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Step 2: Processing Multiple ACH Files

To process multiple ACH files at once:

  1. Access the ACH Processing form on the Operations|Action menu. (Note: Click Select All if you want to process multiple files or select individual files.) For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. After selecting all the files you want to process, click Build File. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. To complete the process, click Yes in the message that displays. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Step 3: Verifying Processing Status

To verify multiple ACH processing status, access the Process Control Dashboard form. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Step 4: Reviewing Email Verifications

The system generates the following emails when multiple ACH processing finishes:

  • The following example displays the email that the system delivers to the email addresses listed on the ACH Originators form.

Note: The system generates an email for each originator processed. For example, if two files were selected for processing, then two emails are generated.

[Image: screenshot]

  • The following example displays the email that the system delivers to the user who built the files, which includes a summary of the originators that processed and whether they were successful or failed.

[Image: screenshot]

Step 5: After Multiple ACH Processing Finishes

Once the process is complete and the emails are generated, the files are sent to the PrismHR SFTP server.

Note: PrismHR is responsible for defining and setting up the SFTP server for clients when processing multiple ACH files.

New Hire Download Processing

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/New_Hire_Proc/New_Hire_DL_Proc_PHR.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/New_Hire_Proc/New_Hire_DL_Proc_PHR.htm

New Hire Download Processing

Note: Note that the New Hire Reporting form (Process ID: HRPY.WORK*NEW.HIRE.REPORTING.ENTRY) can include information on employees from restricted clients. The system produces a complete file which includes annual salary without exceptions based on the Client Access Group and the restricted clients listed in it. For this reason, it is recommended that access to this form be limited to users with unrestricted access to all clients in the database.

The New Hire Reporting feature builds a file that includes all required criteria and meets specifications for all states.

The utilities for new hire report processing are:

  • New Hire Reporting - Creates a New Hire Report that you can review for accuracy before building the download file to send to the state. See Previewing New Hire Reporting.

  • Clear New Hire Reporting Dates - Clears the previously assigned Hire Report Dates so you can run New Hire Reporting again. Currently this process is performed in the backend system for PrismHR; .

  • Set New Hire Reporting Dates - Sets the date for previously reported employees so they will not be included in the New Hire Reporting file. This process is also performed in the backend system for PrismHR; .

O Method FLSA Calculation of D/T Hours

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/O_Method_FLSA_Calc_DT_Hours_HRP.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/O_Method_FLSA_Calc_DT_Hours_HRP.htm

O Method FLSA Calculation of D/T Hours

In California, double-time (D/T) hours are paid at one times the regular rate of pay. When there are more than two rates of pay or non-discretionary income, the weighted average method is used for calculation premium pay or HALF pay.

Your organization can set up the system to allow for the 50% premium pay calculation for the D/T pay code, thus including D/T in the weighted average at one times the regular rate, and the DT1 pay code (for straight double-time pay) then calculates at one time the weighted average rate.

Your organization can use a DT1 pay code as the regular double-time pay code using the 1x pay rate, and DTPREM as the premium double pay code, with a rate that is 1x the calculated weighted average rate for the O-type FLSA Rulesets.

  1. Set up the DT1 pay code so that the Add to D/T Hours (Threshold) option is selected.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Set up the DTPREM pay code as required.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Set up an FLSA Ruleset where:

  • The Reporting Method is set to (O) Overtime Hours Reported Separately.

  • The Premium Pay Code is set to HALF.

  • The Double Premium Pay Code is set to DTPREM.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In PrismHR, enter the ruleset ID in the FLSA Ruleset field of the Client Details Payroll tab.

  2. Enter the FLSA Work Week Ends On day.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. The next payroll will calculate correctly.

Note: Note: Note that the payroll’s period end date must coincide with the FLSA Work Week Ends On day.

Overriding Geocode for Employee Residence

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Override_GeoCode_EE_Residence.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Override_GeoCode_EE_Residence.htm

Overriding Geocode for Employee Residence

You can override the employee’s Resident Address Geocode on the Employee Details form, so the system will calculate taxes using a different taxing municipality than the one for their residence.

Note: Before beginning this process, refer to the Vertex Calculation Guide in the Customer Resource Center to determine the geocode for the appropriate taxing municipality for the selected employee.

To override the geocode for the employee residence, do the following:

  1. Go to the Employee Details form on the HR|Change menu.

  2. Enter the Employee ID, then select the Address tab.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In the Override Geocode field, enter the geocode determined in step 1.

  2. Enter the Override End Date on which the Override Geocode should no longer be used.

Note: If there is no end date at this time, then leave this field blank. The geocode override remains in effect until it is changed.

  1. Click Save.

Setting Up Pay Cards and Direct Deposit

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Pay_Card/Pay_Cards_and_Direct_Deposit.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Pay_Card/Pay_Cards_and_Direct_Deposit.htm

Setting Up Pay Cards and Direct Deposit

Your organization can set up employees with a pay card, for the employee to receive their pay. If employees choose to, they can set up a checking account to use for direct deposit instead. The system automatically shifts their pay from the pay card to the checking account once the checking account passes pre-note.

Custom Feature Code for the Client

You need to set the custom feature for each client to enable this feature.

To enable the custom feature code:

  1. Open Client Details (found in the Client menu under Client|Change).

  2. Open the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and select Custom Features.

  3. Enter the Feature Code PAYCARD-AUTOMOVE.C.

[Image: Feature Code PAYCARD-AUTOMOVE.C]

  1. Click Accept.

  2. Click Save in Client Details.

Bank Routing for Pay Cards

You need to set up the information for the financial institution to be used for pay cards with the debit card update option.

To set up bank routing for pay cards:

  1. Open Bank Routing (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change).

  2. Enter the Routing Code for the financial institution (also known as the transit number). The other information populates.

  3. Select PayCard (Automove). This indicates that your organization uses the account for pay cards that can automatically be replaced by a checking account.

  4. Select Debit Card Update. This indicates that this routing code supports debit cards and requires an update to the issuer for use of a new card.

  5. If you track debit card updates, the Debit Card Track option displays. Select this option if this financial institution tracks a debit card ID. The system then requests the debit card ID at ACH entry.

[Image: Bank routing setup with PayCard enabled]

  1. Click Save.

Pay Card for Employee

You need to set up each employee with the pay card.

To set up employee pay cards:

  1. Open Employee Details (found in the Back Office menu under HR|Change).

  2. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window.

  3. Select the Deposit tab.

  4. Ensure that the Status fields are set correctly.

[Image: Deposit status settings]

  1. Enter the Direct Deposit Information:

  • From the Type drop-down, select Checking.

  • Enter the Transit Number (routing code). The Bank Name displays.

  • Enter the employee’s Account Number.

  • Select the appropriate option from Pay Type (typically All).

  • Select the appropriate option from the Method drop-down (typically Remaining Balance).

  • If appropriate, enter values in the Amount and Limit fields.

  • From the Status drop-down, select Deposit Active.

[Image: Direct Deposit Information panel]

  1. Click Save.

The account displays in Employee Portal (EP) as a Pay Card account.

Direct Deposit Settings

The employee can go into EP and change the direct deposit settings. For example, they might want to send some or all of their pay to a checking account.

  1. The employee logs into EP.

  2. They navigate to their direct deposit settings (your organization determines the menu path).

  3. The employee clicks Add Account and then enters their account information. They must define a checking account.

[Image: Transit Number, Account Number, Calculation Method (remainder), and Account Type (checking)]

  1. The employee saves their changes. The account is added to the list as Pending.

[Image: Checking account and pay card accounts set up]

The checking account is in prenotification status.

[Image: Direct Deposit Information panel, with a checking account in Prenotification status]

Once it passes pre-note, the system deactivates the pay card and distributes the employee’s pay to the new account. Until that time, the system continues to pay the employee via the pay card.

Note: Note: If the employee chooses to continue to deposit some moneys to the pay card and some to the new account, the system distributes the amounts appropriately.
