Processing Payroll: Wire Transfer Pending Status

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Payroll_Proc_WTP_Status.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Payroll_Proc_WTP_Status.htm

Processing Payroll: Wire Transfer Pending Status

Payroll processing includes an approval step called Wire Transfer Pending (WT.PEND) to ensure that a client’s electronic funds have been successfully transferred before payroll checks can be processed.

  • Setting the Client to Wire Transfer

  • Processing the Wire Transfer

  • Resetting the Wire Transfer Status

Setting the Client to Wire Transfer

Use this procedure to set up the client to be use wire transfer.

Note: Note: During payroll processing, you cannot bypass the payroll approval step if the client is set up for wire transfer. Instead, the system automatically sends the approval notification email after you calculate the payroll.

  1. Make sure the correct client record is shown near the top of the page. If not, click on it and then select the correct client.

  2. Open Client Details (found in the Client menu under Client|Change).

  3. Select the Billing tab, then locate the Electronic Billing panel.

  4. Set the ACH Status to Wire Transfer Client.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Processing the Wire Transfer

Once the client has been established as a Wire Transfer Client, and after the payroll calculation (and any other intervening steps that apply), the payroll enters Wire Transfer Pending status

[Image: screenshot]

.

Note: Note: Refer to the relevant topics in the payroll online help for the procedures involved in processing a payroll.

[Image: screenshot]

The payroll remains in the Wire Transfer Pending status until a user indicates that your organization has received the funds.

  1. Open Payments Pending (found in the Back Office menu under Operations|Action).

  2. Click the Payroll number. The entire row highlights.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Mark Received. The line clears from Payments Pending.

  2. In SuperBatch, the status becomes Wire Transfer Received and Payroll Is Ready to Finalize. Users can continue to process the payroll.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note that if a user recalculates the payroll, the status resets. Return to step 1 of processing the wire transfer.

Resetting the Wire Transfer Status

Users can reset the payroll from Wire Transfer Pending to Payroll Pending Review (INIT.COMP) from Payroll Control.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. If the payroll is currently in the Wire Transfer Pending status and needs to be reset to Payroll Review, open Payroll Control:

  • From SuperBatch, click the options button for the payroll [Image: screenshot] and select Payroll Control.

  • From the Payroll menu, select Payroll Control (found under Payroll|Change) and then enter the Payroll Number.

  1. Click Reset Wire Transfer.

  2. Click Yes in the message to confirm that you want to reset the payroll to Payroll Pending Review status.

  3. When you are finished, click Save.

Accounting for 401(k) Retirement Plan Matches

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/PEO_Client_Accounting_401K_Match.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/PEO_Client_Accounting_401K_Match.htm

Performing PEO Client Accounting for 401(k) Match

Occasionally, you may need to account for a 401(k) Retirement Plan Match when the client isn’t billed for the match. In this instance, use the following instructions to do this in PrismHR.

To account for a 401(k) Retirement Plan Match, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change, select Billing Codes.

  3. On the Billing Codes form, create a memo bill code. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. To update the G/L Setup, navigate to the G/L Setup form on the System|Change menu. Select the desired GL Template, then select Billing Code in the GL Type field. Enter the GL Account Number in the applicable memo billing code and save the record.

[Image: screenshot]

Daily Method

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/PerDiemWages/Per_Diem_Daily_Method.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/PerDiemWages/Per_Diem_Daily_Method.htm

Daily Method

With the daily method, the client reports total wages, along with the number of days each individual driver has been out. The system takes the number of days out that the user enters and multiplies it by the per day amount. The daily amount is set up on an individual employee basis.

Create a per diem Daily method pay code:

  1. Open Pay Codes form (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change).

  2. Enter a per diem Daily Pay Code and Pay Description for your organization.

  3. Complete the fields in the Pay Code, Per Diem Method Rules, and Billing Code panels as appropriate.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Set up Daily Per Diem rate for an employee:

  1. Open the Employee Details form (found in the HR menu under HR|Change).

  2. Enter the Employee to whom the daily per diem method applies.

  3. Select the Pay tab.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Daily Per Diem rate for this employee.

  2. Click Save.

Mileage Method

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/PerDiemWages/Per_Diem_Mileage_Method.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/PerDiemWages/Per_Diem_Mileage_Method.htm

Mileage Method

With the mileage method, the client reports the total mileage. The pay rate per mile varies by tier and is broken into taxable wages and non-taxable per diem wages. Both the taxable and non-taxable per diem wages are defined as rate per mile.

Note: Note: This works only for companies that do not have Shift set as an entry for Detail Time Sheet Parameters.

Ceate a per diem Mileage method pay code:

  1. Open the Pay Codes form (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change).

  2. Enter a per diem Mileage Pay Code and Pay Description for your organization.

  3. Complete the fields in the Pay Code, Per Diem Method Rules, and Billing Code panels as appropriate.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Set up Mileage Rates for per diem:

  1. Open the Mileage Rates form (found in the Client menu under Client|Change).

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Mileage Code to use for per diem rates.

  2. Enter a Description.

  3. Enter the Mileage Rate, which is the gross amount paid per mile.

  4. Enter the Per Diem Rate, which is a portion of the total rate that makes up the non-taxable per diem. The system automatically calculates the Taxable Rate.

  5. Click Save.

Add Mileage as an option in Detail Time Sheet Entry:

  1. Select the appropriate client and then open Client Details.

  2. Select the Time Sheet tab.

  3. In the Detail Time Entry Parameters panel, enter a new row.

  4. Add Mileage as a Detail Time Sheet Field.

  5. Set the Required, User Access, and Hidden options as appropriate.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Payroll processors must enter the defined mileage code and mileage rate:

  1. When processing the payroll, for each employee who earned mileage, enter the information as described below:
a. Access Detail Time Sheet Entry.
b. Enter the Pay Code, defined earlier.
— —
c. Enter the Units Paid (miles).
— —
d. In the Mileage field, enter the mileage rate, defined earlier.
— —
e. Verify the Pay Rate per mile.
— —

[Image: screenshot]

f. Click Save.
g. Select another employee, or click Return.
— —
  1. When ready, process the payroll.

Setting Up Per Diem Wages

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/PerDiemWages/Per_Diem_Wages.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/PerDiemWages/Per_Diem_Wages.htm

Setting Up Per Diem Wages

This section describes how to set up driver per diem wages in PrismHR that are particular to the trucking industry.

You can define per diem wages using one of three methods:

  • Percentage method: Total wages are entered as a whole and PrismHR breaks this information down into taxable wages and non-taxable per diem wages. The percentage used to calculate the non-taxable per diem amount is set up at the Position or Department level.

  • Mileage method: The client reports the total mileage. The pay rate per mile varies by tier and is broken into taxable wages and non-taxable per diem wages. Both the taxable and non-taxable per diem wages are defined as rate per mile.

  • Daily method: The client reports the total wages, along with the number of days each individual driver has been out. The system takes the number of days out that the user enters and multiplies it by the per day amount. The daily amount is set up on an individual employee basis.

Processing Pay On-Demand Payments

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Processing_Pay_On_Demand_Payments.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Processing_Pay_On_Demand_Payments.htm

Processing Pay On-Demand Payments

Using the Pay On-Demand feature, you can pay employees in real time without having to wait for an ACH transaction. This process uses pay cards to pay employees off-cycle for instant payment. The most common uses for this feature are:

  • Paying someone quickly for hours missed

  • Paying a terminated employee’s wages on the same day they are terminated

To generate a file for processing payments, use the Pay On-Demand Processing, which is available on the Operations|Action menu. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Only service providers can run pay on-demand files to pay employees. However, worksite managers are also allowed to set up pay on-demand accounts and process pay on-demand payrolls.

Using the Pay On-Demand Feature

Perform the following tasks in order when you are ready to use the Pay On-Demand feature:

Task/Guide — Description

Store employees’ account information — Use the Pay On-Demand Account panel on the Employee Details form to store employees’ account information for pay on-demand processing.

Payroll Setup Guide — Create a manual payroll to use for pay on-demand processing.

Mark manual payroll as Pay On-Demand payroll — Select the Pay On-Demand option on the Payroll Options form to pay employees on-demand through a manual payroll, where the system places the funding information in a file format for the pay on-demand vendor.

Add employees with active Pay On-Demand Account on Payroll Control form — When a manual payroll is set to Pay On-Demand, only employees with an active pay on-demand account display in the list when you select an Employee in the third panel on the Payroll Control form. (This also applies when selecting employees using the Quick Load option.)

Payroll Processing Guide — Process the manual payroll.

Generate pay on-demand file — Use the Pay On-Demand Processing form to generate the pay information in a file that can be uploaded to a vendor.

Provide the vendor with the generated file — Clients are responsible for transferring the generated file to the vendor.

Setting Up Employee’s Account Information

Enter the employees’ account information for pay on-demand processing in the Pay On-Demand Account panel on the Deposit tab in the Employee Details form. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: An employee might also have this pay card information set up in the Direct Deposit Information panel. However, if they need to use this pay card for Pay On-Demand payments, you also need to enter the account information in the Pay On-Demand Account panel. (The system only uses the account information entered in the Direct Deposit Information panel for ACH transactions.)

When you set the pay on-demand account Status to Active, after you enter a Transit Number and move to the Account Number field, the system automatically displays the Vendor associated with that transit number. In this example, the transit number is associated with the rapid! PayCard vendor:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: In addition to the Pathward transit number (124085244), the system also pre-populates the Vendor field with rapid! PayCard if you use the Greendot transit number (124303162).

Note: Note the following:

  • Employees are only allowed one pay on-demand account where these types of funds will be deposited.

  • You can only use a pay on-demand account when the payroll is marked as Manual in the payroll options. (The system ignores this account for any other payroll type.).

  • If you complete some of the fields but not all of them and click Save, the following error message displays, “You must complete the Pay On-Demand Account setup on the direct deposit section.” Click OK to continue.

  • The system records changes made to the pay on-demand account information in the audit log.

  • Payrolls set to Pay On-Demandignore the settings in the Amounts Excluded from Direct Deposit panel.

Setting Field Security for Pay On-Demand Account Panel Fields

In addition, you can set field security for the fields in the Pay On-Demand Account panel by doing the following:

  1. Open the User Roles form on the System|Change menu and select the user role.

  2. Select Field in the EMPLOYEE.PER*MAINT.DEPOSIT process ID row. The Field Security Maintenance form opens.

  3. Click Add and select the Pay On-Demand Account panel fields, which begin with “POD,” then click Accept. The fields display in the Field Security Maintenance form. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click N in the Required column to set the field security to Y.

Note: You can also set field permissions to Enable, Disable, or Hidden on the employee record. (When set to Hidden, the Manager field does not display.)

  1. Click Save, then click Save in the User Roles form.

Setting Up Payroll

Set up employee on-demand payments by selecting the Pay On-Demand field on the Payroll Options form. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: To access the Payroll Options form, select Options on the Actions menu in the Payroll Control form.

Selecting Pay On-Demand allows you to pay employees on-demand through a manual payroll, where the system places the funding information in a file format for the pay on-demand vendor.

Note: Manual payrolls set to Pay On-Demanddo not pay employees using the ACH process.

Note: Note the following when using Pay On-Demand:

  • If you save a manual payroll with Pay On-Demand enabled and then later disable it, the following warning displays:

[Image: screenshot]

  • If you select Suppress ACH with Pay On-Demand selected, the following error message displays:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: This message also displays if you create a manual payroll with Pay On-Demand selected and then apply a Payroll Option Template with Suppress ACH selected within a timesheet. The error message displays and the system does not apply the Payroll Option Template.

Before Initiating Pay On-Demand Processing

When you’re setting up a manual Pay On-Demand payroll, it’s important to note the following:

  • If you enter employees who do not have a pay on-demand account in their employee record before setting the payroll to Pay On-Demand on the Payroll Options form and click Save, the following error message displays:

[Image: screenshot]

In this situation, make a selection as follows:

  • Yes: Employees without an active pay on-demand account are removed from the payroll, then you can click Save again and the payroll will save.

  • No: Employees without an active pay on-demand account are not removed, but you cannot save the payroll. At this point, you must either disable Pay On-Demand or manually remove these employees from the payroll.

In addition, if you try to disable the Pay On-Demand field on the Payroll Options form after payroll calculation completes, this field is dimmed and you cannot make this change.

  • If you apply a Payroll Option Template that is set to Suppress ACH to a manual Pay On-Demand payroll and click Save, the following error message displays:

[Image: screenshot]

Click OK to continue, then select a non-ACH Payroll Option Template.

Working with the Pay Date

When you set a manual payroll to Pay On-Demand, the system automatically sets the Pay Date to the current date since the employee will be paid today (instant payment). For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Adding Employees with Active Pay On-Demand Account

When a manual payroll is set to Pay On-Demand, only employees with an active pay on-demand account display in the list when you select an Employee in the third panel on the Payroll Control form. (This also applies when selecting employees using the Quick Load option.)

Note: If you enter an Employee who does not have an active pay on-demand account an error message displays, “Employee <xxxxx> must have an active Pay On-Demand account to be added to a Pay On-Demand payroll.”

Note: Note the following:

  • If you try to change that automated Pay Date the system displays a warning, “This is a Pay On-Demand payroll so the Pay Date must be the current date.” (Click OK to continue.)

  • If you enter or select a Pay Date that is not the current date and then select the Pay On-Demand field on the Payroll Options form the system displays a warning, “Pay On-Demand payroll must be paid using the current date. By selecting this option the pay date is updated to today.” (Click OK to continue.)

After you click Accept and return to the Payroll Control form, the system automatically changes the Pay Date to the current date.

Reviewing Pay On-Demand Employee Status

After you process a pay on-demand payroll, D*PD displays in the Status column on the Banking tab in the Payroll Voucher form for employees whose direct deposit account is set to Pay On-Demand in their payroll control record. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Reviewing the Number of Pay On-Demand Checks Paid

You can review the number of checks paid in payrolls that use the Pay On-Demand feature (that is, the payroll type is Manual and the vouchers have the D*PD status) in the Pay On-Demand Count column. This column displays on the Payroll History form, which is available on the Payroll|View menu. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Generating the Pay On-Demand File

After you complete the tasks listed above, use the Pay On-Demand Processing form to generate the pay information in a file that can be uploaded to a vendor. (Note: Clients are responsible for transferring the generated file to the vendor.)

Note: After generating the file, it remains in the File History, so you can reference this information if there is an issue with the file transfer or if you need to review what was included in the last file.

To generate a file that contains employees set to Pay On-Demand:

  1. Select Pay On-Demand Processing from the Operations|Action menu.

Note: Note the following:

  • When you access the Pay On-Demand Processing form, the system locks the form to ensure that other users cannot process the file at the same time. After you click Close, the system unlocks the form and the following message displays:

[Image: screenshot]

  • If the Pay On-Demand Processing form is locked when you access it, you can clear the lock by selecting Pay On-Demand Clear Lock on the Operations|Change menu.
  1. Generate a file that contains the pay on-demand information by selecting the Vendor Type. After you generate this file, you can transfer it to the selected vendor. (The system adds the file to the File History panel.)

Note: Note the following:

  • If the selected Vendor Type does not have any additional pay on-demand payrolls available for processing the following error message displays, “There are no Pay On-Demand vouchers to be processed for the vendor <vendor name>.” Click OK to continue.

  • Select the rapid! PayCard vendor type to process a pay on-demand file for the rapid! Paycard vendor, so the file format is set to match the vendor’s expected output.

  • Select the Other vendor type to process a pay on-demand file for a vendor who is not supported, which provides access to information that you can relay to your vendor.

  1. The system displays the Voucher Count and the Total amount (the net pay value of all the vouchers) for the selected vendor type.

  2. Click Generate File. The system stores the file in the File History panel and a Download File link displays, where you can do the following:

  • Download File link: Click this link to open a browser window that displays a link to the file in the bottom-left corner, then click that link to open the file in .csv format. This file contains data that you need to provide to the pay card vendor.

  • File History panel: After generating the file, you can review the data again that displays in this panel. (After selecting the sort criteria, the vendor’s most-recent files display.)

Searching for a File

After generating a file that you can upload to its vendor, you can view the file’s data again by searching for it using the sort criteria in the File History panel:

  • File Date: Enter or select the date when the file was run.

  • Vendor Type: Select the vendor type. (See step 1, above, for more information about the Vendor Type.)

Note: The system stores files for up to six months.

After making the sort criteria selections, the table displays up to 100 of the vendor’s most-recent files. (If there are more than 100 files, you can use the paging options to scroll through the rest of the files.)

The table displays the following information:

  • Date/Time: Date and time the file was initially run

  • Created By: User name of the user who generated the file

  • File Name: Unique name of the file

  • Vendor: Name of the vendor selected when the file was created

  • Voucher Count: Number of vouchers included in the file

  • Total: Net total of the vouchers listed in the table

  • File: Link that opens the file

Viewing Employees Set Up with Pay On-Demand Accounts

You can view which employees have pay on-demand accounts and which of those accounts are active or inactive on the Pay On-Demand Account Report form.

To access this form, select the Pay On-Demand Account Report link, which displays on the Pay On-Demand Processing form, under the Related Actions header. (You can also access this report on the Operations|Payroll menu.) For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note the following:

  • This form uses Client Access Group security.

  • Service providers and worksite managers can only view employees who they are allowed to access.

  • The Pay On-Demand Account Report link does not display for users whose roles are set to INQUIRY or DENY for the Pay On-Demand Account Report form.

  • If you view the Pay On-Demand Account Report before clicking Generate File but after selecting a Vendor Type in the Pay On-Demand Processing form the file generation fails and the following error message displays, “There were no Pay On-Demand vouchers to be processed for <vendor>,” even if there are vouchers available to process.

  • If you re-select the Vendor Type and generate the file again without viewing the Pay On-Demand Account Report, the system completes the process.

Voiding Pay On-Demand Payments

Employees who are paid using pay on-demand processing have an invoice amount that is debited on the ACH file. However, if you void the pay on-demand voucher, the invoice amount displays as a credit on the ACH file.

Note: Note the following:

  • If you run the pay on-demand process (that is, you generate a file) and then you complete a void, no changes occur in the history file.

  • If you do not generate a file and a void occurs, then the system does not include the voucher in the next file creation.

Recalculating Overtime in a Payroll

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Recalc_OT_In_Payroll.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Recalc_OT_In_Payroll.htm

Recalculating Overtime in a Payroll

These instructions explain how to recalculate overtime for a payroll in PrismHR.

Overview

In this example, the process calculates any additional overtime for hourly salespersons that were:

  • Paid weekly on an hourly basis

  • Paid a bonus or commission on the first week of each month for sales in the previous month

The calculations are based on regulations defined in the Code of Federal Regulations, Title 29, Part 778 published by the Department of Labor (DOL) because the employees were paid overtime on weekly pay cycles based on a fluctuating work week. When the company pays the commission that covered the prior pay periods, the company must recalculate the overtime, incorporating the paid commission amounts and paying the overage to the employee at the same time as the bonus or commission.

For example:

  • An employee has earned $384 in bonuses or commissions for a four-week period.

  • You must pay their bonus or commission in a payroll type of Special (created in Payroll Control) and enter the bonus or commission amount on the Batch Payments form for that payroll.

  • The employee is due overtime compensation after 40 hours.

  • During the prior four-week period, the salesperson worked 44, 40, 44, and 48 hours.

Therefore, the salesperson is due additional compensation weeks 1, 3, and 4. They are not owed any additional compensation for week 2 because they did not work overtime.

Based on the following formula (described in Part 778 of the regulations), the employee is due additional compensation of $4.36 each for week 1 and week 3:

  • $384 (bonus/commission) / 4 (weeks) = $96

  • $96 / 44 (hours worked that week) = $2.18

  • $2.18 / 2 = $1.09

  • $1.09 x 4 (overtime hours) = $4.36 additional compensation

Using the same formula above, you would pay an extra $8 for week 4 in which the employee worked 8 overtime hours.

How to Recalculate Overtime

Use this procedure to recalculate overtime for a payroll.

  1. Open Pay Codes (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change.

Show me an example of the Pay Codes panel:

Figure 89: Pay Codes

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Define the pay code that you use to pay the commission (or any other type of pay) along with the following values:

  • Select Fixed in the Calculation Method field

  • Select Units in the Pay Reason field

  1. Click Save. Use this pay code to pay the additional overtime for the company.

  2. Open Client Details (found in the Client menu under Client|Change).

  3. Select the Payroll tab.

  4. In the Payroll Processing Options panel, enter the new pay code in the Recalculated O/T Pay Code field.

Show me an example of the Payroll Processing Options panel:

Figure 90: Payroll Processing Options

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

  2. Open the Batch Payments form (found in the Payroll menu under Payroll|Change.

Show me an example of the Batch Payments form:

Figure 91: Batch Payments

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: Bonus/commission amounts must be paid in a Special batch.

When you set up the payroll to pay the bonuses/commissions on the Payroll Control form, the Period Start and Period End fields must reflect the period during which the employee was paid overtime. You must also indicate that the system needs to recalculate overtime.

Referring to the previous example, assume the four-week period was in February and the commission batch has a pay date of March 4th. The Period Start date is set as 1/31/18 and the Period End date is 2/25/18.

  1. Select the Payroll Number.

  2. Select the Employee ID.

  3. Select the Pay Code.

  4. Enter the Pay Amount.

  5. Open the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and select Options.

  6. Select the Recalculate Overtime option in Payroll Options.

Show me an example of the Recalculate Overtime option in the Payroll Options form:

Figure 92: Recalculate Overtime option in Payroll Options

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Accept.

  2. Click Save.

  3. Calculate the payroll and PrismHR recalculates overtime.

Refunding a Garnishment Payment

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Refund_Garnishment_Payment.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Refund_Garnishment_Payment.htm

Refunding a Garnishment Payment

You might need to refund a garnishment payment when:

  • Your organization took a garnishment payment in error.

  • The payee returned the garnishment check and you need to credit the employee.

Step 1: Void the Garnishment Check

  1. Open Void and Reissue Checks (found in the Payroll menu under Payroll|Action).

  2. You can enter the Checking Account from which the check was drawn. If you use the search to locate the checks, the search form lists only checks drawn from that account.

  3. For each check you need to void:

  1. — Enter the Check Number of the check to void. It must be a check issued for the selected client (displayed at the top of the form). The other fields populate with the information on that check.

  2. — Enter a Void Reason for the check. This will display on the Banking tab of the Payroll Voucher form.

  1. Click Void and Reissue Checks. The system voids the check in the list.

Step 2: Refund Employee

Since the system does not automatically create a refund to an employee for the garnishment deduction, you need to set it up as a negative deduction on the employee’s garnishment record.

  1. Open Employee Garnishments (found in the Payroll menu under Payroll|Action).

  2. Enter the Docket Number. The other fields in the form populate.

  3. Make sure that there is no Stop Date on the actual garnishment record.

If the garnishment truly needs to be stopped going forward, enter the date it should stop after you process this refund.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In the Override Rules panel, enter the amount as a negative in the Override Amount field.

  2. Enter the pay date of the current payroll batch in the Override Start Date and Override Stop Date fields.

If you need to issue the refund off-cycle, you need to create a manual batch with a “penny” check (.01 hours at .01 cents per hour) and use the date from that batch.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In the Calculation Rules panel, make sure the Total Garnishment is one cent over what has been collected to date, so the system will read the record as active.

[Image: screenshot]

[Image: screenshot]

Setting Up Reported Tips

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Reported_Tips.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Reported_Tips.htm

Setting Up Reported Tips

This topic guides you through the payroll process of setting up reported tips and tip overtime. The procedures include:

  • Updating System Parameters

  • Updating Position Codes

  • Updating Pay Codes

  • Setting Up FLSA Rulesets

  • Updating Pay Groups

  • Viewing Time Sheet Entry

Updating System Parameters

Use the Tip Credit Make Up Pay Code field on the System Parameters form to enter the code for tip credit.

To set the global tip credit make up pay code:

  1. Open System Parameters (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change).

  2. Scroll down to the Global Codes Setup panel.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select the appropriate Tip Credit Make Up Pay Code. The code will be used to pay the tip credit make up amount calculated.

  2. Click Save.

Updating Position Codes

Use the Positions form to indicate this job is a tipped position.

To set up a position code as a tipped position:

  1. Open the Positions form (found in the Client menu under Client|Change).

  2. Enter the Position Code to edit.

  3. Select Tipped Position to indicate this is a tipped position.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Updating Pay Codes

Use the Pay Codes form to update pay codes to properly calculate tips.

To set up pay codes for tipped employees:

  1. Open the Pay Codes form (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change).

  2. Enter the Pay Code to edit.

  3. Scroll down to the Processing Options panel.

  4. If appropriate, select one of these options:

  • Select Used to Pay Cash Wage for Tipped Employees if this pay code is used to pay cash wages for tipped employees.

  • Select Pay Codes Used to Pay Employees for Charged Tips to compensate an employee for charged tips.

  1. If this is a reported tip pay code, you can select Collect Tip Sales on Time Sheet to display the Tip Sales column next to this pay code column in Summary Time Sheet Entry. This is a memo field for recording the sale amount associated with the reported tip and is used for reporting purposes.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Sample Pay Codes

This pay code is used to pay tipped personnel a Minimum Cash Wage:

[Image: screenshot]

Security Best Practices

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Security_Best_Practices.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Security_Best_Practices.htm

Security Best Practices

System Security Features

You can use PrismHR security features to mitigate risk, including multi-factor authentication (MFA), direct deposit change notifications, password expiration and complexity settings, Internet Protocol (IP) whitelisting, and several audit reports.

Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA)

Enabling MFA offers an additional layer of security to verify a user’s identity and prevent unauthorized access to employee data. In addition to a username and password, MFA requires one more method of authentication to verify the user’s identity before login access is granted on a particular device. The system sends a generated code to the user’s email account or mobile device through SMS text messaging to further verify the user’s identity. If enabled, the MFA process triggers when it does not recognize the browser or device attempting to login, or if a set number of days have passed since the last MFA attempt.

In PrismHR, your organization can enable multi-factor authentication at the system, client, or user level. Enabling MFA at the system level affects all service provider, worksite manager, and worksite trusted advisor users. A separate option is also available for employees accessing Employee Portal (EP).

To enable MFA at the system level, use the Authentication Services form (Back Office > System Parameters > Action Menu > Authentication Services) in PrismHR:

[Image: screenshot]

Service Providers

MFA is enabled by default for all service provider users in PrismHR. For more information, please see Enabling Multi-Factor Authentication for Service Provider Users.

SMB Clients

MFA can be set up for all your clients or on an individual client basis. MFA is enabled automatically for all worksite managers and trusted advisors. All new managers and advisors will have MFA enabled by default.

Direct Deposit Notification

You can configure ClientSpace to notify employees when a change is made to their direct deposit information. These notifications can help worksite employees and service providers be more proactive in detecting and preventing fraud attempts.

Enforce Strong Passwords

A strong password is the foundation of system security. Strong passwords consist of at least six characters and are a combination of numbers, letters, and symbols. PrismHR passwords can be case-sensitive—both uppercase and lowercase letters are recommended.

Ensure your passwords for superusers and managers are sufficiently complex. You can encourage or even require your users to change passwords periodically. For help with passwords, use a password-generation resource such as strongpasswordgenerator.com and a password management solution such as lastpass.com.

In PrismHR, you can require the use of punctuation with passwords and set password expiration limits using the Authentication Services form (Back Office > System Parameters > Action Menu > Authentication Services).

Be Suspicious

Educate your users to be wary of emails with embedded links or attachments of any kind. While they may look legitimate, clicking a link or attachment can hand over access to your computer to hackers.

Phishing efforts often include requests for account credentials from what appears to be a co-worker. Once a hacker has access to your computer, all your stored passwords may be available.

Restrict User Access

In PrismHR, you can restrict individual user access to specific IP addresses or a range of addresses. This means that any device (computer, mobile phone, tablet) in your network must come from an approved IP address. By restricting user access, you limit the devices and networks with access to PrismHR, making it much more difficult for an outsider to gain entry to the system.

Audit Reports

PrismHR has robust reporting features that can help tip you off to suspicious activity. PrismHR strongly recommends that you run the following reports:

  • Employee ACH Information and ACH Journal

  • Checking Accounts and ACH Originators

  • Client Banking Information

  • Login History Report

  • Sensitive Information Changes Report

When you inspect your reports, look for an unusual number of changes, or for account numbers/transit numbers that are the same or very similar. Confirm that any changes are accurate. Fraudulent fund transfers are typically initiated and tested in small amounts just before a large sum is removed from the system.

Additionally, PrismHR recommends a review of all ACH audit records, ideally right before the ACH files are transmitted. This will confirm that nothing was altered during the business day. Even one change that was not authorized can indicate a vulnerability.

Report Instructions

PrismHR strongly recommends that you run these reports. To check that changes are legitimate, run the same report (Find All Changes to a File) with different parameters (below).

Employee ACH Information and ACH Journal

  1. Select System Administration > File > Audit Control Maintenance > File Audit Reports > Find Changes to a File Report.

  2. In the Audited Filename field, enter one of the following:

  • To run the report for employee ACH information, enter EMPLOYEE.ACH.

  • To run the report on the ACH journals, enter ACH.JOURNAL.

  1. Leave the Audited Record ID and Audited Fieldname fields blank.

  2. For the dates:

  • If running the report first thing in the morning, enter the previous day in the first Update date range field, and then enter T in the second field to indicate today’s date.

  • If running the report in the evening, enter T in both fields to see all changes made today.

  1. Leave Optional Company ID blank.

  2. Click F2-Create Report.

Carefully review any changes in numeric fields (account numbers, transit numbers, and so on). Check for:

  • Activity that occurs extremely late or extremely early in the day. This activity is suspicious.

  • Changes from direct deposit of funds to Green Dot Bank or American Express pre-paid cards. These modifications require extra scrutiny to ensure they are legitimate.

Checking Accounts and ACH Originators

  1. Select System > Report > Find Changes to a File.

  2. In the Audited Filename field, enter one of the following:

  • To run the report for checking accounts, enter CHECKING.ACCOUNTS.

  • To run the report on the ACH origination accounts, enter ACH.ORIGINATORS.

  1. Leave the Audited Record ID and Audited Fieldname fields blank.

  2. For the dates:

  • If running the report first thing in the morning, enter the previous day in the first Update date range field, and then enter T in the second field to indicate today’s date.

  • If running the report in the evening, enter T in both fields to see all changes made today.

  1. Leave Optional Company ID blank.

  2. Click F2-Create Report.

  3. Make sure the bank account numbers are correct. These bank account numbers are where transfers would go for refunds, payments to the originators, and so on.

Client Banking Information

  1. Select System Administration > File > Audit Control Maintenance > File Audit Reports > Find Changes to a File Report.

  2. In the Audited Filename field, enter CLIENT.MASTER.

  3. Leave the Audited Record ID field blank.

  4. In the Audited Fieldname field, enter one of the following:

  • To check your clients’ ABA/Routing Numbers, enter ABA.NUMBER.

  • To check your clients’ Bank Account Number, enter BANK.ACNO.

Note: Both of these numbers are maintained in the Client Details Billing tab in PrismHR.

  1. For the dates:

  • If running the report first thing in the morning, enter the previous day in the first Update date range field, and then enter T in the second field to indicate today’s date.

  • If running the report in the evening, enter T in both fields to see all changes made today.

  1. Leave Optional Company ID blank.

  2. Click F2-Create Report.

  3. Make sure that any changes are legitimate.

Login History Report

The Login History Report records login history information including the time, date, and IP address used for the login. This report is useful to identify any unusual IP addresses used to log in to a user’s account.

  1. In PrismHR, click Reports.

  2. From System|Report, select Login History Report. The Login History Report opens.

  3. You can use the following selection criteria and sort options to filter the report results:

Use this option To do this
Identifier ID Enter an identifier ID or click the link to select from a list.
Company ID Enter a company ID or click the link to select from a list.
Employee ID Enter an employee ID or click the link to select from a list.
User Database Select a user database: - Prism Users (HRP.USERS file) - Webservice Users (WEBSERVICE.URSERS file)
  • Prism Users (HRP.USERS file)
  • Webservice Users (WEBSERVICE.URSERS file) | Result | Select a result: - success - fail |
  • success
  • fail | Start Date on or after | Enter or select a date for the report to start. | | End Date on or after | Enter or select a date for the report to end. | | Primary Sort | Select one of the following sort options: - Date - Identifier - Result - Company - Employee |

  1. Click Run.
Sensitive Information Changes Report

In PrismHR, this report runs on the first calculation of a payroll. The process looks to see if any changes to sensitive employee information has changed since the last payroll run for the employee. This report is automatically generated.

[Image: screenshot]

Click View Report. The changes to sensitive employee information since the last payroll run and the report displays. In the following example, the ACH Transit Number of an employee’s ACH entry was changed to a popular routing number used for cash cards.

[Image: screenshot]

Create Processes

Ongoing vigilance is the best practice to minimize the vulnerability of your organization and your clients. To ensure the safety of system information, establish best practices for your company and work with a security expert to mitigate your overall security risk.

Time Off plans support a flat amount per hours worked time off accrual method that complies with state-mandated paid sick leave laws.

Setting Limits on Accruals

[Image: screenshot]

If the accruals should be limited to certain pay codes, enter Pay Codes for hourly accruals. If this field is left blank, the system will use all pay codes that are flagged as Pay Code is Used to Pay Productive Work.

Maximum Usage Limit Per Plan Year

With Display Payroll Warning when Maximum Usage Limit Exceeded enabled, if an employee uses more sick hours than allowed based on the Maximum Usage Limit Per Plan Year, a payroll calculation error occurs, “Time off hours exceeded the maximum for Employee ID <ID number>, Absence Code <code>, Time Off Accrual Schedule <accrual schedule>. Please correct problem and re-run initialization.”

Maximum hours for a detail time sheet entry

Entering an amount in the Maximum hours for a detail time sheet entry field prints this amount under the Paid Time Off section of the check stub. This amount appears as Sick Available for Use and is used when the employee can accrue more hours than they can use in the plan year.

[Image: screenshot]

Employee Time Off Plan Enrollment

Time Off Plan Start Date

When enrolling employees in the Employee Time Off Register, the Start Date must be entered carefully. The Flat Amount by Hours Worked accrual method will adjust automatically. If a past date is entered, on the first calculated payroll, hours will accrue retroactively for all payrolls since that date in the current register year. This will occur regardless of the Calculated Through Date entered during the enrollment.

In the following example, if payroll is processed and dated 6/30/2019, the employee will accrue sick time on all vouchers dated 4/8/19 to 6/30/19.

[Image: screenshot]

When enrolling employees in the plan retroactively, no adjustment is needed. On the first calculated payroll, PrismHR will calculate the total hours worked from the benefit start date of the current year to the payroll date and accrue in full. This calculation will occur with each payroll. Since this plan will automatically adjust, Time Off Register adjustments are not possible when using this method. Any adjustment made will be reversed on the next payroll, as the system will constantly calculate back to the benefit start date of the current year and accrue all hours.

If YTD Calculated hours are entered during the enrollment, no hours will accrue until the system calculates that the employee has exceeded the accrual that was entered based on hours paid.

Setting Up Scheduled Time Off Register Accruals

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Setting%20Up%20PTO%20Register%20Accrual%20Schedules_Prism.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Setting Up PTO Register Accrual Schedules_Prism.htm

Setting Up Scheduled Time Off Register Accruals

Scheduled accrual frequencies allow your organization to determine the setpoints at which the employee can accrue time off benefits. The starting point and basis for the calculations is the Benefit Start Date field on the Employee Time Off Register form, which you can define in a new or existing record.

The process is as follows:

  • Set up Time Off Register Codes

  • Create a new Accrual Register

  • If required, modify an existing register

How the Accrual Calculations Work

To understand how to use the system properly, follow the explanation and read over the tips and tricks at the end of this section.

Explanation

When you initialize a payroll batch, these are the steps the system performs to arrive at whether the employee has reached an accrual setpoint:

Step Action
1 The system defines the “calculation date” based on the On option associated with the Accrual Frequency. In this example, today’s date is 07-26-2018 and the pay date is 08-01-2018. The value in the On field is Pay Date. Therefore, the calculation date is 08-01-2018.
2 The system calculates the starting dates and places them in the schedule table. In this example, the starting dates are: - 05-01-2018 (3 months from Benefit Start Date) - 08-01-2018 (6 months from Benefit Start Date) - 02-01-2019 (12 months from Benefit Start Date)
  • 05-01-2018 (3 months from Benefit Start Date)
  • 08-01-2018 (6 months from Benefit Start Date)
  • 02-01-2019 (12 months from Benefit Start Date) If any additional scheduled accrual dates were defined, they would be calculated in the same manner.

So in this example, the table of scheduled accrual dates so far is:

05-01-2018

08-01-2018

02-01-2019

Step — Action

3 — If the last scheduled accrual date is prior to the calculation date, the system uses the Subsequent Interval (in Months) parameter to add scheduled accrual dates until the last scheduled accrual date is after the calculation date. Continuing with the example, the last parameter for building the accrual table is the Subsequent Interval (in Months) which is 12. Since the last scheduled accrual date falls long before the calculation date of 08-01-2018, the system adds 12 months to the last scheduled accrual date. Now the last accrual date (02-01-2019) is after the calculation date, so the system stops building the accrual table.

The accrual dates table now looks like this:

05-01-2018

08-01-2019

02-01-2019

02-01-2020

Step — Action

4 — Once the table is built, the system looks through the list of scheduled accrual dates to determine if an accrual should be triggered for the employee time off record being processed. The system compares the scheduled accrual dates to the value it knows from the last time it successfully accrued, known as the Accrued Thru Date, which is also displays on the Employee Time Off Register form.

Note: Note the following:

  • If the condition is false (either one of the questions is false) the system moves up one row to the preceding schedule date in the table.

  • If this condition is true (both the questions are true) the system is “triggered” and sets the new “accrued through date” value to the scheduled accrual date.

Continuing with this example, the last Accrued Thru Date on the Time Off Auto Register Enrollment form is 02-01-2018. Since the calculation date is 08-01-2018, the accrual would be triggered when the system encounters the 02-01-2019 scheduled accrual date.

Tips & Tricks

Remembering how the scheduled triggers work is key when you are setting up your schedule, the most important being that the subsequent interval is added to the last starting monthly interval. The following examples illustrate several different kinds of scheduled triggers in PrismHR.

  • If you want to set up an accrual schedule of once a quarter (every three months), your value in the Scheduled Triggers panel would be 3.

[Image: screenshot]

  • If you want to set up an accrual schedule such that the subsequent interval falls on the anniversary of the Benefit Start Date on the Employee Time Off Register form, your last starting monthly interval value should be 12 and the subsequent interval should be 12.

[Image: screenshot]

If you set up an accrual schedule such that the last starting monthly interval value should be 8 and the subsequent interval is 12, the subsequent scheduled accrual dates will fall on the anniversary of the 8th month, not the Benefit Start Date on the Employee Time Off Register form.

[Image: screenshot]

Remembering the type of accrual frequency date is also important. If you are not aware of the value of the On field, you might become confused as to why an accrual did not “come through” when you expected:

  • Today’s date is 07-26-2018. The value of the On field is Period End Date. The period end date 07-31-2018, but the pay date is defined as 08-01-2018.

  • The system defines the “calculation date” based on the On option. The calculation date is 07-31-2018.

  • The Benefit Start Date is 02-01-2018. Your values in the Scheduled Triggers panel are 6 and 6, so the next scheduled accrual date is 08-01-2018.

  • It is easy to assume that the paycheck dated 08-01-2018 would show an accrual, since it coincides with a scheduled accrual date. However, an accrual would not be included. Since the calculation date is 07-31-2018, it is less than the next scheduled accrual date. The employee has not accrued anything as of the last day in July.

  • The system will recognize the accrual on the next paycheck when the calculated date is greater than or equal to the scheduled accrual date.

Setting Up Time Off Accrual Schedules

Time Off accrual schedules must be set up first in order to create a new accrual record.

To create a new accrual schedule, perform the following steps:

  1. Select Time Off Accrual Schedules under Client|Change. The Time Off Accrual Schedules form opens. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select the appropriate code in the Schedule Code field.

  2. Select the appropriate Accrual Frequency.

  3. If you selected Scheduled for an Accrual Frequency, perform the following step:

  1. — The system displays the Scheduled Triggers panel where you can define the accrual pattern or schedule. The system creates a table based on this form. It calculates dates starting with the Benefit Start Date on the Employee Time Off Register form, adding the number of months in the Starting Monthly Intervals column to the Benefit Start Date, then the value of the Subsequent Interval (in Months) field.

[Image: screenshot]

b. Enter the appropriate intervals in the Starting Monthly Intervals column.
c. Enter the recurring intervals in the Subsequent Interval (in Months) field.
— —
  1. Select the date type through which the accrual calculation is triggered in the On field. (The options vary based on the selected Accrual Frequency.)

Note: If you leave the On field blank, the system assumes the value is Pay Date. When you initialize the next payroll batch, the system defines the calculation date based on the On option.

  1. Click Save.

More Information

For information about creating the employee Time Off register and modifying an existing employee Time Off register, refer to the Time Off User Guide.

Setting a Percentage of Gross Pay to Non-Taxable

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Setting_Percentage_Gross_Pay_Non_Taxable.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Setting_Percentage_Gross_Pay_Non_Taxable.htm

Setting a Percentage of Gross Pay to Non-Taxable

You can set up a pay code, a shift, and the client to have the system deduct a percentage of an employee’s gross pay and move that amount to a non-taxable pay code. This process ties the pay code to a shift code, but does not lower the employee’s gross pay.

For example, let’s say the gross pay were $1000.00 and you wanted to reduce it by 20%. That lowers the gross pay to $800.00. The difference of $200.00 can be assigned to a non-taxable pay code.

To set up PrismHR to support this, you must first create a pay code with specific settings:

  1. Open the Pay Codes form (found in the Back Office menu [Image: screenshot] under System|Change).

  2. Create a pay code with these settings (as in Figure 151):

  • The Calculation Method is Per Diem.

  • The Per Diem Method is Percentage.

  • The Per Diem Taxable Pay Code is Regular.

  • The Per Diem Non-Taxable Pay Code is Expense Reimbursement.

Show me an example of the Pay Code settings:

Figure 151: Pay Code settings example

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Next, set up a shift for the client:

  1. Open the Shifts form (found in the Client menu under Client|Change).

  2. In addition to the settings you require, add the following:

  • For the Differential Method, select Percentage .

  • Enable the Include in Pay Rate option.

  • Enter the Per Diem Percent.

  1. Click Save.

Figure 152 shows the settings required for the preceding example.

Next:

Setting Up ACH Deposits with Cachet Financial Services

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Setting_Up_NatPay_Prism.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Setting_Up_NatPay_Prism.htm

Setting Up ACH Deposits with Cachet Financial Services

This article describes the process of setting up an ACH Originator in PrismHR for Cachet.

This process includes:

  • Setting up Cachet as your ACH Originator

  • Creating a bank account for the client

  • Creating/adjusting your existing payroll checking account

  • Assigning the checking account to client companies

Setting Up Cachet as Your ACH Originator

In order to set up Cachet as an ACH Originator, you must first confirm that consolidated ACH file processing is enabled.

To enable consolidated ACH file processing, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office. Select System Parameters under System|Change. The System Parameters form opens.

  2. In the Consolidated ACH File Processing Used field, select Yes, if it is not already selected.

Once this setting has been confirmed, begin the process of setting up ACH Originators.

  1. Click Back Office. Select ACH Originators under System|Change. The ACH Originators form opens.

[Image: ACH Originators fields]

  1. Enter the appropriate ACH File Originator information for Cachet by completing the following fields. See Using ACH Originators for information about the other fields on this form.

Field — Description

Processor ID — Enter or select the financial institution. The PrismHR Customer Support Team recommends that you use easy-to-remember abbreviations for the processor IDs. (Note: Do not enter spaces in this field.)

Processing Bank ID — Enter or select the bank routing number for your bank.

Processing Company ID — Enter your Federal IRS Number (FEIN), beginning with the number 1. For example, if your FEIN number is “240000054,” then enter 1240000054.

Processing Company Name — Enter your company name as the PEO.

ACH File Options — Select the Put Company ID on ACH file detail lines position 40-54 option.

Third Party Format — Select Cachet.

  1. In the Cachet panel, do the following:

[Image: screenshot]

Field Description
Generate Cachet ACH File Format Select this field.
Cachet Funding Transaction Days Prior To Pay Date Enter 1 in this field.

Note: Note: Do not enter other information or select other fields in this panel.

  1. Click Save.

Creating a Bank Account for the Client

To continue the ACH setup process, you must create a bank account for each client. For example, if there are 100 clients, then you need to create 100 client bank accounts.

To create a client bank account, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office. Select Bank Accounts under System|Change. The Bank Accounts form displays.

[Image: Bank Accounts form]

Note: Note: Enter the same information as found on the Billing tab in the Client|Change > Client Details form, specifically in the Electronic Billing panel.

  1. Complete the following fields to continue the bank account setup for each client you want to include in the Cachet file. See Maintaining Bank Accounts for information about the other fields.

Field — Description

Account ID — Enter or select your checking number, which must be an integer.

Reference — Enter the client’s name.

Bank Name/Bank Address — Enter the bank’s name and address that the client uses.

Offset Account ID — Enter or select the ID of your bank where you deposit or withdraw funds.

Rebate Bill Code — Enter NETPAY.

Employer ID — Enter or select the employer ID, which is the same ID on the Client Details record for that client, or enter the employer ID on your bank account, if the client is assigned a bank account with a different employer.

  1. In the ACH Setup panel, enter the following information:

[Image: ACH Setup fields]

Field Description
ACH Processor ID Enter or select the same ACH Processor ID that you created earlier.
Company Name (Batch Header) Enter the client’s company name.
  1. Click Save.

Creating/Adjusting Your Existing Payroll Checking Account

Cachet requires two checking accounts to perform ACH processing: one account for direct deposits to employees, and a second account for withdrawals from your client.

Note: Note: These steps may vary depending upon your payroll checking account set up. Please log in to the Customer Resource Center and click Request Customer Support, if you have any questions. A support representative will contact you as soon as possible.

Making Adjustments to Your Checking Account

If you have an established checking account for payroll, and you want to have Cachet process your ACH, then you need to input an Offset Account ID in the Bank Accounts form and change the ACH Processor ID to Cachet in the ACH Setup panel.

To make adjustments to your bank account, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office. Select Bank Accounts under System|Change. The Bank Accounts form opens.

  2. In your bank account, in the Offset Account ID field, enter or select the ID of the account you set up earlier.

  3. In the ACH Setup panel, enter the following information:

Field Description
ACH Processor ID Enter or select the ACH Processor ID that you created earlier.
Company Name (Batch Header) Enter your company name.
  1. Click Save.

Creating a New Checking Account for Client Withdrawals

To create a new checking account for client withdrawals, do the following:

  1. Click Back Office. Select Bank Accounts under System|Change. The Bank Accounts form opens.

  2. Enter the new Account ID and the same bank information as your established checking account.

  3. In the ACH Setup panel, enter the following information:

Field Description
ACH Processor ID Enter or select the ACH Processor ID that you created earlier.
Company Name (Batch Header) Enter your company name.
  1. Click Save.

Note: Note: If you do not have an account established for payroll, then you need to create a new checking account for employee deposits and employee withdrawals.

Assigning the Checking Account to Client Companies

To assign the checking account to client companies, do the following:

  1. In the Client menu, select Client Details (under Client|Change). The Client Details form opens.

  2. Select the Account tab.

  3. In the Bank Accounts panel, enter the following information:

Field — Description

Direct Deposit Account — Assign the Bank Account that you created earlier.

Receipts Account — Enter your Bank Account where invoice payments and other payroll transactions related to this client will be processed.

  1. Click Save.

Performing Standard Pay Imports

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Standard_Pay_Imports.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Standard_Pay_Imports.htm

Performing Standard Pay Imports

Importing pay data from time keeping systems typically requires a combination of the methods described (usually methods 2 and 3) due to the various levels of information being imported.

You will define the parameters related to the import, then decide how you will map the time keeping system codes to PrismHR codes by:

  • Defining the parameters of import

  • Mapping foreign codes to existing PrismHR pay codes

  • Defining which data to import from the data file

Pay Import Definition

Use the Pay Import Definition form to define the parameters of a specific import scenario. Once set up, the Import Definition can be used repeatedly providing the import scenario does not change.

To define the parameters:

  1. Open Pay Import Definition (found in the Back Office menu under System|Change).

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Definition ID and Description used for this import definition.

  2. Select Hourly Only to only allow hourly employees to be loaded from the download.

  3. Select the File Format and Delimiter used to delimit the import record fields.

  4. If necessary, enter a value for Number of Header Lines to Skip. This setting skips any header content lines in the import file, and begins the import after that. For example, if the first line in the file has the column headings, and the second line is the first record, enter 1.

  5. Select Rates Have Four Decimal Places if the pay rates have four decimal places.

  6. Select Allow Zero Pay on Time Sheets if zero amounts should be passed to time sheets.

Note: Note: Do not use the Special Processing Routine fields unless directed to do so by the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

  1. Enter the Special Processing Routine used to convert this import file.

  2. Enter the Special Routine after Import to execute immediately after importing the file.

  3. Enter the Post Processing Routine to execute at the end of the import process.

Note: Note: You must select All Special Routines Using Message Format if this is a web-based import. If this parameter is not selected, do not select it unless directed to do so by the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

  1. Select All Special Routines Using Message Format if the system uses a routine to pass back status and message information.

  2. Select Show Original File Data in Error Report if the error report should show the complete line of data from the original file.

  3. From the Use STD Allocation On drop-down, select the standard allocation to use for time.

In the When Posting Imported Data to Time Sheets panel:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. From the Clear Existing Pay From Time Sheets for drop-down, select how to manage existing information:

  • All Employees: Clears all pay information for all employees in a batch when the file imports.

  • Employees in Import File Only: Employee data in the import file overwrites any existing data only for those employees. If other employee data exists in the payroll batch, their data is preserved.

  • None: No data is cleared.

Note: Note: If you chose to clear existing pay (All Employees or Employees in Import File Only), you can only select Overwrite existing amounts from the Update existing pay on time sheets drop-down. If you chose not to clear existing data (None), then you can choose a method for how to handle existing pay.

  1. From the Update existing pay on time sheets drop-down, select whether the imported data overwrites any existing pay data or adds to it.

  2. Select Auto Add Option (when employee is in file import but not on payroll) to have the system automatically add active employee time sheets.

  • Auto Add Active Employees to Payroll: The system automatically adds the time sheets for all active employees. There are some instances where employees will not be added:

  • The employee’s onboarding is still in progress.

  • It is a regular or special batch and the employee’s pay group is not included.

  • It is a manual batch.

  • Auto Add All Employees to Payroll: The system automatically adds all employees to the time sheet. This affects employees who were terminated and re-hired. They need to be paid but have not yet been re-hired in the system. They must also be assigned to the pay group in the import.

  • Do Not Auto Add: The system will not automatically add active employees.

  1. Select Recalculate Overtime Pay Rate to have the system recalculate overtime pay rates (based on the rate multiplier) on imported overtime hours that use the definition.

For example, an employee is included in the import with a pay rate of $10. If this option is not selected, then the employee will be paid $10. If this option is selected, then the employee will be paid at the overtime rate (such as $15 for a 1.5 multiplier).

  1. Click Save.

The first method describes the process of setting up pay data import information by “mapping” pay codes from a time keeping system to existing pay codes in PrismHR.

Note: Note: The data in the following table will be used as an example to set up the Import Field Definitions and the Import Translation Maintenance screens.

The second method involves importing pay data that has been prepared to match the client’s existing time sheet column headings.

The Import Field Definitions correspond to the Pay Code Columns 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 7 on your Company Master Maintenance SF6 - Headings tab, as shown below.

Note: Note: Pay Code Columns 6 and 8 are not used.

[Image: screenshot]

To create an import definition for Method 2:

  1. Open Client Details (found in the Client menu under Client|Change). Select the Time Sheet tab to open the Client Time Sheet form.

  2. Fill in the fields on the Time Sheet Pay Data Import Definition form with the values as described in “Standard Pay Imports”.

  3. Enter the information into the Import Field Definitions table. This information defines which fields of the external file will be imported into PrismHR and how the data will be treated.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: The values 1,2,3, etc., associated with the Designates Amount Paid #and Designates Hours Worked or Units Paid # Field Names as shown in Figure 4 correspond with the type of pay data in the Pay Code Column Headings in the Client Details form.

Note: Note: Any field that is missing will be skipped.

Note: Note: The Convert column is used when data is presented in a non-native format. For example, if 80 hours was listed as 8000 with no decimal, enter MR2 (masked right with two decimal places) in the Convert column. Contact F. W. Davison & Company for other Convert codes that are available.

  1. Leave the Conversion Mask column blank. For Method 2, no translation is needed since the pay codes are controlled by the Client Details form.

WARNING: A change in the Pay Code Column Headings on the Company Master Maintenance record will impact Method 2.

  1. Click Save.

Standard Pay Imports - Method 3

The third method involves importing more pay codes than are defined in the Client Details form. This method can also be used for importing pay codes from time clock systems that create one record per employee.

If a pay code column is not available on the existing time sheet (as defined on the Client Details form) or you do not want to use those columns, you can number each pay code column using a range of 10 - 20 as shown below and import the unsupported pay codes.

[Image: screenshot]

To create an import definition for Method 3:

  1. Select System|Change|Pay Import Definition. The Pay Import Definition form opens.

  2. Fill in the fields on the Pay Import Definition form with the values as described in the section above.

  3. Enter the Foreign Code (C10* - C20*) and the Prism Code for each pay code to be converted.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: Foreign Code Columns are entered as C10* - C20* in the Import Translation Maintenance panel as values C10* - C11* in the Foreign Code column shown below represent the data columns that will be imported from the time clock system as shown in Figure 6.

  1. Leave the PTO Type Flag column blank.

  2. Enter the Field # and the Field Name for each pay code to be converted.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Field Name Definitions

The following table provides a description of the codes used in the Field Name column in the Import Field Definitions panel.

CODE F3 HELP DESCRIPTION COMMENTS
$DATE Date worked or Period End Date Optional: Used if the import supports multiple work days, where the day of work if significant for Overtime calculations or Certified Payroll. This field otherwise indicates the ‘Period End’ if all data are sent in summary. Note that the date entered here must correspond with the date of the batch open, otherwise the data will not be ‘seen’. If not supplied, works as standard Prism with all time accrued on the last Work Day.

$HOURSPOS Must be used with $CODEPOS See method 1. Shows hours paid. If no $HRSWRKPOS, then also show hours worked.
$AMOUNTPOS Must be used with $CODEPOS. See Method 1. Shows a flat amount payment.
$VARPOS Auto decide if hours or amount by code in CODEPOS. See Method 1.
$HRSWRKPOS Hours worked (used with $CODEPOS). See Method 1. Shows hours worked. Must be used with an $HOURSPOS or $VARPOS.
$CODE1 Designates pay code 1 (detail only). See Method 2. Each $CODEX column of Method 1 relates back to the matching pay code set up on the Company Master Maintenance screen. See Note 1 below this table.

Note: Note: $CODE1 - $CODE5 are not typically used since the position sets the code. Use of these codes will force a “Detail” time sheet line to be built.

Note: Note: Each mapped column can only have either $AMOUNTXX or $HOURSXX, but not both. For example, JURY must be $HOURSXX, while BONUS must be $AMOUNTXX. Codes 10 -20 allow pay data columns to be imported that are not part of the standard Company setup, and if all used, represent support for more than 15 standard types of pay which has thus far handled the widest import spreadsheets presented.

Creating Custom Menu Items

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/SysAdmin/Creating_Custom_Menu.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/SysAdmin/Creating_Custom_Menu.htm

Creating Custom Menu Items

You can define custom menu items in PrismHR. As part of this process, you must assign the user to a role that grants access to the Custom Process form.

To define custom menu items, perform these steps in the order defined in this table.

To — Perform this action

Define the SSO Service Note: If the SSO Service is already defined, skip to the next step. — 1. Click Back Office. 2. From System|Change, select System Parameters. 3. Select SSO Services from the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] . [Image: screenshot] 4. Click + to add a new row. 5. Enter the remaining fields as required: - Service ID: Unique identifier for the service. - Description: Description of the service. - Type: Service type is usually External for a vendor and Internal for services hosted on your company’s servers. Internal opens the target service in an inline frame. External opens the target service in a separate window or tab (dependent on browser settings). - Service URL: Location of the product.

Create the Custom Process Create a Custom Process to make the SSO service a menu item that is available to add to user roles. — 1. Click Back Office. 2. From System|Change, select System Parameters. 3. Select Custom Process from the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] . 4. Fill in these fields as required. - Process ID: Enter a unique process ID. - Name: Process name that displays in menu and search. - Category: Menu where the custom process belongs. - Sub-Category: Indicates where the custom process belongs within the selected menu. If you select Other, you can enter a custom sub-category. - Disabled- Suppresses the display of custom processes. - Display in Menus: Displays the custom process in the menu to users who have access to it. - Display in Search: Displays the custom process to users who have access to it and use the search field to find it. - Disable Resizing: Enable to prevent the custom process from adjusting its size based on the browser window. - Role Specific: If the menu item is limited to a specific user role, select Service Provider or Worksite Manager as appropriate. If the menu item should be available to user roles of either type, leave this drop-down set to Select. - Version Type: For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR. - SSO Service: Select a service type. For example, Onboarding. - SSO Action: Leave the SSO Action field blank for tandem SSO. Only enter an SSO Action if you know the specific value that should go in this field for a legacy SSO service. - Use SSO Init: Options are SSO, which uses the legacy SSO code. This is used only for products developed a while ago. The other option is Tandem SSO, which uses the SSO methods in the PrismHR Services API. This is the typical selection. - External App: indicates whether this was defined as an external application in SSO Services. You cannot edit it here. - Parameters: For future development. Does not currently impact the functionality of PrismHR.

Add the Process to a User Role Once a custom process exists, add it to the appropriate user roles so users can see it. (see Defining User Roles for more information.) — 1. Click Back Office. 2. From System|Change, select User Roles. 3. Enter a Role ID to edit a role. 4. Click Add. The User Role Maintenanceform opens. 5. Locate the custom process in the list and check the Select check box. 6. Click Accept to return to User Roles. The next time a user assigned that role logs into the system, they will have access to that form as indicated in Custom Process (in the menu, the search, or both).
