Configuring Document Upload for High-Availability Web Servers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/SysAdmin/Doc_Upload_Config_for_HA_Servers.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/SysAdmin/Doc_Upload_Config_for_HA_Servers.htm

Configuring Document Upload for High-Availability Web Servers

This procedure applies only to on-premise organizations.

Database administrators who need to support multiple web servers for a high-avaibility (HA) configuration can create a DOC.CONFIG record in GLOBAL.PARAMS for each service user.

  1. In the central database location, copy the main DOC.CONFIG record.

  2. Append .username to the new record (for example, DOC.CONFIG.userbob). The username is the one used to connect from the web server to the database server

  3. Make any necessary edits to the configuration record (for example, the WEB.IP configuration).

When the specified user connects, the system uses the configuration information from their record. If the user has no custom configuration record, the system uses the default DOC.CONFIG record’s information.

[Image: screenshot]

========================================================================

Granting User Access to Former Clients and Employees

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/SysAdmin/Grant_Client_Access_to_Former_EE.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/SysAdmin/Grant_Client_Access_to_Former_EE.htm

Granting User Access to Former Clients and Employees

Grant users temporary access to a client, such as when a manager might need access for a terminated client to get their information.

To grant temporary access to former clients and employees:

  1. Access the Users form on the System|Change menu.

  2. Enter the employee’s User ID. The information populates. In the example of a recently terminated employee, the access to the client is Denied. [Image: screenshot]

  3. Select Data Security from the Actions menu. The Data Security form opens.

  4. Click the Access link (top of form). The Optional Override Access Thru dialog box displays.

  5. Enter the date through which the user can access the client. User access will be denied after 11:59:59 p.m. (Central Time) on that date. [Image: screenshot]

  6. Click Submit. The Data Security form displays the user’s temporary access.

  7. Click Save. The User form now displays the user’s Current Access displays the new setting.

  8. Click Save again.

========================================================================

Importing Tax Adjustments

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Tax%20Adjustment%20Import.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Tax Adjustment Import.htm

Importing Tax Adjustments

Tax registers contain a running total of amounts deducted from employee paychecks. You can process tax adjustments when required, such as when an employee has an incorrectly assigned withholding tax.

Note: Note: Proceed carefully with the tax adjustment process. When you process a tax register adjustment, you are changing the only record of taxes withheld for employees in the system.

This procedure describes how to import an Employee or Payroll Tax Adjustment into PrismHR. Information is also provided to view the appropriate voucher once the tax adjustment import has occurred. The steps involved are:

  • Creating a Spreadsheet

  • Importing the Tax Adjustment

  • Verifying Imported Tax Information

Creating a Spreadsheet

To create this spreadsheet:

  1. Create an Excel Spreadsheet with this tax import information. Remove any commas or headers in your data to avoid tax adjustment import problems. | Column | Information | | — | — | | Col A | Company ID | | Col B | Employee ID | | Col C | Adjustment Date (MM/DD/YYYY) | | Col D | Employer ID | | Col E | Adjust Client Tax Register? (Y/N) | | Col F | Tax Code | | Col G | Exempt Pay | | Col H | Sheltered Earnings | | Col I | Gross Taxable | | Col J | Over Limit | | Col K | Tax Amount | | Col L | Suppress Billing? (Y/N) | | Col M | School District |
  2. Save the Excel file as a tab-delimited text file.
  3. Record the file name and where it was saved.
  4. Proceed with Importing Tax Adjustments.

Importing Tax Adjustments

To import tax adjustment information into PrismHR:

  1. Click Payroll.
  2. From Payroll|Change, select Tax Adjustment Import. The system displays the Tax Adjustment Import form. [Image: screenshot]
  3. Click Upload File. A file upload dialog box displays.
  4. Click Choose File and navigate to the tab-delimited .txt file you created.
  5. Highlight the file name and click Open, then click submit. The system displays whether the file uploaded successfully and how long the upload took.
  6. Click Close. The contents of the file displays in the form.
  7. Click Import. If there are errors in the import file, the system displays what the error is. For example, an invalid client ID generates this error. [Image: screenshot]
  8. Fix any errors in the import file and save it again as a tab-delimited text file.
  9. Retry the import if required.

Verifying Tax Adjustment Information

To view the tax adjustment information imported into PrismHR:

  1. Open the Employee Payroll Vouchers screen (found under Payroll|View).
  2. Enter the appropriate Employee ID.
  3. Select the appropriate Year. A list of employee vouchers is generated.
  4. Select the appropriate Voucher Number. The system displays the Payroll Voucher dialog.
  5. Click the Tax tab.
  6. Locate the tax adjustments under the appropriate panels (for example, Employer Tax).
  7. Click Close.

Task Notes

If a tax adjustment creates a refund, the system generates a credit voucher but does not generate a check. To print a check, follow the instructions in Payroll Reports

Note: In cases where checks are not created, TAXREF is listed on the voucher.

After import, accounting is performed on the adjustment voucher.

Transferring an Employee to Another Client

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Transfer_Employee_PrismHR.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Transfer_Employee_PrismHR.htm

Transferring an Employee to Another Client

You can move employee records and accompanying check information to a different client using the Transfer Employee to New Client form.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

Before You Begin

Before you begin the transfer process, run a gross earnings report for reference.

  1. Open the Employee Details form (located in the HR menu under HR|Change).

  2. After selecting the appropriate employee ID, select Payroll Summary from the Actions menu.

Benefits Plan

Ensure that benefit plans are set up in the To Client if you want the employee to remain enrolled in that plan.

For example, the employee in the figure below is currently enrolled in ACA Not Reported Plan and Gwen’s Life Insurance. These plans would need to be set up in the To Client in order for the employee to participate.

[Image: screenshot]

Time Off Plans

In addition to benefit plans, any Time Off plans that the employee is currently enrolled in would also need to be set up in the To Company. In the example below, employee M14437 is set up for VAC – VAC Time Off plan.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Time Off Plan balances do not transfer, so you need to make adjustments for the balances.

Running the Process

To move records:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From Operations|Action, select Transfer Employee to New Client.

  3. Complete these fields as required:

Field Description
From Client/To Client From Client: Click the link and select the client ID for the client that the employee is transferring from. To Client: Click the link and select the client ID for the client that the employee is transferring to. > Note: Note: To ensure that an employee remains enrolled in a benefit plan after transfer. benefit plans must be set up in the To Client.
Employee/To Employee Employee: Click the link and select the ID of the employee whose information requires a transfer. To Employee: Displays the employee ID for the company where the employee is being transferred.
Employer/To Employer Employer: Displays the employer ID assigned to the current client. To Employer: Displays the employer ID assigned to the new client where the employee is being transferred. Observe the following behavior that occurs during these transfer scenarios: - If the new client and the current (old) client have different primary employer IDs and the same Service Type of Professional Employer (PEO), you can perform a valid transfer of employees between those clients by selecting the employee’s new employer ID, which the system updates in the employee record under the new client. In addition, if you select the non-primary employer ID in the above scenario, a transfer alert displays, “The primary employer for Client <new/to client ID> is <primary employer ID>. You will receive payroll calculation errors for this employee if Employer <selected Employer ID that is non-primary> is not set up as an alternate employer in this client.” - If the new client and the current client have different primary employer IDs and different Service Types, such as PEO and ASO, or they both have the same ASO Service Type, when you enter the full employee ID or paste the employee ID in the Employee field, you can only select a valid (primary) employer ID. Also, after you make the selection, the To Employee field displays “New Employee will be created.”
  • If the new client and the current (old) client have different primary employer IDs and the same Service Type of Professional Employer (PEO), you can perform a valid transfer of employees between those clients by selecting the employee’s new employer ID, which the system updates in the employee record under the new client.
  • If the new client and the current client have different primary employer IDs and different Service Types, such as PEO and ASO, or they both have the same ASO Service Type, when you enter the full employee ID or paste the employee ID in the Employee field, you can only select a valid (primary) employer ID. Also, after you make the selection, the To Employee field displays “New Employee will be created.” | Pay Group/To Pay Group | Pay Group: Displays the employee’s current pay group. To Pay Group: Select a pay group for the destination client. The search contains only valid pay groups for the destination client. | | Current Retire Plan/To Retire Plan | Current Retire Plan: Displays the currently enrolled retirement plan. To Retire Plan: Displays the retirement plan where the employee is being transferred. | | Current Position/To Position | Current Position: Displays the employee’s current position. To Position: Select or enter a valid position for the destination client. (If you select this field, the Select Position list that displays contains only valid positions for the destination client.) | | Last Hire Date | After selecting an employee associated with the From Client selection, the employee’s last hire date displays in this field. | | To Location | Select or enter a valid location for the destination client. (If you select this field, the Select Location Code list that displays contains only valid locations for the destination client.) | | To Benefit Group | Select or enter a valid benefit group for the destination client. (If you select this field, the Select Benefit Groups list that displays contains only valid benefit groups for the destination client.) | | To Client Hire Date | Enter or select the hire date to use for the company where the employee is being transferred. After entering or selecting a date in this field, a dialog containing that date displays. Select Yes to confirm that date should be used to update the Status, Type, Status Type History, Position History, and Pay History. If you select No, the system updates that information using the most recent date for that record in the From Client employee record after the transfer finishes. Note: If you re-enter a new date in this field after selecting an option in this dialog, the dialog displays again. After you select an option in the dialog, the following occurs: - Yes: A transfer alert message displays: “Employee Status, Type, Status Type History, Position History and Pay History dates will be <date from To client hire date field>.” - No: A transfer alert message displays: “Employee Status, Type, Status Type History, Position History and Pay History dates will be the most recent date from Client <ID of the From Client>.” |
  • Yes: A transfer alert message displays: “Employee Status, Type, Status Type History, Position History and Pay History dates will be <date from To client hire date field>.”
  • No: A transfer alert message displays: “Employee Status, Type, Status Type History, Position History and Pay History dates will be the most recent date from Client <ID of the From Client>.” | Transfer Y-T-D Check Stubs | Transfers the year-to-date check stub details to the company the employee is transferring to. | | Terminate Employee in Original Client | Select this option only if you want to terminate the employee and their benefits in the original client. When you select this option, a mandatory date field displays, so you can accept the current date (default) or enter or select which date the transferring employee will be terminated in their old client. > Note: Note the following: - The date field only displays if you select the Terminate Employee in Original Client field. - The date field is mandatory, so you must either accept the current (default) date or select a date, which can be a future or past date. Otherwise, when you complete the other fields but leave this field blank, an error message displays when you click Transfer, “All mandatory fields must be filled in.” - If you enter a future date, the status and status date information do not update in the employee record until the termination date occurs. However, the status type history record immediately reflects the future-dated information. - When you select the Terminate Employee in Original Client field, a transfer alert displays, “Terminating the employee in the original client can cause a change to the employee’s Benefits Thru Date in the original client.” |
  • The date field only displays if you select the Terminate Employee in Original Client field.
  • The date field is mandatory, so you must either accept the current (default) date or select a date, which can be a future or past date. Otherwise, when you complete the other fields but leave this field blank, an error message displays when you click Transfer, “All mandatory fields must be filled in.”
  • If you enter a future date, the status and status date information do not update in the employee record until the termination date occurs. However, the status type history record immediately reflects the future-dated information.
  • When you select the Terminate Employee in Original Client field, a transfer alert displays, “Terminating the employee in the original client can cause a change to the employee’s Benefits Thru Date in the original client.” | Term Reason Code | Select a valid termination reason code (only those codes with “Termination” included in their Valid Actions on the Employee Termination form display) for the original client when transferring and terminating an employee in one action. Note: The Term Reason Code field only displays if you enable Terminate Employee in Original Client. | | Terminate Benefits Coverage Today | If you select the Terminate Employee in Original Client field and this field, the system forces the terminated group benefits to end coverage today. > Note: Note: You must select both fields for this update to take effect. | | Transfer Alerts | Displays messages describing what occurred during the transfer process. |
  1. Click Transfer.

Errors and Information

As you run the process, some or all of the following prompts might display.

  • Benefit Plans: If the benefit plan does exit in the To Company you will receive the following prompt, “The employee is enrolled in benefit plan <name> but the plan is not set up in the new company. This enrollment will not be copied to the new client if you transfer the employee.”

  • Job Codes: If the employee’s job codes do not exist in the To Company, the system prompts to create it by displaying the following prompt, “Job Code <name> does not exist for client <client ID>. Should the Job Code by copied from <client ID>?”

  • Retirement Plans: Select which retirement plan to enroll the employee in the To Company. For example, “This employee is enrolled in 401k plan NEW, which is not available in company 158. Which 401k plan do you want to use in company 158? 1 = CL401K, 2 = NR401K.”

Note: If only one plan is offered in the To Company, a different prompt displays. For example, “This employee is enrolled in 401k plan KRISTY, but company 283039 offers only plan NRPLAN. Do you want to enroll the employee in that plan?”

  • Change of Employer: If the To Company does not have the same employer as the From Company, you are prompted to assign the employee to the employer in the From Company. For example, “This employee currently works for employer 120 in company 000120. The primary employer for company 283039 is employer 606. Do you want to assign the employee to employer 606 in company 283039?”

  • Process Complete: The following prompt indicates the employee has been transferred to the To Company, “Employee has been transferred. Please note that the employee will need to be manually entered on the batch time sheet for any batch currently in progress.”

Note: You must manually add the employee to any batches currently in progress, or you could delete and recreate the batch for the system to automatically add.

  • Benefits Not Copied: A complete listing of benefit plans not created is provided, “Warning: The employee had some benefits that could not be copied to the new company because they are not valid there. Print out the following report and manually enroll the employee in equivalent plans under the new company.”

What Does Not Transfer

The following items do not transfer during this process:

  • Time Off plan balances. (You must set up the Time Off plan in the To Company and complete adjustments for the balances.)

  • Enrollments in FSA Plans.

  • Garnishment Payment History.

Which Transfers Need Confirmation

The original shift code, division code, department code, project code, and benefit group will transfer from the old record.

Note: If these codes do not exist in the new company, they can be adjusted on the Employee Details form or added to the list of valid codes. If you have already added the employee to the timesheet, do not forget to adjust these codes on the timesheet as well.

Verify the Information

When the process is complete, verify the changes on the Name tab in the Employee Details form.

From Company

Verify that the employee is terminated under the From Company.

To Company

Verify that the employee information is complete and active in the To Company.

Note: If a date was entered during the transfer process, the Last Hire Date field displays that date. If a date was not entered during the transfer process, the Last Hire Date field displays the hire date entered by the From Company.

YTD Check Details

On the first processed payroll, verify that the YTD information on the check stub is correct and represents the information prior to running the transfer employee process.

Setting Up PEO Client Accounting

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/TS-PEO_Client_Accounting.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/TS-PEO_Client_Accounting.htm

Setting Up PEO Client Accounting

PrismHR lets you create an accounting download file and report for your client that breaks out invoice numbers and uses the client’s own account numbers.

You must perform four major tasks to create a report and download file:

  • Define the global or client accounting templates.
  • Create the accounting download directory. (Note: These instructions are for On-Premise customers only.)
  • Enable the client accounting for a client company.
  • Create the download file.

Understanding Accounting Templates

Using General Ledger Account Numbers

The client accounting templates associate your company’s payroll and billing codes with the client’s general ledger account numbers. These associations enable PrismHR to build an accounting download file and a report with data created during payroll processing.

A client company may account for different types of earnings, deductions that have the Rebate bill code, and employer-paid taxes—or other items that are not included in payroll checks, such as delivery fees or report fees. PrismHR allows you to create an accounting entry per bill code. PrismHR also allows you to use “wildcards” that will be swapped out for the appropriate number at the time of payroll initialization; for example, your client charges REG (Regular) pay to account 5101.EEDP and OT (Overtime) pay to account 5105.EEDP. (The wildcard EEDP stands for Employee’s Department.) Wildcards and their usage are explained in “Defining Accounting Templates.”

The format of the client account codes usually depends on the company’s accounting system. PrismHR can create an accounting download file in one of the following formats:

  • A generic comma-delimited file

  • A file that can be easily imported directly into PeachTree, QuickBooks, or Great Plains

Note: Note: If you want to create a QuickBooks Billing template, contact PrismHR HR Support to submit a Professional Service engagement/request.

  • A custom layout

There are a few ways to enter client account numbers so that client accounting is generated:

  • Global Accounting Template alone.

  • Global Accounting Template with client-level overrides.

  • Global Accounting Template with invoice-format overrides (Note: If you want to use an account at the billing format, that number is referenced from the Bill Codes in the Company Account field on the Billing Formats form.)

  • Client Accounting Template alone.

Using Client Bill Format Account Number Overrides

One feature in PrismHR that is available—but not likely to be used—is replacing account numbers on the global or client account template with account numbers on the invoice format. If the invoice format contains summarized items, you can replace the account number on the invoice format with the client accounting template account numbers.

You can use this feature to make sure the client’s accounting detail is no more than the detail provided on the invoice. This same control is achieved on the client accounting setup by making sure items grouped together for invoicing all go to the same client account number.

For example, the client company uses a bundled accounting format. You do not want your 005 (Comp), 004 (Taxes), and 006 (Admin Fee) billing codes to charge different client accounting codes. If you did, the client would essentially receive a breakdown of their bundled rate.

Because it is common for many different clients to share a bill format, this option is usually not the best choice. It offers little more than what you could accomplish by carefully designing the client-level and/or global templates. It also makes the template impossible to share.

Defining Accounting Templates and Using Wildcards

Defining the Global Client Accounting Template

PEOs can set up client accounting in the following ways:

  • By Client Template (PEO Client Account form): Usually, PEOs produce client accounting reports and download files using account numbers and formats specific to each client company.

  • By Global Account Template: In a few cases, a PEO might create global account numbers; for example, the PEO might have a group of clients that are defined separately but feed a common accounting system (for example, a franchise operation). The global template allows different clients to share the same accounting setup, where only varying accounts would be set up on the individual clients.

WARNING: The client accounting templates that prepare downloads must work correctly. Make sure you understand the format requirements before making changes.

To define the global PEO client accounting template:

  1. Access the Global PEO Client Accounting Templates form in the System|Change menu.

  2. Complete the following:

Field Name Description
Accounting Template ID - To create a new global client accounting template that can be shared by multiple clients, enter the name of global Accounting Template here. - To change a global client existing accounting template, enter the client accounting template ID or click the Accounting Template ID link and select the one that you want to change from the Select Global Accounting Template dialog box.
  • To create a new global client accounting template that can be shared by multiple clients, enter the name of global Accounting Template here.
  • To change a global client existing accounting template, enter the client accounting template ID or click the Accounting Template ID link and select the one that you want to change from the Select Global Accounting Template dialog box. | Template Description | Enter the description of the global client accounting template entered above. > Note: Note: For an existing global client accounting template ID, this field will auto-populate. You can edit the template description here. | | Client Accounting System | Select the client accounting system to be used for this global client accounting template from the following options: - Generic - this is the default option. - PeachTree - QuickBooks - QuickBooks Billing - Great Plains - Custom > Note: Note: For an existing global client accounting template, this field will auto-populate. You can edit the client accounting system here. |
  • Generic - this is the default option.
  • PeachTree
  • QuickBooks
  • QuickBooks Billing
  • Great Plains
  • Custom | Match Format | If present, this will test the entries to ensure they follow the format. Enter a format to ensure that the G/L Account numbers entered into each code section matches the format in this field. The options you can use in this field are N, A, or X, where N is a number, A is an alphabetic character, and X is a wildcard, which means you can use anything. A zero in front of the X (that is, 0X) indicates that section of the code can be any length and any character. (An error displays, “This entry does not match the mask pattern!”, if the Acct Format entered does not adhere to the Match Format.) For example, if you enter 4N-2N, which indicates four numbers, with two numbers in this field, so any G/L number you enter must be in that format. For example, 4321-23 is a valid Acct Format, but 64321-23 is not because its format is five numbers, with two numbers in this field. | | Earning Codes | Enter the earning code here to build the client accounting template or click the Earning Code link to select the one you want from the Pay Code Search dialog box. You can select more than one earning code for a client accounting template by clicking on the (+) sign before the Earn Code link. The Description of the earning code will auto-populate which cannot be edited.   • For an existing client accounting template ID, this field will auto-populate.

  • You can edit the Deduction Account Format which will override the Deduction Account Format for that deduction code in the existing client accounting template. | Tax Codes | Enter the Vertex tax code here to build the global client accounting template or click the Tax Code link to select the one you want from the Select Tax Code dialog box. The Description of the tax code will auto-populate which cannot be edited. In the Tax Account Format, enter the account number format. > Note: Note: - For an existing client accounting template ID, this field will auto-populate. - You can add or delete a tax code here which will change the existing global client accounting template. - You can edit the Tax Account Format which will override the Tax Account Format for that tax code in the existing global client accounting template. |
  • For an existing client accounting template ID, this field will auto-populate.

  • For an existing client accounting template ID, this field will auto-populate.
  • You can add or delete a bill code here which will change the existing global client accounting template.
  • You can edit the Other Bill Account Format which will override the Tax Account Format for that bill code in the existing global client accounting template.

  1. Click Save to either save the new global client accounting template or save the changes made to an existing global client accounting template. Upon saving, the form is cleared.

  2. Click Delete to delete an existing global client accounting template any time.

  3. Click Clear to clear this form.

Defining a PEO Client Accounting Template

The PEO Client Accounting Maintenance file defines the client accounts the system builds into the client accounting download file and report.

To create a client-specific accounting template:

  1. With the appropriate client company selected, go to the PEO Client Accounting form (found in the Client menu under Client|Change).

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Create an identifying name for this template in the Account Template field.

If you select the Custom option, the Custom Layout panel opens.

[Image: screenshot]

  • In the Cust Column field, enter a row number, usually starting with 1.

  • If the option you select requires a mask, select a Mask option.

DMY 14281 2 2007 Date mm (numeric month of year) yyyy (4-digit year)
DDMY 14281 05 02 2007 Date Day Month Year (Same on USA and European Systems)
DDMY2- 14281 05-02-07 Date Day-Month-Year (Same on USA and European Systems)

DDMAY 14281 05 FEBRUARY 2007 Date Day Month Year (Same on USA and European Systems)
DYMD[4,2,2] 14281 2007 02 05 Date Year (4 digit) Month (2 digit) Day (2 digit (Same on USA/Euro Systems)
G0\ 1 pick\ is\ a\ quick\ dbms pick Group Group Extraction using the delimiter of “\ ”
G1\ 1 pick\ is\ a\ quick\ dbms is Group Group Extraction using the delimiter of “\ ”
G1\ 2 pick\ is\ a\ quick\ dbms is\ a Group Group Extraction using the delimiter of “\ ”

G2\ 1 pick\ is\ a\ quick\ dbms a Group Group Extraction using the delimiter of “\ ”
G4\ 1 pick\ is\ a\ quick\ dbms dbms Group Group Extraction using the delimiter of “\ ”

  • In the Delimiter field, select the type of file you need (tab- or comma-delimited).

  • If you want the heading to be on the first line of the file, select the Show Heading option.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. If you have been instructed to do so by the PrismHR Customer Support Team, enter the QuickBooks Billing Template used for billing export.

The QuickBooks Billing Template field is for Quick Book Extracts custom logic implementation. The system uses this instead of the Accounting Template. Use this field

  1. Enter the Global Account Template used for this client.

  2. Enter the Name of Output File to specify the name of the file that the system creates for the client accounting download, if any.

If you define an output file, PrismHR automatically creates the file during payroll processing and users can download it from the Payroll Reports or Payroll Summary. (Note: If using a Global Account Template, any account number assigned to a code in the PEO Client Accounting Template overrides the account number for that same code on the global account.)

  1. Enter the Company Offset Account number used to handle the accounting offset for this client.

  2. For each pay code that uses a different format:

  • Enter a pay code in the Earn Code field to build an accounting format. You can also enter an asterisk (*), which indicates “all remaining” pay codes. The asterisk must be the last item on the list, if used; the system ignores all items listed after the asterisk row.

  • Enter the Earn Account Format. This overrides any format defined in the template and the common format.

  1. For each tax account that uses a different format:

  • Enter the Vertex Tax Code to create client accounting. You can also enter an asterisk (*), which indicates “all remaining” tax codes. The asterisk must be the last item on the list, if used; the system ignores all items listed after the asterisk row.

  • Enter the full code or the tax identifier, which is the part after the dash. The state/county/local segments of the number precedes the dash. The first two numbers after the dash indicate the kind of tax. Refer to your documentation or voucher data to determine what codes to use.

  • Enter the Tax Account Format. This overrides any format defined in the template and the common format.

  1. For each deduction code with the Rebate bill code that uses a different format, do the following:

  • Enter a Deduction Code to build an accounting format. You can also enter an asterisk (*), which indicates “all remaining” deduction codes. The asterisk must be the last item on the list, if used; the system ignores all items listed after the asterisk row.

  • Enter the Deduction Account Format. This overrides any format defined in the template and the common format.

  1. For each bill code that uses a different format, do the following:

  • Enter an Other Bill Code that is not covered by the other fields, for example, employee setup, report, administrative, compensation, or delivery fees. You can also enter an asterisk (*), which indicates “all remaining” bill codes. The asterisk must be the last item on the list, if used; the system ignores all items listed after the asterisk row.

  • To set up an offset for a billing code that is set to Memo Client Acct Only, enter MEMO-OFFSET in the Other Bill Codes field.

  • Enter the Other Bill Account Format. This overrides any format defined in the template and the common format.

  1. Select Use Non-Bill Accounting to open the Non-Bill Accounting panel.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter Employee (EE) Deduction Code information as follows:

  • Enter an EE Deduct Code. Select EE Deduction Code for a list.

  • Enter the EE Deduct Acct number.

  • Enter the EE Deduct Acct Offset.

  1. Enter Employee Tax Code information as follows:

  • Enter an EE TaxCode. Select EE Tax Code for a list.

  • Enter the EE Tax Acct number.

  • Enter the EE Tax Acct Offset.

  1. Enter the Employee Arrears account number for the receivables portion in the EE Arrears Received field.

  2. Enter the ContraExpense account number for the receivables portion of the employee arrears in the EE Arrears ContraExp field.

  3. Click Save.

If you are building onto a global template, enter only differences or additions on the Company Accounting Maintenance screen. The system will use the data from the global template for any field on this screen that you leave empty.

Note: Note: After initializing payroll, access Payroll > Process > Batch Processing > Display Initialized Invoice, then click F9 - Client Acctng to review the journal entry. At this point, you can change the accounts and re-initialize the payroll; however, once you have posted the payroll, you cannot change the accounts. Any changes you make will affect unposted payrolls only. In summary: You can change accounts for initialized payrolls. You cannot change accounts for posted payrolls.

Using the Global PEO Client Account Template

After you set up a Global PEO Client Account Template, you can assign that template to the Client in the PEO Client Accounting Setup form.

To assign the template:

  1. Go to Client|Change|PEO Client Accounting and select or enter a template name in the Account Template field.

  2. Select the appropriate Client Accounting Template.

  3. Click Global Account Template and select the template you want to assign to this Client’s accounting template.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. After selecting the Global Account Template, click Save. The system generates the PEO Client Accounting based on the setup in the Global PEO Client Accounting Template; however, if you need to override any of the account numbers set up for a specific code in the Global Account Template, you can enter that code in the appropriate section of the PEO Client Accounting form and the system will use the G/L number you assign.

Determining the Download Directory

After you have processed payroll, you can create an accounting download file to give the client later. You must assign a valid global directory path to which PrismHR writes all client download files. The path is known as the Client Journal Export path.

Note: Note: Important! This section is for On-Premise customers only, whose systems might already be configured.

The file name used must be defined in PrismHR prior to use. Please contact a Support representative if this field is not populated on your system. The underlying file will have to be created. If you are not comfortable creating a DIRectory-level (DIR) file, please contact Support for assistance. This directory file should exist before you start writing Client Journal data. Otherwise, undesired outcomes—including data loss of those Client Journals—might occur.

To determine the accounting download directory (Client Journal Export) path, perform the following steps in the account in which you want the actual directory to exist. If you do this from the PrismHR (SCORPEO) directory, the new directory will exist in the PrismHR (SCORPEO) directory on the server.

To assign the template:

  1. Click Back Office. From System|Change select System Parameters. Navigate to the Global Accounting panel at the bottom of the form.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the directory name in the field labeled Export Path for Company/Acctng.

Enabling Client Accounting for a Client

Once you have created the client template and are ready to proceed with client accounting, you must enable the client company. Do not enable the client for client accounting until you have completed the client and global templates (see “Defining Accounting Templates”).

To enable client accounting for a client:

  1. Go to the Control tab on Client Controls (accessed from Client|Change|Client Details).

  2. Navigate to the Client Accounting panel.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: Do not enable a client for client accounting, if the template has not been completed.

  1. Enable the Generate Accounting option. The client company is now enabled for client accounting.

  2. Enter the accounting template ID to use for this client in the Accounting Template field. You can select Accounting Template and choose whether the template is a client or global template.

  3. Access Client|Details|Control and click Override By Position in the Employer Accounting panel to enable the Position Code.

Enabling the Position Code adds another panel, Account Overrides, to the Positions form, which you can access from Client|Change|Positions. In the Account Overrides panel, enter a Pay Code and a G/L Account Number that overrides the G/L Account Number on the PEO Client Accounting form for that same Pay Code, for this specific Position.

  1. Click Save.

Service Provider and Worksite Manager Roles

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/UserRoles/Differences_SP_and_WSM_Roles.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/UserRoles/Differences_SP_and_WSM_Roles.htm

Service Provider and Worksite Manager Roles

There are two types of user roles: Service Provider and Worksite Manager. Assign the user role type in the User Type field when you create a new role.

  • Service providers are users who work for your organizations and should not have many restrictions on data entry or other processes. You can assign these roles to service provider user accounts.

  • Worksite managers are users who work for your clients and can be restricted by optional required fields or approval policies. You can assign these roles to worksite manager and worksite trusted advisor user accounts.

Note: Note: Account Management roles (for service providers) and Human Resource Roles (for worksite managers and worksite trusted advisors) determine what actions users can perform, and what assignments they can have. They are not related to the user roles that you set up.

========================================================================

Understanding Role-Based Security

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/UserRoles/RoleBased_Security.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/UserRoles/RoleBased_Security.htm

Understanding Role-Based Security

Role-based security allows administrators to grant access to a system based on roles. These roles are typically associated with job functions.

Note: Note: We strongly recommend that you use the principle of least privilege when assigning user roles. Your users need only the privileges necessary to do their jobs.

For example, you might have roles for Payroll Processors and Benefits Representatives. You could apply these roles to users to grant them access to the relevant forms.

[Image: screenshot]

You might also have a role for Benefits Administrators. This grants access to administrative forms that you do not necessarily want to give all Benefits Representatives. You can assign both the Benefits Representative and Benefits Administrator roles to users who should have access to forms for both roles.

[Image: screenshot]

If a user is assigned more than one user role, and one allows access to a feature while another denies it, then the system does not allow the user to access the feature.

[Image: screenshot]

On the other hand, if the form is allowed in one role and removed from the other, then a user with both roles can access it.

[Image: screenshot]

The administrator grants access to forms and removes them from user roles.

Note: The Reports section in this guide describes the reports available that you can run to review user role details, worksite manager/worksite trusted advisor user setup, and data security access information.

========================================================================

Setting Up Work Shift Differential Pay

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/Task%20Sheets/PD/Work_Shift_Diff_Pay.htm Path: Task Sheets/PD/Work_Shift_Diff_Pay.htm

Setting Up Work Shift Differential Pay

Use the Shifts, Client Details, and Pay Codes forms to setup work shift differential pay, which involves doing the following:

  • Creating a shift code with pay differential

  • Setting up timesheet access to shift codes

  • Including shift differential calculations in pay codes

Creating a Shift Code with a Pay Differential

This procedure provides an example of setting up a shift code with a pay differential for a night-shift employee.

  1. On the Client|Change menu, select Shifts. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Shift Code name.

  2. Enter a brief shift Description.

  3. Select Fixed in the Differential Method field.

  4. Enter the Differential Amount.

  5. Select Include in Pay Rate if this differential pay will be added to the employee’s pay rate. Otherwise, it will be paid using a separate pay code.

  6. Select the Regular Pay Code to pay the shift differential for the employee.

  7. Select the Overtime Pay Code to pay the shift differential for the employee.

  8. Enter the Overtime Pay Factor to multiply the Overtime Shift Premium amount by when paid using a separate pay code.

  9. Select Calc O/T Differential on Straight Rate if the differential on overtime pay is to be calculated using the straight pay rate.

  10. Click Save.

Setting Up Time Sheet Access to Shift Codes

This procedure ensures the shift code displays on the timesheet.

  1. On the Client|Change menu, select Client Details.

  2. Select the Time Sheet tab.

  3. In the Detail Time Entry Parameters table, set the shift parameters. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Setting Up a Shift Differential Calculation

This procedure ensures that the pay codes you use are setup to include a shift differential calculation.

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. On the System|Change menu, select Pay Codes.

  3. Select the desired Pay Code.

  4. Scroll down to the Processing Options panel and select Include when Calculating Shift Differential Pay.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.
